L35: ## 1. Executive Summary
L36: ## 1.1. Product Introduction
L37: Tisrelizumab-jsgr (tisrelizumab) is a recombinant humanized immunoglobulin G4 (IgG4) monoclonal antibody that binds to the programmed death 1 (PD-1) receptor and blocks the interaction between PD-1 with its ligands PD-L1 and PD-L2, releasing PD-1 pathway-mediated inhibition of the immune response, including the antitumor immune response. Tisrelizumab is supplied in single vials of 100 mg/mL (10 mg/mL) clear to slightly opalescent, colorless to slightly yellow solution. Tisrelizumab is not currently approved in the U.S. In China, it is approved for the treatment of:
L38: - patients with relapsed or refractory classical Hodgkin's lymphoma who received at least 2 prior lines of systemic chemotherapy regimens.
- patients with locally advanced or metastatic urothelial carcinoma with PD-L1 high expression and whose disease progressed during or following platinum-containing chemotherapy or within 12 months of neoadjuvant or adjuvant treatment with platinum-containing chemotherapy.
- in combination with two chemotherapy regimens as a first-line treatment for patients with advanced squamous non-small cell lung cancer
L39: ## 1.2. Conclusions on the Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
L40: Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness (SEE) was established with one adequate and well-controlled clinical investigation and confirmatory evidence.
L41: The Applicant submitted the results of a single adequate and well-controlled study to support the claims of effectiveness for the proposed indication. RATIONALE-302 (NCT03430843) is a randomized, international, open-label trial in patients with locally advanced unresectable or metastatic esophageal squamous cell carcinoma (ESCC) with disease progression on/after previous therapy. Patients were eligible if they had histologically confirmed predominant squamous cell histology, irrespective of PD-L1 status, and ECOG PS of 0 or 1. Prior chemotherapy in the neoadjuvant/adjuvant setting was permitted if completed before enrollment and patients had a recurrence-free period of less than 6 months. Patients with prior therapy with PD-1 or PD-L1 monoclonal antibodies were not eligible for the trial. Tumor cell PD-L1 was determined by a central lab using the Ventana SP263 assay (vCPS).
L42: Patients were randomized (1:1) to receive
L43: - Tisrelizumab 200 mg intravenously (IV) every 3 weeks (Q3W)
- Investigator's choice of chemotherapy (ICC): paclitaxel (135 mg/m2 IV Q3W), docetaxel (75 mg/m2 IV Q3W), or irinotecan (125 mg/m2 IV on Days 1 and 8 of every 3-week cycle). Regional variations on dosing regimen for taxanes were allowed.
L44: Patients were treated until disease progression, unacceptable toxicity, or 2 years of tisrelizumab treatment. The stratification factors for randomization were geographic region (Asia [excluding Japan] vs. Japan vs. US/EU), ECOG performance status (0 vs. 1) and ICC choice (paclitaxel vs. docetaxel vs. irinotecan). PD-L1 expression was not a stratification factor. The primary endpoint of the trial was overall survival (OS) in all patients; the key secondary endpoint was OS in patients with PD-L1 \ge 10\%. Additional efficacy outcome measures (no alpha allocated) included objective response rate (ORR), duration of response, and progression-free survival (PFS) in all patients and in the subpopulation with PD-L1 \ge 10\%.
L45: In RATIONALE-302, tisrelizumab provided a modest but statistically significant improvement in OS compared with chemotherapy. The OS HR in the ITT analysis was 0.70 (95% CI 0.57, 0.85; one-sided p-value = 0.0001) with a median OS of 8.6 months (95% CI 7.5, 10.4) in the tisrelizumab arm and 6.3 months (95% CI 5.3, 7) in the ICC arm. Although the secondary endpoint of OS in the PD-L1 \ge 10\% subpopulation (n: 157 patients, 31% of the overall population) was statistically significant at the prespecified alpha level (stratified HR 0.53, 95% CI: 0.37, 0.77; one-sided p-value = 0.006), ascertainment of PD-L1 status was missing in 19% of patients, less than a third of patients had PD-L1 \ge 10\%, and PD-L1 status was not a stratification factor for this study. Therefore, although supportive of the primary efficacy outcome, the positive results in the PD-L1 \ge 10\% should be interpreted with caution in the context of these limitations. The ORR was 20.3% (95% CI 15.6, 25.8) in the tisrelizumab arm and 9.8% (95% CI 6.4, 14.1) in the ICC arm. Duration of response was prolonged in the tisrelizumab arm when compared to the ICC arm (7.1 vs. 4 months). The HR for PFS in the ITT was 0.83 (95% CI 0.67, 1.01). These results are supportive of the primary outcomes and consistent with immune checkpoint inhibitors effects (modest effect on PFS and ORR) in ESCC.
L46: Although worldwide ESCC is the dominant histological subtype (~ 90%) of esophageal cancer, approximately 35% of patients in the US present with this histology (~5000 new patients/year). The study was conducted in several geographic regions; however, only 21% of patients in RATIONALE-302 were enrolled in non-Asian countries. Although only exploratory in nature, subgroup analyses of demographic subgroups of the ITT efficacy population do not indicate differential activity based on race/ethnicity (of those explored) or for patients in Europe/North America. Importantly, based on the SEER database analysis, there are no differences in outcomes for patients with ESCC based on race or ethnicity (Then EO, 2020). Although minorities have not been adequately represented in the study, based on the epidemiological data, tisrelizumab mechanism of action, known safety profile of the drug class, and the clinical pharmacology conclusions, the review team agrees that the study results are applicable to the US population.
L47: The submitted evidence meets the statutory evidentiary standard for the approval of tisrelizumab for the treatment of patients with locally advanced unresectable or metastatic esophageal squamous cell carcinoma with disease progression on or after prior chemotherapy. The observed improvement in overall survival with a HR of 0.70 is statistically robust. This finding is supported by consistent results on prespecified exploratory subgroup analyses and secondary and exploratory endpoints. Approval of the Application was delayed pending inspections of the clinical sites and manufacturing facilities, which could not be conducted because of the COVID-
L48: 19 pandemic. Uncertainty exists regarding whether efficacy would be optimized based on selection using one or more PD-L1 cut-offs.
L49: ## BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – BLA 761232
## TEVIMBRA (tislelizumab)
L50: ## Benefit-Risk Assessment (BRA)
L51: ## Benefit-Risk Summary and Assessment
L52: Tislelizumab is a monoclonal antibody that binds to programmed-death receptor 1 (PD-1) and blocks its interactions with both its ligands. This releases the PD-1/PD-L1-mediated inhibition of the immune response, including activation of the anti-tumor immune response. There is extensive clinical experience with immune checkpoint inhibitors, which are approved by the FDA for multiple indications, either alone or in combination with other drugs.
L53: The safety and effectiveness of tislelizumab for the treatment of patients with metastatic or locally advanced unresectable esophageal squamous cell carcinoma (ESCC) with disease progression after prior chemotherapy therapy is demonstrated by the results of a single multicenter, international, open-label, randomized trial, RATIONALE-302 (NCT03430843). The study evaluated tislelizumab as a single agent versus chemotherapy (investigator's choice between irinotecan, paclitaxel, or docetaxel). Patients were eligible if they had histologically confirmed predominant squamous cell histology, irrespective of PD-L1 status, and ECOG PS of 0 or 1. Prior chemotherapy in the neoadjuvant/adjuvant setting was considered prior therapy if patients had a recurrence-free period of less than 6 months. Patients with prior therapy with PD-1 or PD-L1 monoclonal antibodies were not eligible for the trial. Tumor cell PD-L1 was determined by a central lab using the Ventana SP263 assay (vCPS).
L54: Patients were randomized (1:1) to receive
L55: - Tislelizumab 200 mg intravenously (IV) every 3 weeks (Q3W)
- Investigator's choice of chemotherapy: paclitaxel (135 mg/m2 IV Q3W), docetaxel (75 mg/m2 IV Q3W), or irinotecan (125 mg/m2 IV on Days 1 and 8 of every 3-week cycle). Regional variations on dosing regimen for taxanes were allowed.
L56: Patients were treated until disease progression, unacceptable toxicity, or 2 years of tislelizumab treatment. The stratification factors for randomization were geographic region (Asia [excluding Japan] vs. Japan vs. US/EU), ECOG performance status (0 vs. 1), and ICC choice (paclitaxel vs. docetaxel vs. irinotecan). PD-L1 expression was not a stratification factor. The primary endpoint of the trial was overall survival (OS) in all patients; the key secondary endpoint was OS in patients with PD-L1 \ge 10%. Additional efficacy outcome measures (no alpha allocated) included were objective response rate (ORR), duration of response, and progression-free survival (PFS) in all patients and in the subpopulation with PD-L1 \ge 10%.
L57: In RATIONALE-302, tislelizumab provided a modest but statistically significant improvement in OS compared with chemotherapy. The OS HR in the ITT was 0.70 (95% CI 0.57, 0.85; one-sided p-value = 0.0001) with a median OS of 8.6 months (95% CI 7.5, 10.4) in the tislelizumab arm
L58: ## BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – BLA 761232
## TEVIMBRA (tislelizumab)
L59: and 6.3 months (95% CI 5.3, 7) in the ICC arm. Although the secondary endpoint of OS in the PD-L1 \ge10% subpopulation (n: 157 patients, 31% of the overall population) was statistically significant at the prespecified alpha level (stratified HR 0.53, 95% CI: 0.37, 0.77; one-sided p-value = 0.006), ascertainment of PD-L1 status was missing in 19% of patients, less than a third of patients had PD-L1 \ge10%, and PD-L1 status was not a stratification factor for this study. Therefore, although supportive of the primary efficacy outcome, the positive results in the PD-L1 \ge10% should be interpreted with caution in the context of these limitations. The ORR was 20.3% (95% CI 15.6, 25.8) in the tislelizumab arm and 9.8% (95% CI 6.4, 14.1) in the ICC arm. Duration of response was prolonged in the tislelizumab arm when compared to the ICC arm (7.1 vs. 4 months). The HR for PFS in the ITT was 0.83 (95% CI 0.67, 1.01). These results are supportive of the primary outcomes and consistent with immune checkpoint inhibitors effects (modest effect on PFS and ORR) in ESCC.
L60: The observed safety profile of tislelizumab in patients with metastatic or unresectable ESCC was consistent with the established safety profile of checkpoint inhibitors in patients with ESCC and other types of cancer. The primary risks of tislelizumab are immune-mediated adverse events (imAEs), which were reported in 21% patients receiving tislelizumab. Most of the imAEs were Grade 1-2, and the incidence of Grade \ge 3 imAEs was 4%. Grade 3-4 imAEs was less than 1% except for pneumonitis (1.2%). The most frequently reported imAEs were hypothyroidism (9%) and pneumonitis (7%). Overall, patients in the tislelizumab arm experienced less Grade \ge3 adverse events (46%) compared with patients receiving chemotherapy (68%). The most frequently reported Grade \ge3 adverse events in the tislelizumab arm were dysphagia, anemia, and hyponatremia; incidence of anemia and hyponatremia were either similar or lower than the ICC arm. In the ICC arm, the most frequently reported Grade \ge3 adverse events were predominantly hematologic events, consistent with known toxicities of chemotherapy. Permanent discontinuation of tislelizumab due to an adverse reaction occurred in 19% of patients. Adverse reactions which resulted in permanent discontinuation in \ge 1% of patients were hemorrhage, pneumonitis (including pneumonitis and immune-mediated pneumonitis), and pneumonia.
L61: The review team concluded that the overall risk:benefit assessment favored approval of tislelizumab as a single agent for the treatment of patients with metastatic or locally advanced unresectable esophageal squamous cell carcinoma with disease progression on or after first line chemotherapy. The demonstrated improvement in survival for patients randomized to tislelizumab compared to patients randomized to chemotherapy is clinically meaningful, statistically significant, and supported by subgroup analyses in Study RATIONALE-302. The adverse reaction profile observed in patients receiving tislelizumab is consistent with the adverse reaction profiles observed with other immune checkpoints in ESCC and other disease settings. The risks of tislelizumab are largely manageable with patient surveillance, treatment delays, and supportive care in most patients. The risks of tislelizumab are acceptable considering the life-threatening nature of relapsed or refractory metastatic or locally advanced esophageal squamous cell carcinoma.
L62: Of note, in current practice, approvals have been granted in the US for PD-1 inhibitors in the first-line setting for patients with ESCC (e.g., in combination with chemotherapy). RATIONALE-302 did not investigate the use of tislelizumab in patients who received prior anti-PD-1 therapy;
L63: ## BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – BLA 761232
## TEVIMBRA (tisrelizumab)
L64: therefore, it is expected that the approval of this application will only be applicable to a minority of patients who did not receive first-line anti-PD-1 therapy.
L65: ## 1.3. Benefit- Risk Assessment
L66: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Analysis of Condition	
In the US in 2020, there were an estimated 18,440 new esophageal cancer cases (1% of new cancer cases), with an estimated 16,170 deaths (2.7% of all cancer deaths) (ASCO website); the 5-year (2010-2016) relative survival rate for patients with metastatic disease was 19.9% (SEER website). In the US, it is estimated that 35% of patients will have squamous cell histology (ESCC); however, ex-US ESCC is the most common histology (up to 88% of all esophageal carcinomas) (Then EO, 2020). The median overall survival for patients with unresectable or metastatic ESCC is less than one year; for patients with disease progression after first-line therapy treated with chemotherapy, median overall survival is estimated in less than 8 months.
Locally advanced unresectable or metastatic esophageal squamous cell carcinoma is serious, life-threatening disease with poor prognosis, and dismal outcomes in patients with disease progression after first-line therapy. This histology is relatively uncommon in the US (estimated ~5000 patients per year).

Current Treatment Options	
For patients with unresectable or metastatic disease, treatment options are limited to palliative chemotherapy. Chemotherapy combination regimens are generally used and some of the standard combinations most frequently used in the US include fluoropyrimidine and platinum agents. Benefit	
The approval is supported by a single study, RATIONALE-302 (NCT03430843). RATIONALE-302 is a randomized, international, open-label trial in patients with locally advanced unresectable or metastatic ESCC with disease progression on/after previous therapy. Patients were eligible if they had histologically confirmed predominant squamous cell histology, irrespective of PD-L1 status, and ECOG PS of 0 or 1. Prior chemotherapy in the neoadjuvant/adjuvant setting was permitted if completed before enrollment and patients had a recurrence-free period of less than 6 months. Patients with prior therapy with PD-1 or PD-L1 monoclonal antibodies were not eligible for the trial.
Patients were randomized (1:1) to receive tislelizumab 200 mg intravenously every 3 weeks, or investigator's choice chemotherapy (ICC) (paclitaxel, docetaxel, or irinotecan). Patients were treated until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity.
The stratification factors for randomization were geographic region
The submitted evidence meets the statutory evidentiary standard for the approval of tislelizumab for the treatment of patients with locally advanced unresectable or metastatic esophageal squamous cell carcinoma with disease progression on or after prior chemotherapy. The modest observed improvement in overall survival with a HR of 0.70 is statistically robust. This finding is supported by consistent results on prespecified exploratory subgroup analyses.

L82: ## 2. Therapeutic Context
L83: ## 2.1. Analysis of Condition
L84: ## The Applicant's Position:
L85: Esophageal squamous cell carcinoma (ESCC) is the dominant histological subtype (approximately 90%) of esophageal cancer (EC) worldwide (Abnet et al 2018). Regions of high incidence include Eastern to Central Asia, along the Rift Valley in East Africa, and into South Africa. ESCC is more common in the elderly (aged \ge 60 years) and has a mean male to female ratio of 3:1 to 4:1 (Lagergren et al 2017). Epidemiologic factors associated with higher ESCC prevalence include prolonged consumption of tobacco and alcohol, poor nutrition and diet, and low socioeconomic status that can complicate its treatment (Uhlenhopp et al 2020; Tran et al 2005; Engel et al 2003). Certain histopathologic and anatomic features of ESCC (e.g., high-grade dysplasia, early submucosal invasion, location in mid-to-low esophagus) can render the ESCC difficult to detect, prone to rapid lymph node infiltration and distant metastases, and challenging to treat (Gibson et al 2021).
L86: The prevalence of ESCC has decreased in Western regions due to changes in lifestyle and improvements in general health. In North America, ESCC has become less prevalent than esophageal adenocarcinoma, which is twice as common and has a different etiology (e.g., Barrett's esophagus, obesity, reflux), management, and prognosis. Despite this decrease in prevalence, EC remains the 10th leading cause of cancer-related death in the United States (US) overall, and the 7th among males (Patel 2018; Siegel 2020; American Cancer Society 2021).
L87: Diagnosis of ESCC is often made after symptoms appear later in the course of the disease. One-third of US patients present with ESCC that has already spread to regional lymph nodes, and 39% have distant metastases at the time of diagnosis (SEER 2021). The 5-year survival for localized disease is 47.1% but drops to 25.2% for regional disease and 4.9% for patients with distant metastases.
L88: Advanced (metastatic, non-resectable, or recurrent) ESCC is aggressive with frequent recurrence and limited survival. Lymphatic spread of ESCC to internal jugular, cervical, supraclavicular, mediastinal, and celiac nodes is common. Metastases to lung, liver, and distant lymph nodes are most common, but metastases to the adrenal gland, pleura, bone, peritoneum, kidney, and heart also occur (Verstegen et al 2020). Patients with advanced ESCC experience several debilitating symptoms including dysphagia, cough, tracheo-esophageal fistulas, anemia, weight loss, and aspiration pneumonia that complicate care, diminish underlying health, and worsen quality of life. Advanced ESCC is a rapidly progressing and symptomatic disease with poor clinical outcomes.
L89: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L90: FDA agrees that esophageal cancer is a serious and life-threatening illness and acknowledges the differences discussed by the Applicant with respect to esophageal cancer histologies (esophageal adenocarcinoma [EAC] and ESCC) in high-risk regions of the world compared to in lower-risk regions of the world (including the U.S.). In the U.S., approximately 20,640 new cases of esophageal cancer are expected in 2022, and approximately 16,410 deaths are expected. The male:female ratio is 4:1 for both diagnoses and deaths (American Cancer Society 2022). The estimated 5-year survival rate for U.S. patients with esophageal cancer is only approximately 20% (Siegel 2020; American Cancer Society). EAC is more common in Western populations (Torre 2016) and ESCC accounts for less than 30% of all esophageal cancers in the U.S. (American Cancer Society).
L91: In a retrospective analysis of the Surveillance Epidemiology and End Results (SEER) database data from the years 2004 – 2015, in the US 23,804 patients with EAC and 13,919 patients with ESCC were diagnosed. The most common stage of presentation for both adenocarcinoma (36.9%) and squamous cell (26.8%) carcinoma was Stage IV. In this analysis, 66% of patients with ESCC were men, 56% were White, 26% were Black, 8% were Hispanic and 9% were Asian or Pacific islanders. Important for this BLA, as per the analysis of the SEER database, there are no differences in outcomes for patients with ESCC based on race or ethnicity (Then EO, 2020).
L92: ## 2.2. Analysis of Current Treatment Options
L93: Prior to the use of checkpoint inhibitors (CPIs), the therapeutic approach for unresectable recurrent locally advanced or metastatic ESCC after prior systemic therapy was based on the single-agent chemotherapy regimens (second-line treatment), such as docetaxel, paclitaxel, or irinotecan (NCCN 2017). These regimens were generally recommended for patients with good performance status, Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) Performance Status (PS) score of 0 or 1 and were often associated with key toxicities that led to treatment interruptions or delays, dose reductions, and/or treatment discontinuations. Fewer than 20% of patients receiving second-line chemotherapy respond to treatment, with median overall survival (OS) ranging from 5 to 7 months (Abraham et al 2020). Hence, an improvement in the second-line setting was needed that provided a longer survival time, with fewer high-grade adverse events.
L94: Phase 3 study results demonstrating benefit over standard chemotherapy coupled with a more tolerable safety profile are available for 3 immune CPIs including Study 302 for tisrelizumab (also known as BGB-A317), the most recently completed study, and 2 previously completed studies, ATTRACTION-3 for nivolumab and KEYNOTE-181 for pembrolizumab. Results of ATTRACTION-3 or KEYNOTE-181 were not available at the time of initiation of Study 302.
L95: In ATTRACTION-3, nivolumab demonstrated a 23% reduction in risk of death (OS HR of 0.77 [95% CI: 0.62 to 0.96, p-value = 0.019; median OS: 10.9 months compared with chemotherapy median OS: 8.4 months) in previously treated patients with advanced ESCC (Kato et al 2019).
L96: In KEYNOTE-181, although pembrolizumab failed to demonstrate a statistically significant improvement in OS over chemotherapy in patients with previously treated ESCC (OS HR of 0.78 [95% CI: 0.63 to 0.96], p = 0.0095; median OS: 8.2 months versus 7.1 months), it showed an improvement in OS compared with chemotherapy (OS HR of 0.64 [95% CI: 0.46 to 0.90]; median OS: 10.3 months versus 6.7 months) in patients with previously treated ESCC with tumor Combined Positive Score (CPS) \ge 10 (Kojima et al 2020).
L97: Both pembrolizumab and nivolumab were approved for use in advanced ESCC based on the above Phase 3 study results. In July 2019, the US Food and Drug Administration (FDA) approved pembrolizumab for patients with recurrent, locally advanced or metastatic, ESCC whose tumors express programmed death-ligand 1 (PD-L1) CPS \ge 10 with disease progression after one or more prior lines of systemic therapy. In June 2020, the US FDA approved nivolumab for patients with unresectable advanced, recurrent or metastatic ESCC after prior fluoropyrimidine- and platinum-based chemotherapy.
L98: The survival benefit of cytotoxic chemotherapies, as standard of care second-line palliative systemic treatment for recurrent advanced or metastatic ESCC, is marginal. Moreover, they are associated with significant toxicity with substantial morbidity and mortality for patients and frequent treatment interruptions, delays, and dose reductions that increases the risk of disease progression. More recently, immune checkpoint inhibitors targeting the PD-1/PD-L1 axis have further shaped the treatment landscape for patients with ESCC. PD-1 inhibitors, including tisrelizumab, have demonstrated survival improvement over chemotherapy in advanced or metastatic ESCC patients with disease progression after prior systemic therapies.
L99: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L100: The major efficacy outcome measure in KEYNOTE-181 was OS evaluated in patients with ESCC, patients with tumors expressing PD-L1 CPS \ge 10, and all randomized patients. The observed OS hazard ratio was 0.77 (95% CI: 0.63, 0.96) in patients with ESCC (not 0.78 as stated above), 0.70 (95% CI: 0.52, 0.94) in patients with tumors expressing PD-L1 CPS \ge 10, and 0.89 (95% CI: 0.75, 1.05) in all randomized patients (Keytruda UPSI). Based on the results in patients whose ESCC tumors expressed PD-L1 (CPS \ge 10), an improvement in OS was observed among patients randomized to pembrolizumab as compared with chemotherapy with an observed OS hazard ratio of 0.64 (95% CI: 0.46, 0.90).
L101: Per the current USPI, pembrolizumab is approved, in combination with platinum and fluoropyrimidine for the treatment of patients with locally advanced or metastatic esophageal or GEJ (tumors with epicenter 1 to 5 centimeters above the GEJ) carcinoma that is not amenable to surgical resection or definitive chemoradiation, and as a single agent after one or more prior lines of systemic therapy for patients with tumors of squamous cell histology that express PD-L1 (CPS \ge 10) as determined by an FDA-approved test. Nivolumab is approved for unresectable advanced, recurrent or metastatic ESCC after prior fluoropyrimidine- and platinum-based chemotherapy. After submission of this application, on May 27th, 2022, FDA approved nivolumab in combination with fluoropyrimidine- and platinum-based chemotherapy, or in
L102: combination with ipilimumab for the first-line treatment of patients with advanced or metastatic ESCC. Therefore, it is likely that most patients will be treated with immune checkpoint inhibitors in the front-line setting. Given the prior approvals of alternative PD-1 inhibitors in the second-line setting and combination PD-L1 regimens in the first-line setting, this application is not addressing an unmet medical need.

L121: In addition to the summary for meetings under IND135699 listed above, pertinent regulatory history for Study 302 is summarized below.
L122: In the letter issued September 20, 2017 for the Type B Pre-IND/Pre-Phase 3 meeting, FDA:
L123: - encouraged the incorporation of an additional stratification factor for investigator's choice of chemotherapy.
- recommended that BeiGene consider including ECOG performance status (0 versus 1) as a stratification factor in place of gender.
- stated that FDA has no objections to the anticipated geographic distribution provided that the BLA contains adequate justification that the results observed in a predominantly Asian population can be extrapolated to the U.S. population, as discussed in the International Conference on Harmonization (ICH) E5 Ethnic Factors in the Acceptability of Foreign Clinical Data, and the FDA Guidance for Industry ICH E5 Ethnic Factors in the Acceptability of Foreign Clinical Data, Questions and Answers.
- stated that FDA has no objections to the chemotherapy options proposed for use in the investigator's choice control arm; however, the protocol should require investigators to choose the control chemotherapy prior to patient randomization, so that this can be used as a stratification factor for randomization.
- stated that the primary endpoint of OS is acceptable but that a decision regarding whether the proposed magnitude of OS benefit, corresponding to an approximately 2.3-month improvement in median OS, is sufficient to support approval will depend on the totality of the data, including the overall benefit-risk assessment of the application.
- stated that the quality of the safety data and its applicability to the U.S. patient population will be assessed upon submission of the data.
- asked BeiGene to provide case definitions for immune-mediated AEs (imAEs), include more frequent laboratory monitoring, provide management guidelines for all Grade 2-4 imAEs, provide a monitoring plan for potential ocular toxicities, and revise the protocol and consent forms regarding treatment beyond radiologic progression.
L124: On December 12, 2017, FDA issued a Study May Proceed letter for Study 302.
L125: In a letter issued on September 3, 2020, FDA:
L126: - stated that for an agreement of the content of an application, top-line results of Study 302 must be submitted. Regarding the proposed format of the BLA, FDA does not consider necessary submission of efficacy data from a non-ESCC population. The proposed format and pooling of safety data appeared adequate.
- stated that BeiGene should submit narratives of AEs leading to death, AEs leading to discontinuation, and Grade 3-4 AEs of special interest regardless of attribution.
L127: In the letter issued on November 24, 2020, FDA:
L128: - stated that “Although the proposed list of preferred terms for the “Lookup List” appears acceptable, BeiGene did not provide a list of terms or a query for events that could be defined as infusion-related reactions (IRRs). FDA requested that BeiGene provide the search strategy and definition of IRRs in a pre BLA meeting.
- stated that all imAEs should be reported, regardless of the need for systemic steroids or pharmacological treatment.
L129: In the letter issued on April 21, 2021 (preliminary comments issued April 21 and meeting cancellation requested by BeiGene), FDA stated that the results of Study BGB-A317-302 and the proposed clinical package may support a regulatory submission; however, the relevance of the proposed indication is not clear given the March 22, 2021, approval of pembrolizumab and April 16, 2021, approval of nivolumab in the first line esophageal cancer setting (both in combination with a platinum and fluoropyrimidine). If approved, the indication statement may need to identify that the treatment would be for patients in the second-line setting who are PD-1 inhibitor naïve.

L131: ## 4.1. Office of Scientific Investigations (OSI)

BeiGene, Ltd., is located in the Cayman Islands and oversees all global BeiGene operations. The study data is produced by and the clinical sites work under global BeiGene, Ltd. All vendors and CROs have contracts with BeiGene, Ltd. BeiGene, Ltd. has an agreement in place with BeiGene
L134: USA to act as sponsor for all USA regulatory submissions and with BeiGene China to act as sponsor for all China regulatory submissions.” This October 2021 inspection of BeiGene USA was the fourth FDA inspection of this firm and the previous 3 inspections had regulatory classifications of NAI as well.
L135: Inspections of two clinical sites in China were delayed due to travel restrictions during the COVID pandemic. The 3 clinical sites inspected were:
L136: - Dr. Lin Shen (Site 086001 in Beijing which enrolled 24 patients) was inspected November 16, 2021 through November 24, 2021 and had a regulatory classification as Voluntary Action Indicated (VAI). This was the first FDA inspection of this site.
- Dr Xinmin Yu (Site 086027, Hangzhou, 15 patients enrolled) was inspected September 11 through November 15, 2023. The inspection found that the data generated in the site appear acceptable in support of the proposed BLA.
- Dr. Kuaile Zhao (Site 086003, Shanghai, 41 patients enrolled) was inspected October 31, 2023 through November 3, 2023. The inspection found that the data generated in the site appear acceptable in support of the proposed BLA
L137: The OSI team concluded that inspections of the clinical investigators in these sites (Drs. Shen, Yu, and Zhao as well as BeiGene US) revealed no discrepancies or regulatory violations and that RATIONALE-302 appears to have been conducted adequately, with acceptable data in support of the proposed indication.
L138: ## 4.2. Product Quality
L139: The Office of Pharmaceutical Quality, CDER, recommends approval of BLA 761232 for tisrelizumab-jsgr manufactured by BeiGene USA, Inc. Based on OPQ's evaluation of the submission, the Applicant provided adequate information on the proposed drug product to ensure the identity, strength, purity, and quality of the proposed drug product. The overall manufacturing inspection recommendation is approval for all the facilities associated with this application. The proposed labeling and labels include adequate information to meet the regulatory requirements. Refer to the OPQ's review of this application for additional information.
L140: ## 4.3. Clinical Microbiology
L141: This BLA was reviewed by OPQ's Division of Microbiology Assessment. The microbiology reviewers did not identify issues that would preclude approval of tisrelizumab-jsgr; however, two post-marketing commitments (PMCs) will be included in the approval letter for BeiGene to 1) develop a reliable endotoxin detection method that can overcome the LER phenomenon for the tisrelizumab drug product and 2) to implement an in-line pressure monitoring of sterile filtration for the tisrelizumab drug product.
L142: ## 4.4. Devices and Companion Diagnostic Issues
L143: There is no companion diagnostic for this indication. Patients were enrolled regardless of PD-L1 expression. PD-L1 status was centrally determined using the Ventana PD-L1 (SP263) CDx assay and the algorithm of vCPS, which measures the total percentage of the tumor area covered by tumor cells with PD-L1 membrane staining and tumor-associated immune cells with PD-L1 staining at any intensity. PD-L1 assessment was added in study amendment 4, dated March 20, 2020. FDA considered all analyses based on PD-L1 expression exploratory.
L144: ## 5. Nonclinical Pharmacology/Toxicology
L145: ## 5.1. Executive Summary
L146: Tisrelizumab is an IgG4κ monoclonal antibody that recognizes PD-1 and blocks its interaction with PD-L1 and PD-L2. Tisrelizumab has an approximate molecular weight of 148 kDa. Binding of the PD-1 ligands PD-L1 and PD-L2 to the PD-1 receptor found on T cells inhibits T-cell proliferation and cytokine production. Upregulation of PD-1 ligands occurs in some tumors and signaling through this pathway can contribute to inhibition of active T-cell immune surveillance of tumors. Based on the nonclinical data submitted in the BLA, the established pharmacological class (EPC) of “programmed death receptor-1 (PD-1) blocking antibody” is scientifically valid.
L147: In vitro studies showed tisrelizumab bound to human and cynomolgus monkey PD-1 with nanomolar affinity and absence of cross reaction with mouse PD-1, supporting the use of cynomolgus monkeys as a pharmacologically relevant species for toxicity assessment. Tisrelizumab blocked the binding of human PD-1 to human PD-L1 and PD-L2 in a cell-based assay and promoted T-cell activation measured by IL-2 secretion by a PD-1 expressing cell line. Tisrelizumab did not have in vitro complement-dependent cytotoxicity (CDC) activity or antibody-dependent cellular cytotoxicity (ADCC) activity. Anti-tumor activity was observed in three xenograft models (A431 human epidermoid carcinoma, BCCO-028 colon cancer, and BCLU-054 non-small cell lung cancer). Anti-tumor activity was observed in human PD-1 transgenic mice with B16/F10 mouse melanoma cells.
L148: Tisrelizumab was evaluated in a 13-week repeat-dose toxicology study in cynomolgus monkeys. Monkeys received once weekly intravenous administration of tisrelizumab at doses up to 30 mg/kg. There was no mortality. Findings included an increase in lymphoid aggregates and immune cell infiltration in various tissues.
L149: Embryo-fetal developmental toxicology studies were not conducted with tisrelizumab. The Applicant provided a literature-based assessment of the potential reproductive toxicity of tisrelizumab. Data from the literature indicates the PD-1/PD-L1 pathway plays a critical role in maintaining maternal immunological tolerance to the fetus during pregnancy. In allogeneic mouse models of pregnancy, blockade of PD-1/PD-L1 signaling resulted in an increased incidence of fetal loss but no fetal malformations. There are no reports of fetal malformations associated with PD-1 deficiency in mice; however, late onset autoimmunity can occur. Based on these findings, potential risks of administering the product during pregnancy include increased rates of abortion or stillbirth and an increased risk in offspring developing immune-related disorders or altered immune response. Labeling states the product can cause fetal harm if administered during pregnancy based on its mechanism of action. Effective contraception for 4 months after the last dose is recommended based on 5 half-lives. The Applicant did not evaluate transfer of tisrelizumab to the fetus or into animal or human milk; however, IgG antibodies can be present in milk. Based on the potential for serious adverse reactions in a breastfed child,
L150: women are advised not to breastfeed during treatment with tislelizumab-jsgr and for 4 months after the last dose.
L151: Genetic toxicology and carcinogenicity studies were not warranted.
L152: Based on data from the literature and its mechanism of action, treatment with tislelizumab has the potential for enhanced immune responses to infection or impaired microbial immune responses. The absence of PD-1 pathway signaling in mouse models of lymphocytic choriomeningitis virus (LCMV) infection led to fatal CD8+ T cell-mediated killing of virally infected vascular endothelial cells, systemic vascular leakage, and cardiovascular collapse (Frebel et al., 2012; Mueller et al., 2010). In PD-1 deficient mice (C57BL/6), infection with *M. tuberculosis* resulted in decreased survival that correlated with uncontrolled bacterial proliferation in the lungs. It is unclear whether decreased in survival in this model resulted from an inability to mount an appropriate antibacterial response or a failure to control the immune response leading to normal tissue destruction and organ failure (Lazar-Molnar, et al., 2010). As this data represents a potential mechanistic risk associated with the use of a PD-1 signaling inhibitor, a brief description of this data is included in the proposed label under Section 13.2.

L158: ## 5.3. Pharmacology
L159: ## Primary pharmacology
L160: The Applicant determined the binding affinity of tislelizumab for human PD-1 using surface plasmon resonance (Study BLGC-004). The binding affinity (Kd) of tislelizumab for human PD-1 was 0.15 nM.
L161: Cynomolgus monkeys were a pharmacologically relevant species for toxicity assessment based on sequence homology and binding. As assessed by ELISA, tislelizumab bound to human and monkey PD-1 in a dose-dependent manner (EC50 of 17.3 ng/mL and EC50 of 27.4 ng/mL, respectively). Tislelizumab did not bind to mouse PD-1 (see Figure 1; Study BLGC-003).
L162: **Figure 1. Binding of Tisrelizumab in Nonclinical Species**
L163: (Applicant Figure Reproduced from Study BLGC-003)

L204: ## Secondary Pharmacology
L205: ## The Applicant's Position:
L206: Tislelizumab uses a form of human Immunoglobulin G4 (IgG4) in the fragment crystallizable region (Fc) which limits IgG4 Fc-mediated effector protein binding. This characteristic was
L207: ## TEVIMBRA (tislelizumab)
L208: verified in binding assays by enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay (ELISA) and fluorescence-activated cell sorting (FACS).
L209: Unlike many other human IgG antibodies, tislelizumab has diminished binding capacity to human Fc gamma receptors, including FcγRI, FcγRIIAH131, FcγRIIAR131, FcγRIIB, FcγRIIIAV158, FcγRIIIAF158, and FcγRIIIb, which may result in diminished Fc gamma receptor mediated effector functions. In addition, tislelizumab has diminished binding to C1q, the initiating component of complement complex formation, involved in complement-dependent cytotoxicity (CDC) induction.
L210: Cell-based functional assays were conducted to assess whether tislelizumab could induce antibody-dependent cellular cytotoxicity (ADCC) or CDC. Results showed that tislelizumab had no detectable ADCC or CDC activity.
L211: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L212: The FDA generally agrees.
L213: ## **Safety Pharmacology**
L214: Stand-alone safety pharmacology studies were not conducted nor required for this product in accordance with International Conference on Harmonization (ICH) S9 Nonclinical Evaluation for Anticancer Pharmaceuticals. The effects of tislelizumab on vital organ functions (including cardiovascular, respiratory and central nervous systems) were evaluated as part of single-dose and 13-week repeat dose toxicity studies in monkeys under Good Laboratory Practice (GLP) conditions.
L215: In the single dose study, there were no treatment-related changes in electrocardiogram parameters at doses up to 100 mg/kg, administered via intravenous infusion. Similarly, there were no changes in electrocardiogram parameters after 13 weeks of bi-weekly dosing at doses up to 30 mg/kg. There were also no clinical signs or histopathological changes indicative of any cardiac, respiratory, or central nervous system toxicity.
L216: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L217: No effect was observed on electrocardiogram (ECG), heart rate, blood pressure, or respiratory or central nervous system safety pharmacology parameters evaluated as part of the general toxicology studies.
L218: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L219: The FDA generally agrees; however, See FDA's Assessment under 5.5.1.
L220: ## **5.4. ADME/PK**
L221: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L222: ## Absorption
L223: A series of in vivo studies were conducted to characterize the pharmacokinetic (PK) and/or toxicokinetic (TK) properties of tislelizumab in cynomolgus monkeys. A PK study was conducted at single and repeated doses via intravenous infusion (IV). TK studies were conducted in single, and 13-week repeat dose toxicity studies. In addition, two single dose studies were conducted to evaluate the comparability of PK profiles among batches produced by different manufacturing sites during the clinical development of tislelizumab.
L224: The results demonstrated that tislelizumab mean terminal half-life (t_{1/2}) was approximately 3 to 8 days (74 to 183 hours); maximum serum concentration (C_{max}) ranged from 90 to 999 µg/mL; area under the concentration-time curve from 0 to 1008 hours (AUC_{0-1008h}) ranged from 12,322 to 163,755h*µg/mL; the volume of distribution (V_d) was approximately 22 to 52 mL/kg; and the clearance (CL) was approximately 0.18 to 0.31 mL/h/kg in monkeys at a single dose of 3, 10, or 30 mg/kg. The half-life of tislelizumab in cynomolgus monkeys supported biweekly dosing in the repeat dose toxicology study.
L225: The systemic exposure (C_{max} and AUC) increased dose proportionally from 3 to 30 mg/kg without apparent sex differences. No apparent drug accumulation or changes in systemic exposure were noted after repeated doses weekly for 5 doses or every two weeks for 6 doses.
L226: Two single dose PK studies were conducted in cynomolgus monkeys to compare the PK profiles among batches produced by 3 different manufacturing sites during the clinical development. The PK profiles of these batches were comparable.
L227: Anti-drug antibodies (ADAs) were detected in most monkeys at each dose level in all studies but did not have apparent effects on the systemic exposure.
L228: Distribution, metabolism, and excretion studies were not conducted per ICH S6 (R1) Preclinical Safety Evaluation of Biotechnology-Derived Pharmaceuticals.
L229: **The FDA's Assessment:**
The FDA generally agrees.
L230: ## 5.5. Toxicology
L231: ## 5.5.1. General Toxicology
L232: ## The Applicant's Position:
L233: The nonclinical toxicity and toxicokinetic profile of tislelizumab was characterized in single dose toxicity studies in both mice and cynomolgus monkeys and in a 13-week repeat dose study in cynomolgus monkeys. In addition, studies were conducted to evaluate tislelizumab tissue cross reactivity, neutralizing ADA activity, and cytokine release responses.
L234: No mortality or treatment related toxicities were noted in mice or cynomolgus monkeys at a single dose up to 100 mg/kg via intravenous infusion.
L235: ## TEVIMBRA (tislelizumab)
L236: No treatment related toxicity, including inflammation and immunotoxicity, was identified in monkeys at repeated doses up to 30 mg/kg every two weeks for 13 weeks (total 7 doses).
L237: No specific safety concerns were identified in the vital functions of the cardiovascular, respiratory, or central nervous systems in monkeys. No specific cross reactivity between tissue cell surface epitopes and tislelizumab was noted in monkey or human tissues. No apparent cytokine releases were noted in assays conducted with human whole blood cells or peripheral blood mononuclear cells (PBMCs). The dose of 30 mg/kg in monkeys is approximately 10-fold of the recommended human dose (200 mg) based on body weight and resulted in exposures (AUC) approximately 5-fold to 8-fold higher than those in patients receiving the recommended human dose of 200 mg.
L238: Neutralizing ADA were detected in most of monkeys in all studies, however, there was no immune related toxicity or apparent effect on systemic exposure.
L239: The systemic exposure appeared to increase dose proportionally without apparent sex difference. No drug accumulation or changes in systemic exposure were noted after repeated doses every two weeks for 13 weeks.
L240: The toxicology studies and resulting data are considered adequate to support the clinical development and marketing authorization application of tislelizumab.
L241: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L242: The FDA generally agrees. Immune cell infiltration was observed in various tissues including the heart and skin. One high dose female has mild myocardial degeneration.
L243: **Study title/ number:** A 13-week Intravenous Infusion Toxicity Study of A Monoclonal Antibody BGB-A317 with A 6-Week Recovery Period in Cynomolgus Monkeys/P14-057-CD
L244: GLP compliance: Stated compliance with US GLP (21 CFR Part 58).
L245: FDA considers this to be a non-GLP study based on results of study inspection. A follow-up GLP-compliant 13-week repeat-dose toxicology study in cynomolgus with intravenous (IV) injection every 2 weeks (Q2W) did not further inform the safety profile. Considering the toxicology profile observed in Study No. P14-057-CD and the pharmacology data are consistent with the class, the totality of the data supports the nonclinical safety of tislelizumab.

L252: ## 5.5.2. Genetic Toxicology
L253: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L254: Tislelizumab, as a monoclonal antibody, is not expected to interact directly with deoxyribonucleic acid (DNA) or other chromosomal material. No genotoxicity studies were conducted as they are not recommended for a monoclonal antibody per ICH S6(R1) Preclinical Safety Evaluation of Biotechnology Derived Pharmaceuticals.
L255: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L256: The FDA agrees that genotoxicity studies were not warranted.
L257: ## 5.5.3. Carcinogenicity
L258: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L259: No carcinogenicity studies were conducted as they are not recommended per ICH S9 Nonclinical Evaluation for Anticancer Pharmaceuticals.
L260: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L261: The FDA agrees that carcinogenicity studies were not warranted.
L262: ## 5.5.4. Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology
L263: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L264: ## **Fertility and Early Embryonic Development Study**
L265: No gross lesions or histopathologic changes were noted in the reproductive organs of male or female cynomolgus monkeys in the 13-week repeat dose study.
L266: No fertility or early embryonic development study was conducted per ICH S9 Nonclinical Evaluation for Anticancer Pharmaceuticals, as observation that fetal exposure to tisrelizumab may increase risk of developing immune-mediated disorders or altering the normal immune response.
L267: ## **Embryo-Fetal Development Toxicity Study**
L268: A dedicated embryo-fetal development toxicity study was not conducted or recommended per ICH S9 Nonclinical Evaluation for Anticancer Pharmaceuticals. A weight-of-evidence literature-based assessment of the effects of PD-1/PD-L1 on embryo-fetal toxicity in animals, on normal and abnormal pregnancy in women, and on immune-related response in transgenic mice and patients has been performed.
L269: Review of published literature and information on the well-recognized mechanism of action revealed that blocking the PD-1/PD-L1 pathway resulted in fetal loss in animal models and that PD-1/PD-L1 expression plays an indispensable role in maintaining a normal and successful pregnancy. This literature-based assessment provides evidence of that tisrelizumab may cause fetal harm, increase rates of abortion or stillbirth and increase risk of developing immune-mediated disorders or altering the normal immune response in fetuses if administered to a pregnant woman.
L270: Therefore, no embryo-fetal toxicity studies in animals were conducted for this risk assessment.
L271: ## **Prenatal and Postnatal Development Toxicity Study**
L272: No study was conducted or recommended per ICH S9 guideline Nonclinical Evaluation for Anticancer Pharmaceuticals.
L273: Based on the literature, PD-1 plays an important role in suppressing aberrant immune responses by inhibiting the activation of autoreactive lymphocytes. It is reported that PD-1 and PD-L1 knockout mice develop a late-onset autoimmune phenotype, including lupus-like glomerulonephritis, arthritis, and cardiomyopathy. A meta-analysis suggests PD-1 polymorphisms are associated with susceptibility to rheumatoid arthritis, ankylosing spondylitis and type 1 diabetes. These studies suggest that inhibition of the PD-1/PD-L1 pathway during pregnancy may cause or potentiate autoimmune diseases in infants.
L274: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L275: The FDA generally agrees.
L276: Studies in the literature demonstrate that the PD-1/PD-L1 pathway plays a critical role in maintaining maternal immunological tolerance to the fetus during pregnancy.
L277: - PD-L1 is expressed in the human placenta throughout pregnancy (Petroff, et al. 2003).
- Fetomaternal tolerance was studied in a mouse abortion-prone allogeneic pregnancy model (CBA x C57BL/6) where PD-L1 expression was detected at the uteroplacental interface of the placenta as early as 10.5 days post-conception (Guleria, et al. 2005).administration of a murine anti-PD-L1 monoclonal antibody to allogeneic
L278: - pregnant mice resulted in increased resorptions compared to isotype control
- allogenic fetal rejection was T-cell dependent
- increased rates of resorptions occurred in female PD-L1 deficient mice
- Adoptive transfer of Tregs from wild-type mice to PD-L1 -/- mice improved fetal survival, suggesting that PD-L1-expressing Tregs are responsible for tolerance to fetal alloantigens (Habicht, et al. 2007).
- Blockade of PD-1 reduced the protective effects of Tregs in an abortion-prone mouse pregnancy model resulting in a higher rate of abortion (Wafula, et al. 2009).
- Mice deficient in PD-1 develop autoimmune phenotypes, and therefore fetal exposure may increase the risk of developing immune-related disorders or alterations in the immune response (Wang, et al. 2005).
L279: Administration of a murine anti-PD-L1 monoclonal antibody to allogeneic pregnant mice resulted in increased resorptions compared to isotype control allogenic fetal rejection was T-cell dependent increased rates of resorptions occurred in female PD-L1 deficient mice. Administration of a murine anti-PD-L1 monoclonal antibody to allogeneic pregnant mice resulted in increased resorptions compared to isotype control allogenic fetal rejection was T-cell dependent increased rates of resorptions occurred in female PD-L1 deficient mice
L280: Based on its mechanism of action, the labeling includes a warning for embryo-fetal toxicity, administration of PD-1 blocking antibody is likely to disrupt the maintenance of normal pregnancy (e.g., increased rates of abortion and stillbirth). The labeling also advises females of reproductive potential to use contraception during treatment with tisrelizumab and for at least four months after the last dose.
L281: ## 5.5.5. Other Toxicology Studies
L282: ## The Applicant's Position:
L283: - There was no specific staining observed in the human or cynomolgus tissue cross reactivity studies with tisrelizumab.
- Tisrelizumab had no hemolytic or aggregatory effects in rabbit red blood cells (RBCs) in the in vitro hemolysis test.
- Tisrelizumab did not induce cytokine release in the peripheral blood cells from healthy donors or in human whole blood cells study. The results suggested that tisrelizumab has low risk of causing acute cytokine release syndrome (CRS) arising from PBMCs.
- The potential of enhanced immune recall responses was observed in an in vitro immunologic recall response assay using human PBMC cells, but not in a T-cell dependent antibody response to keyhole limpet hemocyanin (KLH) antigen in a human PD-1 transgenic mouse study.
L284: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L285: The FDA generally agrees.
L286: - The T-cell dependent antibody response to keyhole limpet hemocyanin (KLH) antigen was assessed in a human PD-1 transgenic mouse model. All mice produced a primary and secondary humoral anti-KLH IgG response after immunization with KLH on Days 2 and 23. Tisrelizumab (10 mg/kg) was intraperitoneally administered on Days 1, 8, and 15 to assess the effect on the primary TDAR, and on Days 22, 29, and 36 to assess the effect on the recall TDAR. Tisrelizumab did not induce significant changes in the primary or secondary anti-KLH responses relative to control (Study BGB-A317-IVP-Tox-OT-0001).
- Cytokine release responses in human whole blood cells did not identify a safety signal (Studies: CRAA317-1; CRA-A317-2)

L291: ## 6. Clinical Pharmacology
L292: ## 6.1. Executive Summary
L293: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L294: Tislelizumab is a humanized immunoglobulin G4 (IgG4) variant monoclonal antibody against the programmed cell death-1 receptor (PD-1). The Applicant is seeking full approval of tislelizumab for the treatment of patients with unresectable recurrent locally advanced or metastatic esophageal squamous cell carcinoma (ESCC) after prior systemic therapy. The proposed dosing regimen is 200 mg as IV infusion (60 min for first cycle and 30 min for subsequent cycles if tolerable) once every 3 weeks (21-day cycle) until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity.
L295: The clinical pharmacology review focused on dose selection, exposure-response analyses, and immunogenicity.
L296: Recommendations: The Office of Clinical Pharmacology has reviewed the information submitted in BLA 761232. This BLA is approvable from a clinical pharmacology perspective. The key review issues with specific recommendations/comments are summarized below in Table 8.
L297: **Table 8. Key Clinical Pharmacology Review Issues by FDA**
L298: Review Issue	Recommendations and Comments
Pivotal and Supportive evidence of effectiveness	The primary evidence of effectiveness comes from Study BGB-A317-302. The proposed dosing regimen is supported by a favorable overall survival of tislelizumab monotherapy against investigator-chosen chemotherapy (ICC) with a stratified hazard ratio (HR) of 0.70 (95% CI 0.57, 0.85) and a 1-sided p-value of 0.0001. In addition, the safety profile of tislelizumab was comparable to that of ICC.
General dosing instructions	The proposed tislelizumab dosing regimen of 200 mg Q3W is supported by the following:
Favorable anti-tumor activity: In Study BGB-A317-302, a favorable effect on overall survival was reported with tislelizumab monotherapy against ICC, with a stratified HR of 0.70 (95% CI 0.57, 0.85).
Flat exposure-response for safety in patients with ESCC at PK exposure (i.e., C_{\text{avg,dose1}} and steady state C_{\text{avg}}, C_{\text{max}}, C_{\text{min}}) observed with 200 mg Q3W.
Limitations
Lower dose levels not tested: There is insufficient activity and safety information at lower dose levels (< 200 mg Q3W).
Exposure-response for efficacy was inconclusive because the effect of tislelizumab exposure on survival and potential effects of exposure on ORR may have been confounded by an imbalance in baseline factors of patients in different quartiles of tislelizumab exposure.
L299: Review Issue	Recommendations and Comments
Dosing in patient subgroups (intrinsic and extrinsic factors)	
Based on PopPK analyses using pooled PK data across clinical trials, no clinically significant differences in the pharmacokinetics of tisrelizumab were observed based on age (18 to 90 years), weight (32 to 130 kg), race (White, Asian, or Black), mild to moderate renal impairment (CL_{Cr} \ge 30 mL/min, estimated by Cockcroft-Gault), mild to moderate hepatic impairment (total bilirubin \le 3 times ULN and any AST, estimated by NCI criteria). The effect of severe hepatic impairment (total bilirubin > 3 times ULN and any AST), severe renal impairment (CL_{Cr} 15-29 mL/min), or end stage renal disease (CL_{Cr} < 15 mL/min) on the pharmacokinetics of tisrelizumab is unknown.
In patients who received tisrelizumab-jsgr in Study 302 for up to 22 months, the incidence of anti-tisrelizumab antibodies (ADA) was 14.5% (32/221). Among the ADA positive patients, the incidence of neutralizing antibodies (NAb) was 3.1% (1/32). Tisrelizumab ADA development has no clinically meaningful impact on the PK of tisrelizumab. The effect of ADA on the efficacy and safety of tisrelizumab in patients with unresectable or metastatic esophageal squamous cell carcinoma (ESCC) could not be fully characterized as the number of patients with positive ADA and neutralizing ADA were small.

Labeling	Overall, the proposed labeling recommendations are acceptable upon the Applicant's agreement to the FDA revisions to the labeling. Clinical pharmacology labeling recommendations are detailed in Section 12.2, 12.3 and 12.6.
L300: ## 6.2. Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Assessment
L301: ## 6.2.1. Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics
L302: ## Data:
L303: The clinical pharmacology information in this submission includes characterization of pharmacokinetics, exposure-response (E-R) analyses, and immunogenicity profile of tisrelizumab.
L304: The PK profile of tisrelizumab was characterized using noncompartmental analysis and population PK (popPK) analysis. Exposure-response (E-R) analyses were performed to understand the relationships between PK and efficacy, as well as safety parameters. These analyses support the proposed dosing regimen of 200 mg once every 3 weeks and demonstrated that no dose modifications are needed based on age, weight, race, sex, hepatic impairment (mild to moderate), renal impairment (mild to moderate), and other intrinsic or extrinsic factors. The immunogenicity profile of tisrelizumab has been characterized and its lack of clinically relevant impact on PK, safety, and efficacy in the ESCC population is also discussed in this section.
L305: Additional information are reported in section 6.3.1.
L306: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L307: The clinical pharmacology of tisrelizumab has been well characterized, and the outcomes of the exposure – efficacy and exposure – safety analyses for tisrelizumab support the use of the recommended dose in the target indication.
L308: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L309: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position. Clinical data in Study 302 were limited with respect to the conduct of analyses on efficacy and safety based on race/ethnicity as the trial enrolled only Asian and White patients. Limited data were available in other racial or ethnic groups to assess the potential for differences in PK or PD.
L310: ## 6.2.2. General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
L311: ## 6.2.2.1. General Dosing
L312: ## **Data:**
L313: The recommended dose of 200 mg administered intravenously once every 3 weeks was selected based on safety, efficacy, and PK assessments in the first-in-human (FIH) dose escalation Study 001. In this study, dose ranges of 0.5 mg/kg through 10 mg/kg (once every 2 or 3 weeks) were studied in patients with advanced solid tumors and comparable safety and efficacy profiles were observed between the 2 mg/kg and 5 mg/kg levels. Also, no significant E-R relationships were observed between exposure and efficacy (ie, ORR), or safety endpoints across the dose range tested in this study. Based on this observation, a flat dose of 200 mg (corresponding to a dose between 2 mg/kg and 5 mg/kg) once every 3 weeks was chosen as the clinical dose because tisrelizumab concentrations with this dose largely overlapped with those observed with the 2 mg/kg and 5 mg/kg once every 3 weeks. The less frequent regimen of once every 3 weeks regimen had similar efficacy and safety events compared with once every 2 weeks. The efficacy and safety with the 200 mg once every 3 weeks dose regimen has been subsequently studied in clinical studies including the pivotal study 302 that forms the basis of this Biologics License Application (BLA) submission.
L314: The E-R analyses using data from ESCC patients from the tisrelizumab cohort in Study 302 demonstrated that there were no clinically meaningful relationships between exposure and efficacy endpoints (ORR and OS), and exposure and safety endpoints (imAE, IRR, AE with Grade \ge3, AE leading to dose modification, and AE leading to drug discontinuation). Comparable efficacy and safety response were observed across White and Asian ESCC patients. Similarly, no clinically relevant E-R relationships were observed for efficacy endpoint of ORR and safety endpoints, which include the same endpoints as above, based on tisrelizumab-treated patients in Supportive studies (001, 102, 203 and 204). Taken together, the recommended dose of 200 mg Q3W demonstrated a favorable risk-benefit profile in 2L ESCC patients.
L315: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L316: The recommended dose of tisrelizumab is 200 mg administered as an intravenous infusion once every 3 weeks until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity. Deciding whether to keep a patient on tisrelizumab beyond initial signs of disease progression should be based on factors such as the extent of disease progression and the clinician's assessment of the patient's clinical status.
L317: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L318: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's assessment that the recommended dosage of 200 mg every 3 weeks (Q3W) is acceptable from a clinical pharmacology perspective. However, FDA does not agree that there is sufficient data to evaluate differences in efficacy and safety for dose levels between 2 mg/kg and 5 mg/kg and dosing regimens between once every 2 weeks (Q2W) and Q3W.
L319: In the clinical development program of tisrelizumab, 280 patients with ESCC received 200 mg Q3W and 26 patients with ESCC received 5 mg/kg Q3W. Body weight is a significant covariate to tisrelizumab clearance though the exposure change is small. The exposure of 200 mg Q3W is slightly lower than that of 5 mg/kg Q3W and slightly higher than 2 mg/kg Q3W. In addition, there is no efficacy data in patients with ESCC who received tisrelizumab Q2W. Overall, there are limited data to evaluate efficacy of tisrelizumab in patients with ESCC between 2 and 5 mg/kg doses and Q2W and Q3W dosing regimens.
L320: In addition, a slight trend of higher incidence of imAE and adverse events of special interest (AESI) was observed in patients with advanced solid tumors receiving 5 mg/kg Q3W as compared with those receiving 200 mg Q3W (Figure 8). Due to limited number of patients with solid tumors (n=21) evaluated at the 2 mg/kg dose, a formal comparison of safety between 2 mg/kg and 5 mg/kg is limited. There are also limited safety data for comparison between the Q2W and Q3W dosing regimens because only a small portion of patients with solid tumors received tisrelizumab at the Q2W dosing regimens (n=26 for 2 mg/kg Q2W; n=26 for 5 mg/kg Q2W). Furthermore, there are limited data to rule out any potential differences in safety across different tumors as final safety analyses of other supportive clinical trials for other cancers are pending. Thus, there is limited data to compare safety between 2 and 5 mg/kg doses and dosing regimens (Q2W and Q3W) for patients with ESCC.
L321: **Figure 8. Key safety event rates in different dose groups (Q3W)**
L322: Source: FDA Analysis
L323: Although the tislelizumab exposure-response relationship for efficacy was inconclusive, the finding of effectiveness of tislelizumab was supported by an improvement in overall survival of tislelizumab monotherapy against ICC with a stratified HR of 0.70 (95% CI 0.57, 0.85). In addition, a flat exposure-response for safety was observed in patients with ESCC with PK exposure (i.e., C_{\text{avg,dose1}} and steady state C_{\text{avg}}, C_{\text{max}}, C_{\text{min}}) observed at 200 mg Q3W. Thus, the recommended dosage of 200 mg Q3W is acceptable from a clinical pharmacology perspective.
L324: Reported in vitro binding activities are generally comparable among tislelizumab, nivolumab, and pembrolizumab.
L325: ## 6.2.2.2. Therapeutic Individualization
L326: ## **Data:**
L327: Please see Section 6.3.1.- Sub-paragraph “Clinical Pharmacokinetics”.
L328: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L329: No therapeutic individualization is needed in the proposed indication based on demographic factors (body weight, age, gender, race) or in special populations (hepatic or renal impairment).
L330: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L331: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position. In addition, FDA's PopPK analyses using PK data from all clinical trials (n=1,995) suggests that race is not a significant covariate on the CL and V_c of tislelizumab and has no clinically meaningful effect on tislelizumab PK exposure (i.e. steady state AUC and C_{\text{min}}). Asian, White, and Black patients administered 200 mg Q3W tislelizumab had comparable PK exposure (Figure 9).
L332: **Figure 9. PK exposure across race using population PK approach**
L333: Source: FDA's analysis
L334: In Study BGB-A317-302, 78.5% patients were (n=201) Asian and 20.7% patients were White (n=53). There are no observable differences between Asian and White for ORR and OS (Figure 10). In addition, a flat exposure-response for safety was observed in patients with ESCC with PK exposure (i.e., Cavg,dose1 and steady state Cavg, Cmax, Cmin) observed at 200 mg Q3W (see Appendix 19.4.4). Thus, there appear to be no clinically meaningful differences in efficacy or safety between Asian and White patients.
L335: **Figure 10. Exploratory analysis of ORR and OS for White and Asian patients**
L336: ## 6.3. Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review
L337: ## 6.3.1. General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics
L338: ## **Data:**
L339: An overview of the clinical pharmacokinetics and immunogenicity is provided below.
L340: **Absorption:** Tislelizumab is administered intravenously. Maximum serum concentrations are observed immediately after the end of the infusion.
L341: **Distribution:** Consistent with the volume of distribution of endogenous IgG antibodies, tislelizumab has low volume of distribution (6.42 L).
L342: **Metabolism:** The metabolic pathway for tislelizumab is expected to be non-specific degradation to peptides and amino acids by lysosome through fluid-phase or target-mediated endocytosis.
L343: **Excretion:** Clearance of tislelizumab is 0.153 L/day and does not appear to be time varying. The t_{1/2} was estimated to be approximately 23.8 days.
L344: **Clinical Pharmacokinetics:** A population PK (popPK) analysis was conducted to evaluate the PK of tislelizumab and to identify factors contributing to between-patient variability in PK using pooled PK data from 2596 patients enrolled in 12 clinical studies (Studies 001, 102, 203, 204, 205, 206, 208, 209, 302, 303, 304 and 307).
L345: Tislelizumab exhibited a dose proportional increase in exposure over the entire dose range/dose regimens tested (0.5 mg/kg to 10 mg/kg Q2W or Q3W, including the 200 mg Q3W flat dose). The PK of tislelizumab was best characterized using a 3 compartmental model with linear clearance mechanism. No time-varying clearance was observed in tislelizumab PK. The typical estimates of CL, central volume of distribution (V_c), and peripheral volumes 2 and 3 (V_2 and V_3, respectively), were 0.153 L/day, 3.05 L, 1.27 L, and 2.10 L, respectively, with interindividual variability in CL (26.3%), V_c (16.7%), V_2 (74.7%), and V_3 (99.9%). The terminal half-life (t_{1/2}) was estimated to be approximately 23.8 days. Steady state is expected to be reached in approximately 12 weeks. The accumulation ratios are estimated to be 2.14 and 2.49 for area under the serum concentration-time curve at steady state (AUC_{ss}) and minimum concentration at steady-state (C_{min,ss}), respectively.
L346: PopPK analysis demonstrated that race, baseline alanine aminotransferase (ALT), aspartate aminotransferase (AST), bilirubin, lactate dehydrogenase (LDH), estimated glomerular filtration rate (eGFR) and ECOG PS score did not have a statistically significant influence on tislelizumab PK. Baseline body weight, tumor size of solid tumors, albumin level, immunogenicity, sex, age, and tumor type were found to be statistically significant covariates on the PK of tislelizumab. However, none of them are considered to result in clinically relevant changes in exposure of tislelizumab because the CL and V_c derived based on the extremes (10th and 90th percentiles) of covariate distribution were still within \pm 20\% of the typical estimates. As the impact of these covariates on tislelizumab exposure metrics is relatively small compared to the overall variability of exposure in the population, dose adjustment is not warranted based on these covariates. This is
L347: further supported by the relatively flat exposure-response relationship on efficacy and safety between 2 mg/kg and 5 mg/kg Q3W.
L348: **Immunogenicity:** The immunogenicity profile of tisrelizumab has been characterized in all clinical studies using validated assays. Overall, a 16.5% incidence of treatment-emergent ADA was observed (295 of 1785 evaluable patients). Neutralizing antibodies (Nabs) were detected in 11 (0.6%) patients. At the recommended dose of 200 mg once every three weeks, 16.3% (232 of 1424 evaluable patients) tested positive for treatment-emergent ADA. NAbs were detected in 0.8% (11 of 1424 evaluable patients). PopPK analysis showed that ADA status was a significant covariate; however, based on the flat exposure-response relationships and the assessment of overall clinical response and safety events, the presence of treatment-emergent ADA did not impact PK, efficacy, or safety to a clinically meaningful extent.
L349: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L350: The clinical pharmacokinetics and immunogenicity profile of tisrelizumab have been sufficiently characterized and demonstrated that no dose modifications are needed based on age, weight, race, sex, hepatic impairment (mild to moderate), renal impairment (mild to moderate), and other intrinsic or extrinsic factors.
L351: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L352: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's assessment. In the FDA's assessment, analysis of immunogenicity was limited to patients with ESCC because of the potential for differences in immunogenicity across differences in tumor types. See Table 9 for highlights of FDA's assessment of the clinical pharmacology information.
L353: **Table 9. Highlights of Clinical Pharmacology for tisrelizumab**
L354: Physiochemical properties
Chemical structure and molecular weight	
Chemical Structure of Tisrelizumab
Molecular weight of Tisrelizumab: 144,080 Da with 1318 amino acid residues

Pharmacology
Mechanism of Action	Tisrelizumab is an IgG4 monoclonal antibody that binds to PD-1 and blocks the binding of both PD-L1 and PD-L2, inhibiting PD-1-mediated signaling and enhancing activity in T-cells.
Active Moieties	Tisrelizumab
QT/QTc Prolongation	Not applicable as tisrelizumab is a large monoclonal antibody, which is unlikely to block ion channels.
General Information
Bioanalysis	
In Study BGB-A317-001 and BGB-A317-302, serum tisrelizumab concentrations were measured using a validated antigen-binding enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay (ELISA) method.
A summary of the method validation reports is included in Appendix 19.4.1.
L355: Healthy Volunteers vs. Patients	All studies are conducted in patients with cancer.
Drug exposure at steady state following the therapeutic dosing regimen	In adult patients with cancer, geometric mean steady-state AUC_{\tau} of tislelizumab was 1,283 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} \cdot \text{day} and the C_{\text{max}} was 110 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL} following a dosage of 200 mg Q3W.
Minimal effective dose or exposure	Not determined. There is insufficient anticancer activity and safety information at doses < 200 mg Q3W to determine the minimal effective dose.
Maximal tolerated dose or exposure	In Study BGB-A317-001, the highest dose explored was 10 mg/kg and the MTD of tislelizumab was identified.
Dose Proportionality	The dose proportionality analysis was assessed using a power model with PK parameters obtained following a single dose on Cycle 1 Day 1 in Study BGB-A317-001.
The estimation of the slope of the dose proportionality analysis was approximately 0.98 [0.91, 1.07] for doses 0.5 to 10 mg/kg group for AUC_{0-14\text{days}} and 0.98 [0.91, 1.06] for doses 0.5 to 10 mg/kg group for C_{\text{max}} in 108 patients with cancer.
Accumulation	Steady-state concentrations of tislelizumab are reached after 12 weeks following administration every 3-weeks and the systemic accumulation was 2.1-fold based on PopPK analysis.
Variability	Based on PopPK analysis, the inter-subject variability (CV%) of steady state tislelizumab AUC_{\tau} and C_{\text{max}} was 28.7% and 22.2%, respectively.
L356: **Immunogenicity** In patients who received tislelizumab-jsgr in RATIONALE-302 (Study 302) for up to 22 months, the incidence of anti-tislelizumab antibodies (ADA) was 14.5% (32/221). Among the ADA positive patients, the incidence of neutralizing antibodies (NAb) was 3.1% (1/32) as shown in Table 10.

L368: ## 6.3.2. Clinical Pharmacology Questions
L369: ## 6.3.2.1 Does the clinical pharmacology program provide supportive evidence of effectiveness?
L370: ## Data:
L371: Refer to section 8 for efficacy results from Study 302.
L372: The exposure-efficacy relationships were evaluated in previously treated advanced or metastatic ESCC patients receiving tisrelizumab monotherapy in the pivotal Study 302. In the intention-to-treat (ITT) Population in Study 302, a longer OS was observed in patients in the highest exposure quartile compared with those in the lower three quartiles. However, the lack of a consistent trend in OS by exposure quartiles, the confounding effects of baseline characteristics like tumor size and albumin on OS and tisrelizumab clearance, and the imbalance in distribution of these baseline characteristics across exposure quartiles suggest there is not a meaningful association of exposure with efficacy.
L373: In addition, no exposure-safety relationships were observed for immune-mediated adverse events (imAEs), infusion-related reactions (IRRs), any adverse event (AE) \ge Grade 3, AE of special interest (AESI, imAE + IRR), and AE leading to dose modification or drug discontinuation within the exposure range at tisrelizumab 200 mg once every 3 weeks dosing regimen as monotherapy in ESCC patients in the pivotal Study 302.
L374: ## TEVIMBRA (tisrelizumab)
L375: ## The Applicant's position:
L376: The results from the Study 302 and the supportive studies provide evidence of the positive benefit-risk supporting the efficacy claim of tisrelizumab 200 mg Q3W in 2L ESCC patients. While clinical pharmacology evaluation does not include a direct assessment of benefit-risk, the exposure-response models in safety and efficacy highlight a flat dose – response relationship between 2 mg/kg and 5 mg/kg Q3W from earlier studies, as well as in patients who received 200 mg Q3W tisrelizumab from the 302 study. Taken together, these results support tisrelizumab 200 mg Q3W to be an appropriate dosage regimen for this population.
L377: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L378: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L379: 6.3.2.2 Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought?
L380: ## Data:
L381: Refer to section 6.2.2.
L382: ## The Applicant's Position:
L383: The totality of clinical efficacy and safety data in patients with advanced or metastatic ESCC along with a lack of clinically relevant E-R relationships between PK, efficacy, and safety support the proposed tisrelizumab dose of 200 mg administered intravenously once every 3 weeks.
L384: ## FDA's Assessment:
L385: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position.
L386: 6.3.2.3 Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors?
L387: ## Data:
L388: Refer to section 6.2.2.2.
L389: ## The Applicant's Position:
L390: In the population pharmacokinetic analysis, potential covariates influencing the pharmacokinetics of tisrelizumab have been evaluated. No dose adjustment is required for race, hepatic and renal function-related covariates (eg, ALT, AST, bilirubin, eGFR), and disease-related covariates (eg, LDH, ECOG PS score).
L391: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L392: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position.
L393: ## 6.3.2.4 Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate management strategy?
L394: ## Data:
L395: No dedicated DDI study was conducted.
L396: ## The Applicant's Position:
L397: A PK based drug-drug interaction of tislelizumab with other therapeutics is not anticipated given that the primary elimination pathways are protein catabolism via the reticuloendothelial system or target-mediated disposition. Tislelizumab is not expected to induce or inhibit the major drug metabolizing CYP pathways (Evers et al 2013), as tislelizumab is neither a proinflammatory cytokine nor a cytokine modulator. Therefore, no dedicated drug-drug interaction studies have been conducted with tislelizumab as of the data cutoff date of the Study 302 clinical study report.
L398: Tislelizumab is intended for intravenous infusion administration; therefore, food-drug interactions are not expected.
L399: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L400: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position.
