L436: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L437: The primary evidence supporting the efficacy and safety of tislelizumab in patients with advanced or metastatic ESCC is based on pivotal study 302 (RATIONALE-302). Study 302 is a randomized, controlled, open-label, global study assessing the efficacy and safety of tislelizumab in patients with advanced or metastatic ESCC after prior systemic treatment. Study 302 was designed in accordance with international treatment guidelines.
L438: Supportive efficacy evidence for tislelizumab in the treatment of advanced or metastatic ESCC comes from patients enrolled in Studies 001 and 102.
L439: The remaining studies (BGB-A317-203, BGB-A317-204, BGB-A317-208, and BGB-A317-303; hereafter referred to as Studies 203, 204, 208, and 303, respectively) investigated tislelizumab monotherapy in patients with other advanced cancers and provide additional safety and PK data to support this BLA.
L440: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L441: FDA agrees with Applicant's general description of the clinical trials considered in this review.
L442: ## 8. Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L443: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L444: ## 8.1.1. BGB-A317-302
L445: ## Trial Design
L446: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L447: Study 302 is an open-label, multicenter, randomized Phase 3 study comparing the efficacy and safety of tislelizumab versus ICC (paclitaxel, docetaxel, or irinotecan) in patients with advanced or metastatic ESCC after prior systemic treatment. The study design schematic is presented in Figure 11.
L448: As of the data cutoff date of 01 December 2020, a total of 512 patients were randomized to the Tislelizumab Arm (256 patients) and the ICC Arm (256 patients), with the last patient randomized on 04 March 2020.
L449: **Figure 11. BeiGene: Study Schema of Study 302**
L450: a Paclitaxel could also be given at doses of 80 to 100 mg/m2 on a weekly schedule according to local and/or country-specific guidelines for standard of care; in Japan, paclitaxel was given at doses of 100 mg/m2 on Days 1, 8, 15, 22, 29, and 36, followed by 1 week of rest.
L451: b The docetaxel dose that was used in Japan was 70 mg/m2 on Day 1, and then every 3 weeks.
L452: ## Trial locations
L453: Belgium, France, Germany, Italy, Japan, Korea, Mainland China, Spain, Taiwan, China, the United Kingdom, and US.
L454: ## Choice of control group
L455: The regimen of ICC was selected according to international and/or country-specific guidelines (NCCN 2017; Lordick et al 2016) and advice from health authorities to accommodate differences in treatment management among regions. At the time the study was
L456: designed and was initiated, PD-1 antibodies were not approved for ESCC, making ICC, which was standard of care at the time, an acceptable active comparator.
L457: ## **Diagnostic criteria:**
L458: Subjects with histologically confirmed diagnosis of advanced or metastatic ESCC that had progressed after prior systemic treatment were enrolled.
L459: ## **Key inclusion criteria included:**
L460: - Male or female, aged \ge 18 years
- Histologically confirmed diagnosis of ESCC.
- Tumor progression during or after first-line systemic treatment for advanced unresectable or metastatic ESCC. NOTE: Patients with disease progression that occurs during treatment or within \le 6 months (180 days) of cessation of neoadjuvant/adjuvant treatment (chemotherapy or chemoradiotherapy) are eligible provided all other criteria are met.
- At least 1 measurable/evaluable lesion by RECIST v1.1
- ECOG PS score of 0 or 1 before randomization.
- Adequate organ function
L461: ## **Key exclusion criteria included:**
L462: - Prior treatment with \ge 2 prior lines of systemic treatment for advanced or metastatic ESCC
- Prior therapies targeting PD-1 or PD-L1
- Brain or leptomeningeal metastases that were symptomatic or required treatment
- Apparent tumor invasion of organs adjacent to the esophageal site
- Active autoimmune disease
- A medical condition requiring systemic corticosteroids (&gt; 10 mg daily prednisone or equivalents) or other immunosuppressive medications \le 14 days before randomization.
L463: ## **Dose selection:**
L464: The adopted dosing regimen of 200 mg once every 3 weeks is supported by the PK profile of tislelizumab characterized using noncompartmental analysis, popPK analysis and E-R analyses.
L465: Details are provided in section 6.2.2.1.
L466: **Study treatments:** Tislelizumab: 200 mg IV on Day 1, given every 21 days
L467: ICC:
L468: - Paclitaxel: 135-175 mg/m2 IV on Day 1, given every 21 days. NOTE: Paclitaxel may also be given in doses of 80-100 mg/m2 IV on a weekly schedule according to local and/or
L469: country specific guidelines for standard of care. Japan: 100 mg/m2 IV on Day 1, 8, 15, 22, 29, and 36, followed by one week of rest
L470: - Docetaxel: 75 mg/m2 IV on Day 1, given every 21 days. Japan: 70 mg/m2 IV on Day 1, given every 21 days.
- Irinotecan: 125 mg/m2 IV on Days 1 and 8, given every 21 days.
L471: ## Treatment assignment
L472: Patients were enrolled and randomized in a 1:1 ratio stratified by region (Asia [excluding Japan] versus Japan versus US/Europe), ECOG PS score (0 versus 1), and ICC options (paclitaxel, docetaxel or irinotecan). Site personnel accessed the IRT system to randomize patients to treatment arms and to enable study drug dispensation. A block size of four was used in the stratified randomization.
L473: ## Blinding:
L474: N/A. This was an open-label study
L475: ## Dose modification, dose discontinuation:
L476: ## Dose Delay or Modification for Tislelizumab
L477: Dose reduction of tislelizumab was not allowed in this study. Dose delay or interruption of &lt; 12 weeks was permitted. If the administration delay was ≤ 10 days, the delayed dose was administered. If the delay was &gt; 10 days, the delayed dose was omitted in the affected cycle. If a patient was benefiting from study treatment while meeting the discontinuation criteria, continuation of study treatment was permitted upon discussion and agreement with the medical monitor.
L478: ## Dose Delay or Modification for Chemotherapy
L479: Study treatment-related toxicities were required to be resolved to baseline or Grade 0 to 1 before administering the next dose, except for alopecia or Grade 2 fatigue. A maximum of 2 dose reductions for each chemotherapeutic agent was permitted. Once the dose had been decreased, it remained reduced for all subsequent administrations or was further reduced if necessary. There were no dose escalations in this study. Chemotherapy treatment may have been delayed up to 21 days if the reason for the delay was toxicity/adverse event. All subsequent chemotherapy doses were rescheduled according to the last chemotherapy dose administration date. If considered in the best interest of the patient and consistent with local practice, investigators could have used supportive measures or treatment and/or secondary prophylaxis instead of dose reductions for the next treatment cycle. If any chemotherapy agent was held for &gt; 6 weeks from the anticipated
L480: treatment date, or the dose level -2 was not tolerated, chemotherapy was permanently discontinued.
L481: ## Administrative structure:
L482: BeiGene, Ltd is the developer of tislelizumab. Study 302 is a global, multicenter study sponsored by BeiGene, Ltd. c/o BeiGene USA, Inc. (San Mateo, CA, USA) and conducted under US IND (b) (4) BeiGene, Ltd. is the holder of this IND, (b) (4) provides CRO services for clinical operations and medical monitoring in Europe, South Korea, Japan and China. BeiGene, Ltd developed the statistical analysis plan (SAP), performed the statistical analyses, and prepared the clinical study report (CSR), which presents the efficacy, safety, and PK data for the planned final analysis, with a data cutoff date of 01 December 2020.
L483: This study included an Independent Data Monitoring Committee (IDMC) that periodically monitored safety data. The IDMC could have also recommended modifications to the study procedures or conduct based on available data. The IDMC consisted of members with pertinent expertise who provided oversight of the study in compliance with applicable regulations, legislation, and associated guidance for the nature of the study.

L500: ## **Subject completion, discontinuation, or withdrawal:**
L501: Subjects were considered to have completed the study when they were no longer actively being followed for survival. Subject completion could occur for the following reasons: patient withdrawal of consent, lost to follow-up, death, or study termination by BeiGene, Ltd. After patients had completed the study, no further study procedures or assessments were performed. No patients were replaced, regardless of the reason for study discontinuation or withdrawal.
L502: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L503: FDA agrees with the general trial description above. Additional important eligibility criteria were:
L504: - Organ functionAbsolute neutrophil count \ge 1500 cells/mm^3
- Platelet count \ge 100,000 cells/mm^3
- Hemoglobin \ge 9 g/dL or \ge 5.6 mmol/L
- Estimated glomerular filtration rate \ge 30 mL/min/1.73 m^2
- Serum total bilirubin \le 1.5 x ULN (or &lt; 3 x ULN in patients with Gilbert's syndrome)
- Prothrombin time/international normalized ratio \le 1.5 x ULN unless the patient was receiving anticoagulant therapy
- Aspartate aminotransferase (AST) and alanine aminotransferase (ALT) \le 2.5 x ULN (or \le 5.0 x ULN in patients with liver metastases)
- Patients with Hepatitis B or infection were eligible ifHBV that is inactive/asymptomatic carrier, chronic, or active: HBV DNA &lt; 500 IU/mL (or 2500 copies/mL) at screening. Patients receiving antivirals at screening should have
L505: been treated for &gt; 2 weeks and HBV &lt; 500 IU/mL before randomization and should have continued treatment for \ge 6 months after study drug treatment discontinuation.
L506: • Patients with detectable HCV RNA and who were receiving treatment at screening were required to remain on continuous, effective antiviral therapy during the study.
L507: Additional exclusion criteria were
L508: • history of gastrointestinal perforation and/or fistula or aorto-esophageal fistula \le 6 months before randomization;
L509: • tumor invasion into organs located adjacent to the esophageal disease site (eg, aorta or respiratory tract) at an increased risk of fistula in the study treatment assessed by investigator;
L510: • uncontrollable pleural effusion, pericardial effusion, or ascites requiring frequent drainage (recurrence \le 2 weeks of intervention);
L511: • HIV infection;
L512: • standard comorbidities (cardiovascular risk factors, infection, history of pneumonitis, etc) for checkpoint inhibitors therapy.
L513: While AEs reporting continued for up to 30 days after treatment completion, serious imAEs were reported for 90 days after the last dose of tislelizumab.
L514: ## Study Endpoints
L515: ## The Applicant's Description:
L516: ## Primary Endpoint
L517: - OS in the ITT analysis set - defined as the time from the date of randomization until the date of death due to any cause in all randomized patients.
L518: The primary efficacy endpoint was designed according to regulatory guidelines (FDA Guidance on Clinical Trial Endpoints for the Approval of Cancer Drugs and Biologics, December 2018) and in consultation with the US Food and Drug Administration (FDA), European Medicines Agency (EMA), Center for Drug Evaluation of National Medical Products Administration (NMPA), and Pharmaceuticals and Medical Devices Agency (PMDA). In an advanced or metastatic solid malignancy with a poor prognosis, improvement of overall survival is the most clinically meaningful and robust primary endpoint. As a direct measure of clinical benefit, it provides evidence relevant to patients in each region and country. Adequate sensitivity analyses were predefined in the statistical analysis plan to explore and address the effect of subsequent anticancer treatment and the robustness of overall survival in the study (please refer to the below section “Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments”).
L519: ## **Key Secondary Endpoint:**
L520: - OS in the PD-L1 positive analysis set - defined as the time from the date of randomization until the date of death due to any cause in PD-L1 positive population
L521: Overall survival in patients with PD-L1 vCPS \ge 10% was selected as the key secondary endpoint to explore the predictive role of PD-L1 expression in ESCC. PD-L1 is expressed in tumor and tumor-infiltrating immune cells in ESCC at a prevalence of 18.4% to 82.8% (Guo et al 2017), which may vary by different detecting antibodies, scoring methods, and cutoffs chosen. PD-L1 expression level showed a trend of correlation with the clinical efficacy of anti-PD-1 treatment in multiple studies, including KEYNOTE-181, ATTRACTION-3, and ESCORT (Kojima et al 2020; Kato et al 2019; Huang et al 2020). In these studies, PD-L1 prevalence ranged from 40% to 50%. However, the association between PD-L1 expression and clinical outcomes of tislelizumab treatment in ESCC is not fully understood and therefore further investigated in this study.
L522: ## **Other Secondary Endpoints:**
L523: - Overall response rate (ORR) in both the ITT analysis set and the PD-L1 positive analysis set - defined as the proportion of patients who had complete response (CR) or partial response (PR) assessed by the investigator per RECIST v1.1.
- Progression-free survival (PFS) in both the ITT analysis set and the PD-L1 positive analysis set - defined as the time from the date of randomization to the date of first documentation of disease progression assessed by the investigator per RECIST v1.1 or death, whichever occurs first
- Duration of response (DOR) in both the ITT analysis set and the PD-L1 positive analysis set - defined as the time from the first determination of an overall response (first recorded CR or PR) until the first documentation of progression as assessed by the investigator per RECIST v1.1, or death, whichever comes first.
- Health-related quality of life (HRQoL) in both the ITT analysis set and the PD-L1 positive analysis set – defined as changes in the key patient reported outcomes (PROs) endpoints measured by the European Organization for Research on Treatment of Cancer Quality of Life Questionnaire-Core 30 (EORTC QLQ-C-30) and EORTC Quality of Life Questionnaire-Oesophageal Cancer Module (EORTC QLQ-OES18), and by European Quality of Life 5-Dimension 5-Level Questionnaire (EQ-5D-5L).
- The incidence and severity of adverse events according to NCI-CTCAE v4.03
L524: ## Exploratory Endpoints
L525: - Disease control rate (DCR) in both the ITT analysis set and the PD-L1 positive analysis set - defined as the proportion of patients who have CR, PR and SD assessed by the investigator per RECIST v1.1
- Pharmacokinetic endpoints: summary of serum concentration of tislelizumab to include but not limited to Ctrough
- Assessments of immunogenicity of tislelizumab to determine the incidence of ADA
L526: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L527: FDA agrees with the description of the study endpoints above and that an improvement in OS represents a direct measure of clinical benefit.
L528: ## Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
L529: ## The Applicant's Description:
L530: ## Analysis Sets
L531: ITT Analysis Set included all randomized patients and was the primary analysis set for all efficacy analyses. Patients were analyzed according to their randomized arms. Patients who died before the randomization but who were inadvertently randomized into the study were excluded from the ITT Analysis Set.
L532: PD-L1-Positive Analysis Set included all randomized patients whose tumor PD-L1 vCPS was \ge 10\% as assessed by Ventana PD-L1 (SP263) CDx Assay.
L533: Per-Protocol (PP) Analysis Set included all randomized patients who received \ge 1 dose of their assigned study treatment and had no critical protocol deviations that significantly impacted efficacy or safety evaluation.
L534: Safety Analysis Set included all randomized patients who received \ge 1 dose of study treatment and was the primary analysis set for all safety analyses. Patients were analyzed by actual treatment received.
L535: PK Analysis Set included all patients who received \ge 1 dose of tislelizumab per the protocol, for whom any postdose PK data were available.
L536: ADA Analysis Set included all patients who received \ge 1 dose of tislelizumab, had non-missing baseline ADA result, and \ge 1 postbaseline ADA result.
L537: ## **Efficacy Analyses**
L538: The primary and key secondary endpoints were tested sequentially at a 1-sided alpha of 0.025. No statistical inference was planned for the other efficacy and safety analyses; the p-values presented for these analyses were calculated for descriptive purpose only.
L539: ## **Primary Efficacy Endpoint**
L540: The primary efficacy endpoint for this study was overall survival in the ITT Analysis Set, defined as the time from the date of randomization to the date of death from any cause. In the absence of death on or before data cutoff date, overall survival was censored either at the date that the patient was last known to be alive or the date of data cutoff, whichever occurred earlier.
L541: Overall survival was compared between the Tislelizumab Arm and the ICC Arm. The null and alternative hypotheses were set as follows:
L542: H0: overall survival in the Tislelizumab Arm \le overall survival in the ICC Arm
L543: Ha: overall survival in the Tislelizumab Arm &gt; overall survival in the ICC Arm
L544: The primary analysis of overall survival was to be performed in the ITT Analysis Set once the targeted number of deaths (approximately 400) was reached. The p-value was obtained from 1-sided log-rank test stratified by selected stratification factors at randomization: ECOG PS score (0 versus 1) and ICC option (paclitaxel versus docetaxel versus irinotecan). The region as the third stratification factor was not included in the analysis due to the potential sparse number of events, in particular, with the small number of patients enrolled from Japan.
L545: The median overall survival with 95% confidence interval (CI), if estimable, was constructed using a generalized Brookmeyer and Crowley method. The cumulative probability of overall survival at 6 and 12 months, if estimable, was calculated and presented with 2-sided 95% CI by using Greenwood's formula.
L546: The treatment effect was estimated by fitting a Cox regression model to the overall survival times including treatment arm as a covariate and was stratified by ICC option and ECOG PS score. From this model, the hazard ratio (HR) of overall survival was estimated and presented with a 2-sided 95% CI.
L547: ## **Secondary Efficacy Endpoints**
L548: - Overall Survival (OS) in patients with PD-L1 vCPS \ge 10%: The same statistical methods described in the primary efficacy analysis were applied for the analysis of this key secondary efficacy endpoint.
- Objective Response Rate (ORR): ORR per RECIST v1.1 was tested using Cochran-Mantel-Haenszel (CMH) test adjusting for ECOG PS score, and ICC option in the ITT analysis set and PD-L1 positive analysis set. The common odds ratio for ORR adjusted for strata and its two-sided 95% CIs will be calculated. ORR and difference in ORR as well as Clopper-Pearson 95% CI were calculated.
L549: - Progression-Free Survival (PFS): Similar statistical methods described in the primary efficacy analysis of OS were applied to the PFS analysis. According to the primary censoring rule, PFS were censored in patients with 1) no baseline or any post-baseline tumor assessments and without death within two tumor assessments from randomization; 2) no progression at the time of data cutoff or withdrawal from study; 3) new anticancer treatment started before disease progression; and 4) death or progression after more than one missed tumor assessment.
- Duration of response (DOR): The same censoring rule for PFS primary analysis was applied to DOR. Median of DOR per treatment arm with 95% confidence interval was constructed with a generalized Brookmeyer and Crowley method.
- Health-Related Quality of Life (HRQoL): PRO key endpoints included the global health status/QoL (GHS) and physical function as measured by the EORTC QLQ-C30 and esophageal cancer main symptoms of pain, eating, dysphagia and reflux as measured by EORTC QLQ-OES18. Descriptive analysis (mean [standard deviation], median, range and change of mean scores) for the EORTC QLQ-C30 and the EORTC QLQ-OES18 scales and the EQ-5D-5L VAS were summarized by treatment arm. Time to clinically meaningful worsening for key PRO endpoints were evaluated by treatment arm. All HRQoL measures were summarized in the ITT Analysis Set and the patients with PD-L1 vCPS \ge 10%.
L550: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L551: FDA agrees with the applicant's presentation of the statistical analysis plan for efficacy analyses. The overall type I error rate of 0.025 (one-sided) was controlled by testing the primary endpoint of OS in the ITT population and the key secondary endpoint of OS in the PD-L1 vCPS \ge10% patient subpopulation sequentially. According to the study protocol there was no formal interim analysis planned for the primary and key secondary endpoint. Other secondary endpoints of PFS, ORR, DOR and HRQoL were not formally tested with alpha allocation and are considered descriptive only.
L552: ## **Protocol Amendments**
L553: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L554: The original global protocol for this study was dated 13 July 2017. All patients were enrolled after the implementation of Protocol Amendment Version 1.0. Key changes to the protocol are summarized below.
L555: ## **Amendment Version 1.0 (07 November 2017)**
L556: Evaluation of overall response rate, PFS, and duration of response using immune-related RECIST was removed because the tool had not been validated.
L557: The stratification factors were modified (gender was replaced with ECOG PS score and ICC option) per the request from the US FDA.
L558: Alternative paclitaxel and docetaxel treatment regimens were added to provide Japan-specific regimens per the request from the PMDA.
L559: Clarified that patient who had received \ge 2 prior systemic treatments for advanced unresectable or metastatic ESCC were excluded.
L560: Management guidance for infusion-related reactions, severe hypersensitivity reactions, flu-like symptoms, and renal function abnormalities were added.
L561: The guidance for the immune-mediated adverse event management was modified and updated.
L562: ## **Amendment Version 2.0 (06 December 2017)**
L563: The requirement of treatment beyond radiographic progression was further clarified per the request from the US FDA.
L564: Criteria for dose modification of paclitaxel, docetaxel and irinotecan and permanent discontinuation of chemotherapy regimens were clarified, and dose modifications guidelines for specific adverse events and other toxicities were provided, per the request from the US FDA.
L565: ## **Amendment Version 3.0 (08 November 2018)**
L566: CK (creative kinase) and CK-MB (creative kinase cardiac muscle isoenzyme) tests and management guidance were added to monitor the risk of myocarditis more closely.
L567: Incorporated the US FDA request of implementing measures to further decrease the potential for viral reactivation: continuous treatment for 6 months after treatment discontinuation was required for patients with detectable HBsAg or HBV DNA; continuous effective antiviral therapy was required for patients who had detectable HCV and were receiving treatment at screening.
L568: The criterion to exclude patients who had a history of anterior organ transplant, including stem cell allograft, was added per the request from the French National Agency for the Safety of Medicines and Health Products (ANSM).
L569: Immune-mediated adverse event management guidelines were updated: “tislelizumab must be permanently discontinued for any onset of Grade 4 or recurrent Grade 3 immune-mediated adverse events.”
L570: A new appendix of “Determining Line of Therapy in ESCC” was added to further clarify the definition of first-line systemic treatment in inclusion criteria, and first line or front-line systemic treatment was defined as “platinum-based regimen.”
L571: ## **Amendment Version 4.0 (20 March 2020)**
L572: Updated the statistical estimation of the sample size to increase the sample size from 450 to 500 and increase target number of death events from 336 to 400, with the following consideration: (1) overall survival HR assumption was adjusted from 0.73 to 0.75 based on recently published results of anti-PD-1 therapies in second-line treatment of ESCC and (2) addition of a dropout rate of 5%.
L573: The predefined interim analysis was removed due to differences in survival data maturity among regions leading to the lack of geographically representative population for the analysis, which resulted from the disparity in global enrollment rates.
L574: The overall survival in patients with PD-L1 vCPS \ge 10% was added as the key secondary endpoint of this study to reflect the clinical relevance and importance of PD-L1 biomarker in ESCC observed in competitors' published data. (NOTE: PD-L1 assessment [by Ventana PD-L1 SP263 CDx Assay] at a central laboratory was not initiated before the key secondary endpoint was added in this protocol amendment, and PD-L1 status of each patient was unknown.)
L575: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L576: FDA agrees with the summary of significant global protocol amendments. Of note, all but 3 patients were enrolled before Amendment 4.
L577: ## 8.1.2. Study Results
L578: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L579: ## **Data:**
L580: The protocol, any amendments, and informed consent form were reviewed and approved by the Institutional Review Boards/Independent Ethics Committees in conformance with the Good Clinical Practice (GCP) and applicable regulatory requirements. To ensure compliance with GCP and all applicable regulatory requirements, BeiGene, Ltd or designee conducted quality assurance audit(s).
L581: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L582: A total of 07 sites were audited by independent quality assurance (QA) team from (b) (4) with BeiGene involvement in the audit plan and report review. No critical findings, which had a direct and significant impact on patient safety and/or data integrity, were noted. This study was conducted in accordance with BeiGene, Ltd procedures, which comply with the principles of GCP, ICH guidelines, the Declaration of Helsinki, and applicable local regulatory requirements.
L583: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L584: FDA notes BeiGene's statement in Section 9.6 of the study report that the study was conducted
L585: according to principles of Good Clinical Practice as described in the International Council for Harmonization of Technical Requirements for Pharmaceuticals for Human Use guidelines. BeiGene states that to ensure compliance with Good Clinical Practice and all applicable regulatory requirements, BeiGene or designee conducted quality assurance audits. Audit certificates are provided in Appendix 16.1.8 of the CSR.

The 12 investigators with no Financial Disclosure Forms enrolled a total of [REDACTED](b)(6) patients across 5 different sites; no single investigator enrolled more than [REDACTED](b)(6) patients.

Two of the three investigators with disclosed financial interest were sub-investigators (each one for a different clinical site) involved in the screening and recruiting of a total of [REDACTED](b)(6) patients each; [REDACTED](b)(6) patients in the Tislelizumab Arm and [REDACTED](b)(6) patients in ICC Arm at this site. For both sub-investigators, the site first patient dose was on [REDACTED](b)(6), and last patient last dose was on [REDACTED](b)(6). All [REDACTED](b)(6) patients experienced the end of study before [REDACTED](b)(6). All [REDACTED](b)(6) patients provided fewer than 2 months of clinical and laboratory data, all of which was source verified.
L590: The third investigator with disclosed financial interest was a principal investigator who screened [REDACTED](b)(6) patients and enrolled [REDACTED](b)(6) patients: [REDACTED](b)(6) patients in the Tislelizumab Arm and [REDACTED](b)(6) patients in ICC Arm. The first patient dose was [REDACTED](b)(6), and last patient last dose was [REDACTED](b)(6). [REDACTED](b)(6) patients completed the end of study in [REDACTED](b)(6) and [REDACTED](b)(6) patients completed the end of study in [REDACTED](b)(6). L591: ## The Applicant's Position:
L592: BeiGene has determined that the disclosed financial interest had minimal potential to bias the outcome of the study.
L593: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L594: In accordance with 21 CFR 54, the Applicant submitted information for all but 12 Principal Investigators and Sub-investigators who participated in Study 302 [REDACTED](b)(6) in Spain and 11 in the United Kingdom) and did not complete financial disclosure forms. In many of these cases, the investigator had left the institution and attempts to reach them were unsuccessful. As noted above, 3 investigators for Study 302 did disclose financial interests (provided in Module 1.3.4)
L595: which were reviewed. Of these, 2 were sub-investigators at the same site (not at different sites, as stated by the Applicant, above).
L596: - One investigator (principal investigator for site [REDACTED](b)(6)) received $32,663 from BeiGene for conference/meetings and travel reimbursements. This site enrolled [REDACTED](b)(6) patients ([REDACTED](b)(6) randomized to the tislelizumab arm).
- One investigator (sub-investigator at site [REDACTED](b)(6)) received $29,025 from BeiGene for conference/meetings and travel reimbursements. This site enrolled [REDACTED](b)(6) patients.
- One investigator (sub-investigator at site [REDACTED](b)(6)) received $24,768 from BeiGene for conference/meetings and travel reimbursements. This site enrolled [REDACTED](b)(6) patients.
L597: Regardless, upon review of the information provided about these investigators and in the context of this study that randomized 512 patients and had a primary endpoint of overall survival, FDA concluded that these disclosed financial interests (concerning [REDACTED](b)(6) of the studied population) are not likely to have had a material impact on the conduct of Study 302 and conclusions derived from this clinical study. Sensitivity analysis removing the [REDACTED](b)(6) patients from sites [REDACTED](b)(6) and [REDACTED](b)(6) did not decrease the magnitude of treatment effect between the arms (the stratified OS HR excluding these [REDACTED](b)(6) patients was 0.68 [95% CI: 0.56, 0.83]).
L598: ## Patient Disposition
L599: ## Data:
L600: A total of 512 patients were randomized 1:1 to receive tislelizumab or ICC (256 patients each) in the ITT Analysis Set (Table 13). More patients were randomized but not treated in the ICC Arm (16 patients) than in the Tislelizumab Arm (1 patient), possibly due to the open-label nature of the study.
L601: Over 90% of patients discontinued treatment by the data cutoff date, most frequently due to radiographic progression (Table 13). More patients in the ICC Arm discontinued treatment due to adverse events or withdrawal by the patient.
L602: Overall, approximately 80% of patients discontinued the study due to death. Withdrew by patient and lost to follow-up were more common from the ICC Arm (6.3%) than the Tislelizumab Arm (2.0%). Approximately 20% of patients in the Tislelizumab Arm and 10% of patients in the ICC Arm remained on study as of the data cutoff date.
L603: The median duration of study follow-up was 8.5 months in the Tislelizumab Arm (range: 0.2 to 31.7 months) and 5.8 months in the ICC Arm (range: 0.0 to 30.8 months).

L609: ## The Applicant's Position:
L610: Study 302 was conducted in accordance with international clinical research guidelines (NCCN 2017). The data cutoff occurred at the pre-determined point of at least 400 deaths. The study met its accrual target, by accumulating sufficient death events (ie 410) to allow endpoint assessment.
L611: Nearly all patients were no longer receiving treatment as of the data cutoff. A higher proportion of patients in the Tislelizumab Arm discontinued the treatment due to radiographic or clinical progression than in the ICC Arm, due at least in part to the higher proportion of patients in the ICC Arm who discontinued for an AE or voluntarily withdrew from the treatment. The primary reason for study discontinuation in both arms was death, the primary endpoint. More patients on the Tislelizumab Arm remained on study as of the data cutoff, consistent with the treatment effect observed. More patients in the ICC arm discontinued the study before their first dose of study treatment, mainly due to patient decision, suggesting a desire for treatment other than the ICC to which they were assigned.
L612: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L613: Although FDA generally agrees with the patient disposition summary presented above, FDA's analysis of the ADSL dataset shows slight differences in the reasons for treatment discontinuation, summarized in Table 14.
L614: **Table 14. RATIONALE-302: Reasons for treatment discontinuation**

L616: Most patients who discontinued treatment discontinued due to progression of disease (69% of the total ITT population when combining radiographic and clinical progression) but there is a 16% difference between arms, which can be explained by an increased incidence of treatment discontinuation and withdrawal by patients in the ICC arm. The specific reasons underlying "withdrawal by subject" (2% vs 11% in the tislelizumab and ICC arms, respectively) were not uniformly captured but, based on the small number documented in the narratives, appear to be
L617: AE-related (e.g. following a series of different AEs in a given patient). Although a similar number of patients (50% and 51% in the tislelizumab and ICC arms respectively) received post-study anticancer treatment, 22% patients in the ICC arm (vs. 4% in the tislelizumab arm) went on to receive monoclonal antibody therapy (mostly other immune checkpoint inhibitors), which may have had an impact on withdrawal of patients from chemotherapy upon toxicity.
L618: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L619: ## **Data:**
L620: A total of 60 patients (11.7%) in the ITT Analysis Set, including 33 patients (12.9%) in the Tislelizumab Arm and 27 patients (10.5%) in the ICC Arm, had \ge 1 important protocol deviation. The percentage of patients with \ge 1 important protocol deviation was similar in the 2 arms (Table 15).
L621: The most common important protocol deviations were those concerning informed consent (21 patients [4.1%]). A total of 15 patients (2.9%) were not re-consented in a timely manner; among them, 9 patients were re-consented with the updated Informed Consent Form (ICF) after the discontinuation from the treatment (Tislelizumab: 4 patients; ICC: 5 patients). Two patients (0.4%) reported missing signature for ICF protocol deviations at study entry. For both patients, the signatures were signed by the investigator after the patients orally consented to participate in the study. Corrective actions were implemented immediately to obtain re-consent/signature from the patients.

L638: ## **Table of Demographic Characteristics**
L639: ## Data:
L640: In the ITT Analysis Set, demographics and baseline characteristics were balanced between the 2 arms, except for the PD-L1 expression status. The median age of all randomized patients was 62.0 years (range: 35 to 86 years), with 194 patients (37.9%) \ge 65 years of age. Most patients were male (Tislelizumab: 84.8%; ICC: 84.0%) and had a baseline ECOG PS score of 1 (Tislelizumab: 74.2%; ICC: 76.6%). In each arm, 26.6% and 24.6% of (Tislelizumab Arm and the ICC Arm, respectively) had never smoked (Table 17).
L641: Most patients in the study were Asian (79.7% [Tislelizumab: 78.5%; ICC: 80.9%]) or White or Caucasian (18.9% [Tislelizumab: 20.7%; ICC: 17.2%]). Most of the Asian patients were Chinese (63.3%) (Table 17).
L642: A total of 157 patients (30.7% in the ITT Analysis Set; 89 [34.8%] in the Tislelizumab Arm and 68 [26.6%] in the ICC Arm) had a PD-L1 expression status of vCPS \ge 10%. There were some imbalances in baseline PD-L1 expression status between the two arms, and sensitivity analyses were conducted., sensitivity analyses were conducted. Results showed that the imbalance in baseline PD-L1 expression status had little impact on the estimate of the treatment effect on the primary analysis of OS (please refer to the below section “Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses”).
L643: In addition, 19.3% of patients overall had missing PD-L1 status; this was balanced across the 2 arms.

L649: PD-L1 positive is defined as vCPS \ge 10%, PD-L1 negative is defined as vCPS &lt; 10%, Missing refers to the patients without sample collection or not evaluable at baseline.
L650: a 3 patients refused to report race and were entered with race of “unknown” by the site.
L651: b 3 patients refused to report ethnicity and were entered with ethnicity of “unknown” by the site.
L652: ## The Applicant's Position:
L653: Demographics and baseline characteristics were similar between the tislelizumab and ICC treated patients, which represent characteristics of the broader ESCC patient population (SEER 2021). Sensitivity analyses found little impact of the slight imbalance in PD-L1 expression status between arms on the estimate of treatment effect. Demographic and baseline characteristics were generally balanced between the two arms in the Asian and Europe/North America subgroup and were similar between the Asian and Europe/North America subgroups with some regional variations.
L654: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L655: FDA agrees with Applicant's general demographics summary, above. The two arms were balanced overall with the exception of a higher percentage of patients in the tislelizumab arm having PD-L1 expression \ge 10\% (which could favor the tislelizumab arm), as noted above. The age of patients enrolled in Study 302 was slightly younger than the overall population of patients with esophageal cancer in the US. The BMI of patients appeared lower than the general BMI of adults in the US; however, mean/median BMI data for US patients with esophageal cancer are not available. Of note, although a key secondary endpoint, ascertainment of PD-L1 expression status is missing in 19% of patients. As the trial was enrolled mostly in Asia, race and ethnicity differ from the US population suffering from ESCC. However (see conclusions on effectiveness), race and ethnicity do not appear to have an impact on prognostic factors in ESCC.
L656: ## **Other Baseline Characteristics (e.g., disease characteristics, important concomitant drugs)**
L657: ## **Data:**
L658: ## **Comorbidities at Baseline:**
L659: Most patients (468 patients [91.4%]) in the ITT Analysis Set had multiple comorbidities at baseline. The most frequently comorbidities (by MedDRA System Organ Class (SOC)) were comparable in the 2 arms and included gastrointestinal disorders (Tislelizumab and ICC: 35.5% each), vascular disorders (Tislelizumab: 35.2%; ICC: 34.0%), and metabolism and nutrition disorders (Tislelizumab: 26.6%; ICC: 29.7%).
L660: ## **Disease History and Characteristics:**
L661: Overall, disease history of patients was similar between the 2 arms, except for metastatic diagnosis at study entry (Table 18). Over 95% of patients had metastatic disease at study entry (Tislelizumab: 98.0%; ICC: 92.2%), the remaining patients (4.9%) had locally advanced disease at study entry (Tislelizumab: 2.0%; ICC: 7.8%). The median sum of diameters of the target lesions, as assessed by the investigator, were similar in the 2 arms; 42.0 mm (range: 10.0 to 261.0 mm) for the tislelizumab Arm and 42.00 mm (range: 10.0 to 238.0 mm) for the ICC Arm. The location of target and nontarget lesions at study entry in the 2 arms was similar, and the most commonly reported distant metastatic visceral organs were lung (Tislelizumab: 116 [45.3%]; ICC: 119 [46.5%]), liver (Tislelizumab: 76 [29.7%]; ICC: 69 [27.0%]), and bone (Tislelizumab: 30 [11.7%]; ICC: 32 [12.5%]).

L665: Percentages were based on N.
L666: Study Entry date referred to randomization date in this study.
L667: a A patient was counted only once within each category but may be counted in multiple categories.
L668: In the 3 PD-L1 subgroups, patient-reported disease history was similar between all 3 subgroups and between the 2 arms within each PD-L1 subgroup. The disease history profile was balanced between the Asia and the Europe/North America subgroups, and between the 2 arms within each region.
L669: ## **Prior Anticancer Systemic Therapies:**
L670: Nearly all patients had received 1 prior line of systemic therapy, with 2 patients (1 patient in each arm) whose prior systemic anticancer therapy did not meet the definition of a line of systemic therapy per protocol. Both cases were identified as important protocol deviations.
L671: Among the 510 patients who received 1 prior line of systemic treatment per protocol, 16.2% received neoadjuvant/adjuvant treatment as first-line systemic therapy (tislelizumab: 19.9%; ICC: 12.5%) and 11.3% received definitive chemoradiotherapy (tislelizumab: 10.2%; ICC: 12.5%) as first-line systemic therapy.
L672: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L673: Overall, comorbidities at baseline, disease history, and prior anticancer systemic therapies were similar between the 2 arms, and representative of the target population. Together with the demographic characteristics, patients characteristics in study 302 were similar to those of patients in recently completed studies, that enrolled patients with ESCC for second-line treatment (Kojima et al 2020; Kato et al 2019; Huang et al 2020), with a few minor variations due to the difference in the regional distribution and the composition of histological types of tumors in the enrolled populations.
L674: Baseline characteristics (including prior anticancer therapies) had few regional variations. Compared with patients in the Asia subgroup, the Europe/North America subgroup had a lower proportion of patients who had undergone prior surgery for ESCC, and a higher proportion of patients who had received prior definitive chemoradiotherapy. The regional variations were similar with that observed in a retrospective study comparing clinicopathologic features of Eastern and Western populations (Zhang et al 2015).
L675: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L676: FDA notes the disease characteristics described above and agrees that the 2 arms were generally
L677: balanced with the exception of a higher percentage of patients in the tislelizumab arm having metastatic disease at study entry (which could favor the ICC arm). Regarding prior therapy, almost all patients (1 protocol deviation per arm) received prior systemic therapy.
L678: Although the chemotherapy regimens received were generally comparable between study regions, there were regional differences. Based on the clinical study report (CSR), the prior systemic therapy included the following drugs based on region:
L679: - Asia:97% platinum (58% cisplatin; 30% nedaplatin)
- 62% taxane
- 45% fluoropyrimidine
- North America/Europe98% platinum (40% oxaliplatin; 37% cisplatin; 26% carboplatin)
- 81% fluoropyrimidine
- 28% taxane
L680: Although there were regional differences in the chemotherapy regimen used, almost all patients (97%) patients received a platinum doublet prior to enrolling in Study 302 and, thus, results from the Study 302 population are relevant to the patient populations in North America and Europe.
L681: ## Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
L682: ## Data:
L683: ## **Treatment Compliance:**
L684: All study drugs were administered at the study site under the supervision of the study staff. Compliance was assessed by periodic reviews of study drug administration data, study drug accountability records, and source documents reflecting the patient's medical care. Summaries of the study treatment exposure and dosing data are presented in Section 8.2.2 Overall Exposure. The results of duration of exposure and number of cycles received were in line with the results of median PFS and anti-tumor response, suggesting a good treatment compliance to the protocol requirement. The median relative dose intensity was 100% and 89.4% for the tislelizumab and the ICC, respectively.
L685: ## **Concomitant Medications:**
L686: ## Permitted Concomitant Therapy
L687: Most concomitant medications and therapies were allowed if deemed necessary in keeping with the local standard of medical care at the discretion of the investigator for the supportive care (eg, antiemetics, antidiarrheals, pain medications, and nutritional support).
L688: ## Therapies Excluded During Tislelizumab Treatment
L689: The following medications were prohibited or restricted at the time of screening and during the administration of tislelizumab:
L690: - Immunosuppressive agents (except to treat a drug-related adverse event)
- Systemic corticosteroids &gt; 10 mg daily (prednisone or equivalent), except to treat or control a drug-related adverse event or for short-term use as prophylactic treatment
- Live vaccines \le 28 days before the first dose of tislelizumab and 60 days after the last dose of tislelizumab
- Herbal remedies with immune-stimulating properties (eg, mistletoe extract) or that were known to potentially interfere with liver or other major organ functions (eg, hypericin). Patients were required to notify the investigator of all herbal remedies used during the study.
L691: ## Therapies Excluded During Investigator-Chosen Chemotherapy Treatment
L692: - Caution was exercised for patients receiving paclitaxel concomitantly with substrates, inhibitors, or inducers of CYP2C8 or CYP3A4. The metabolism of paclitaxel is catalyzed by cytochrome P450 isoenzymes CYP2C8 and CYP3A4. Potential interactions between paclitaxel, a substrate of CYP3A4, and protease inhibitors, which were substrates and/or inhibitors of CYP3A4, were not evaluated in clinical studies.
- Patients receiving docetaxel were required to avoid concomitant use of strong inhibitors of CYP3A4; however, if strong inhibitors of CYP3A4 could not be avoided, the docetaxel dose was reduced per the product label.
- Strong CYP3A4 inducers were not allowed to be administered with irinotecan unless there were no therapeutic alternatives. Strong CYP3A4 inhibitors and UGT1A1 inhibitors or both CYP3A4 and UGT1A1 inhibitors were not allowed when patients were treated with irinotecan unless there were no therapeutic alternatives.
L693: ## Therapies Excluded During All Study Treatment
L694: Concurrent antineoplastic therapy (eg, chemotherapy, hormonal therapy, immunotherapy, or standard or investigational agents [including Chinese herbal medicine and Chinese patent medicines] for the treatment of cancer) and extensive radiation therapy (except for local, palliative radiotherapy) were prohibited or restricted at the time of screening and during the administration of study treatment. Palliative (limited-field) radiation therapy is permitted, but only for pain control or prophylaxis of bone fracture to sites of bone disease present at baseline. During the study, surgical procedures required during study participation were reported as treatments for adverse events and patients were required to avoid abuse of alcohol and other drugs.
L695: A total of 487 patients (98.4%) received concomitant medications on study (Tislelizumab: 97.6%; ICC: 99.2%). The concomitant medications most commonly received by patients in both
L696: arms included proton pump inhibitors (54.1% versus 70.4%), glucocorticoids (36.9% versus 72.5%), unspecified herbal and traditional medicine (32.2% versus 32.1%), and natural opium alkaloids (31.4% versus 31.7%). A total of 90 patients (35.3%) in the tislelizumab Arm and 173 patients (72.1%) in the ICC Arm received concomitant systemic corticosteroids. More patients in the tislelizumab Arm used systemic corticosteroids for adverse events (24.3% versus 15.8%), and more patients in the ICC Arm used systemic corticosteroids for prophylaxis (12.5% versus 63.8%). A total of 35 patients (13.7%) received systemic corticosteroids for the treatment of imAEs. No other immunosuppressive drugs were used by any patient to treat imAEs in this study. A total of 23 patients received thyroid hormone for the treatment of imAEs, and all were in the Tislelizumab Arm.
L697: ## Rescue Medication
L698: No rescue medications were used or needed in Study 302.
L699: ## The Applicant's Position:
L700: Overall, the drug exposure results showed a good treatment compliance of both tislelizumab and ICC, and the concomitant treatments administered were representative of those commonly prescribed for patients in the target population and were not expected to have impacted study results.
L701: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L702: FDA agrees with the Applicant's general assessment above. FDA concurs with the Applicant's data. Although it is stated above that immune suppressive regimens and steroids were restricted or prohibited during tislelizumab treatment, corticosteroids were recommended and used for the management of imAEs in the tislelizumab arm. Other immunosuppressive drugs (e.g. mycophenolate) were not used in Study 302.
L703: ## **Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity/Supportive Analyses)**
L704: ## Data:
L705: ## **Primary Efficacy Endpoint: Overall Survival in the ITT Analysis Set**
L706: Tislelizumab demonstrated a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in OS versus ICC in patients with advanced or metastatic ESCC with disease progression after prior systemic treatment.
L707: With 410 death events (80.1% of all patients randomized) reported, the stratified HR of OS was 0.70 (95% CI: 0.57 to 0.85) with 1-sided p-value of 0.0001, indicating a 30% reduction in the risk of death events in the Tislelizumab Arm compared with the ICC Arm. A 2.3-month improvement in median OS was observed (8.6 months in the Tislelizumab Arm versus 6.3 months in the ICC Arm). The separation of the Kaplan Meier OS plots began at approximately
L708: 2.5 months and remained separate throughout the study follow-up period (Figure 12). The estimated 6- and 12-month survival rates were 62.3% (95% CI: 56.0% to 67.9%) versus 51.8% (95% CI: 45.3% to 57.9%), and 37.4% (95% CI: 31.4% to 43.4%) versus 23.7% (95% CI: 18.5% to 29.3%) in the Tislelizumab Arm and the ICC Arm, respectively. The median follow-up times by reverse Kaplan-Meier methodology were 20.8 months (95% CI: 19.5 to 22.4 months) and 21.1 months (95% CI: 19.3 to 22.8 months) for the Tislelizumab Arm and ICC Arm, respectively.
L709: It is important to note that the magnitude of improvement in OS with tislelizumab in Study 302 was observed in the context of a notably higher rate of use of subsequent anti-PD-1 or PD-L1 inhibitors after discontinuing from study treatment in the ICC Arm (21.5%) than in the Tislelizumab Arm (4.3%).
L710: The OS benefit in favor of tislelizumab was observed across all pre-defined subgroups by demographic and baseline characteristics, including by region and PD-L1 status. These analyses are provided and discussed below.
L711: In addition, several sensitivity/supportive analyses were performed to examine the robustness of the primary analysis results. Treatment effect estimates from sensitivity and supportive analyses were similar to the results of the primary analysis, providing evidence that tislelizumab offered a statistically persuasive and clinically meaningful benefit over ICC.
L712: OS in the Per-Protocol Analysis Set: stratified HR of 0.68 (95% CI: 0.56 to 0.83).
L713: Unstratified OS Analysis in the ITT Analysis Set: unstratified HR of 0.69 (95% CI: 0.57 to 0.84).
L714: Stratified OS Analysis by Strata as collected in the electronic case report form: stratified HR of 0.69 (95% CI: 0.57 to 0.84).
L715: Sensitivity analysis by adding region as one of the strata: stratified HR of 0.70 (95% CI: 0.58 to 0.86).
L716: Sensitivity analysis evaluating the impact of patients randomized but not treated by excluding them from the analysis: stratified HR of 0.69 (95% CI: 0.57 to 0.84).
L717: Supportive analysis in patients with nonmissing baseline PD-L1 expression status (N = 413, combining patients with vCPS \ge 10% and vCPS &lt; 10%): stratified HR of 0.68 (95% CI: 0.55 to 0.85). The consistency of this result with that obtained in the ITT analysis set and in patients with PD-L1 status missing, along with the fact that absence of baseline PD-L1 expression status was random, appears to mitigate concern about potential bias introduced by the missing status subgroup in the interpretation of the primary analysis results in the ITT Analysis Set.
L718: Sensitivity analysis to adjust the treatment effect estimate by adding PD-L1 expression status either as a covariate to the stratified Cox regression model of the primary analysis: stratified HR: 0.72 [95% CI: 0.59 to 0.88]; or as an additional stratification factor: stratified HR: 0.70 [95% CI: 0.57 to 0.87]. Results from these analyses showed that the random imbalance in baseline PD-L1 expression status had little impact on the primary analysis OS treatment effect estimate.

L733: ## Subgroup Analyses for Primary Efficacy Endpoint
L734: Overall, the benefit of tislelizumab in improving OS, when compared with ICC, was observed across all the prespecified subgroups (Figure 13). For all prespecified subgroups, including baseline PD-L1 expression status, age, gender, smoking status, ICC option, ECOG PS score, region, and race, the unstratified HR favored the Tislelizumab Arm (ie, HR &lt; 1).
L735: The OS treatment effect estimated within both the Asia subgroup and Europe/North America subgroup clearly favored the Tislelizumab Arm. In the Asia subgroup, the median OS was 8.5 months (95% CI: 7.1 to 10.3 months) for the Tislelizumab Arm and 6.3 months (95% CI: 5.3 to 7.4 months) for the ICC Arm, and the HR was 0.73 (95% CI: 0.59 to 0.90). In the Europe/North America subgroup, the median OS was 11.2 months (95% CI: 5.9 to 14.8 months) for the Tislelizumab Arm and 6.3 months (95% CI: 4.6 to 7.7 months) for the ICC Arm, and the HR was 0.55 (95% CI: 0.35 to 0.87).
L736: **Figure 13. BeiGene: Subgroup Analysis: Forest Plot of Overall Survival (ITT Analysis Set)**

L739: PD-L1 positive is defined as vCPS ≥ 10%, PD-L1 negative is defined as vCPS &lt; 10%, Missing refers to the patients without sample collection or not evaluable at baseline.
L740: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L741: Study 302 is the largest global Phase 3 study of PD-1 or PD-L1 inhibitor as a second line treatment for ESCC with a global population. Study 302 met its primary endpoint of OS in the ITT Analysis Set with a statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in overall survival demonstrating that tislelizumab treatment decreased the risk of death by 30% and improved median survival by 2.3 months with survival benefits beginning within 3 months of starting treatment and continuing at the 6- and 12-month landmark analyses as shown in Kaplan-Meier curves. Of note, the OS benefit was observed across all pre-defined subgroups by
L742: 102
L743: *Version date: January 2020 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)*
L744: *Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as "Data" and "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.*
L745: demographic and baseline characteristics, including by region and PD-L1 status.
L746: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L747: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's presentation of the primary efficacy results for OS in the ITT population (N = 512). The primary analysis of OS in the ITT population demonstrated a statistically significant improvement favoring the tislelizumab arm (stratified HR = 0.70; 95% CI: 0.57, 0.85; one-sided p-value = 0.0001). This result was robust across the various sensitivity analyses conducted by the applicant. However, FDA notes that the landmark OS rates at 3, 6, 9, and 12 months by arm presented by the applicant should be considered exploratory. In general, the point estimate of event rates at a fixed time point for a time-to-event endpoint can be misleading because it does not represent the entire effect of the treatment and the chosen landmark time is arbitrary.
L748: Regarding the subgroup analyses presented by the Applicant, FDA notes that all subgroup analyses are considered exploratory except for the subgroup of OS in PD-L1 vCPS \ge10% which was the key secondary endpoint tested in hierarchy.
L749: In addition to the results by baseline PD-L1 status presented in Figure 14, the Table 20 summarizes the efficacy results for OS by PD-L1 subgroups, presenting median OS by arm and stratified HRs.

L754: The results for the key secondary endpoint of OS in PD-L1 vCPS \ge10% (N = 157) were statistically significant at the prespecified alpha level (stratified HR=0.53, 95% CI: 0.37, 0.77; one-sided p-value=0.006). However, FDA notes that the results in the PD-L1 vCPS \ge10% subpopulation should be interpreted with caution given that approximately 19% of patients enrolled had missing PD-L1 vCPS (N = 99). FDA also notes that PD-L1 status was not a stratification factor for this study which could lead to potential imbalances in baseline characteristics between arms and unmeasured confounding in these subgroups.

L766: Overall, the OS results across PD-L1 subgroups as defined by vCPS \ge10% and vCPS &lt;10% appeared consistent when considering sensitivity analyses to assess the impact of missing PD-L1 status. However, results of the sensitivity analyses as well as results in the vCPS &lt;10% patient subgroup should be considered exploratory.
L767: Although RATIONALE-302 was revised (Amendment 4) to include a key secondary endpoint of OS in patients with CPS \ge 10%, which was a predictive factor in Study KEYNOTE-181 in patients with ESCC, ascertainment of PD-L1 expression was missing in 19% of patients and the analysis in this subset of patients was not included in labeling (see below in secondary endpoint
L768: analysis). Approximately 10% of the patients had PD-L1 vCPS &lt; 1% population and few events occur, therefore, the scope of interpretation of any additional analyses based on PD-L1 cutoff is limited and will not be shown.

L776: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints
L777: ## Data:
L778: ## **Key Secondary Endpoint: Overall Survival in Patients With PD-L1 vCPS \ge 10%**
L779: Tislelizumab treatment showed a statistically significant improvement compared to ICC treatment in patients with PD-L1 vCPS \ge 10%, with a stratified HR of 0.54 (95% CI: 0.36 to 0.79) and a 1-sided stratified logrank test p-value of 0.0006. A 3.5-month difference in median OS between the 2 arms was observed and considered clinically meaningful (10.3 months [95% CI: 8.5 to 16.1 months] in the Tislelizumab Arm versus 6.8 months [95% CI: 4.1 to 8.3 months] in the ICC Arm).

L783: ## Progression-Free Survival (ITT Analysis Set)
L784: Results of PFS in the ITT Analysis Set showed a 17% reduction in the risk of disease progression or death in the Tislelizumab Arm compared with the ICC Arm (stratified HR: 0.83 [95% CI: 0.67 to 1.01]) (Table 23).
L785: Although the median PFS was slightly shorter in the Tislelizumab Arm than in the ICC Arm, the Kaplan Meier- plots of PFS began to separate approximately 3 months after randomization in favor of the Tislelizumab Arm and remained separate for the remainder of the study (Figure 18)
L786: with higher landmark PFS rates at 6 months (21.7% versus 14.9%), 9 months (15.8% versus 9.6%), and 12 months (12.7% versus 1.9%) for the Tislelizumab Arm versus the ICC Arm.

L798: ## Objective Response and Disease Control Rate (ITT Analysis Set)
L799: Unconfirmed ORR as assessed by the investigators per RECIST v1.1 (secondary endpoint) in the Tislelizumab Arm was more than twice that in the ICC Arm (20.3% [95% CI: 15.6% to 25.8%] versus 9.8% [95% CI: 6.4% to 14.1%]), with an estimated odds ratio of 2.39 (95% CI: 1.42 to 4.01). More patients had a best overall response (BOR) of CR in the Tislelizumab Arm than in the ICC Arm (5 patients [2.0%] versus 1 patient [0.4%]) (Table 24). In a post-hoc sensitivity analysis, confirmed ORR in the Tislelizumab Arm was also more than twice that in the ICC Arm (15.2% versus 6.6%), with 5 CRs in the Tislelizumab Arm and 1 CR in the ICC Arm.
L800: DCR assessed by the investigators (exploratory endpoint) was also numerically higher in the Tislelizumab Arm (46.9%) compared with the ICC Arm (41.8%).

L808: ## Duration of Response (ITT Analysis Set)
L809: The tumor response after treatment with tislelizumab was durable and clinically meaningful compared with treatment with chemotherapy. The median DOR was 7.1 months (95% CI: 4.1 to 11.3 months) in the Tislelizumab Arm compared with 4.0 months (95% CI: 2.1 to 8.2 months) in the ICC Arm, with 10 of 52 responders (19.2%) in the Tislelizumab Arm with ongoing overall responses compared with 0 patient in the ICC Arm at the time of data cutoff.

L813: ## Health-Related Quality of Life (HRQoL)
L814: Validated and most frequently used PROs instruments in oncology studies, especially in studies of PD-L1 inhibitors, were used to measure and assess HRQoL from the patient's perspective. These included the (EORTC QLQ-C30) to measure general cancer signs and symptoms, its esophageal cancer-specific module, the EORTC QLQ-OES18, and the EQ-5D-5L) to measure general health, were used to further characterize treatment benefits of tislelizumab beyond radiographic measures.
L815: The assessment of HRQoL was based on the comparison of key PRO endpoints relevant to esophageal cancer. The PRO key endpoints in this studies included global health status (GHS) and physical function as measured by the EORTC QLQ-C-30 and esophageal cancer main symptoms of pain, eating, dysphagia and reflux as measured by EORTC QLQ-OES18.
L816: The completion rates defined as number of patients who completed questionnaires total number of patients in relevant treatment arm at the predefined visits) were calculated for all 3 questionnaires in all cycles, and they remained at or above 95% in both ITT and PD-L1 vCPS \ge 10% populations.
L817: Changes from baseline on the scores of scales and index of EORTC QLQ-C30 and EORTC QLQ-OES18, and on the scores of EQ-5D-5L (VAS) were calculated using descriptive analysis. Time to clinically meaningful worsening, defined as \ge10 points in direction of worsening in the PRO key endpoints were calculated, using stratified log-lank test with HR at 95% CI. The P
L818: values were nominal. The 10-point threshold was selected based on the EORTC developers' original testing (Osoba et al 1998) and what has been used in the previous clinical studies of PD-L1 inhibitors on HRQoL (Adams et al 2020; Necchi et al 2020; Nishijima et al 2019; Harrington et al 2017).
L819: Treatment with tislelizumab was associated with a better overall HRQoL in Study 302. The better outcomes in HRQoL in the Tislelizumab Arm compared with those in the ICC Arm were related to either a reduction in symptoms (e.g., eating and pain) or slower and milder worsening (e.g., dysphagia and reflux). Improvement in physical functioning was observed in the Tislelizumab Arm compared with the ICC Arm.
L820: Furthermore, in the ITT population, analysis of the time to deterioration showed, patients treated with tislelizumab were at a lower risk for clinically meaningful worsening symptoms of dysphagia (stratified HR of 0.76 [95% CI: 0.53, 1.07]) and reflux (0.50 [95% CI: 0.32, 0.80]) as well as physical function (0.67 [95% CI: 0.45, 1.00]) through the course of treatment. The PD-L1 vCPS \ge 10% patients treated with tislelizumab were at lower risk for pain (0.52 [95% CI: 0.24, 1.13]).
L821: Refer to section 8.2.8 for further details.
L822: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L823: In addition to the statistically significant and clinically meaningful improvement in the primary endpoint of OS in the ITT population, tislelizumab also demonstrated statistically significant improvement in the key secondary endpoint of OS in the PD-L1 vCPS \ge 10% population. The survival benefit of tislelizumab over ICC was accompanied by favorable landmark PFS rates at 6, 9 and 12 months, higher ORR, longer DOR and improvements in HRQoL assessment in the key PRO endpoints of eating, pain, and physical functioning and slower and less notable deterioration in dysphagia and reflux.
L824: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L825: Although FDA confirmed the data as presented above, FDA's interpretation of these results is different. As stated above, given the missing PD-L1 expression data on 19% of patients in RATIONALE-302 and that PD-L1 status was not a stratification factor, FDA notes that the analysis of survival in patients based on PD-L1 expression should be interpreted with caution.
L826: FDA also acknowledges the results of the secondary endpoint of PFS, which are considered exploratory. The p-value for PFS is considered nominal. As noted by the Applicant, median PFS was slightly shorter on the tislelizumab arm compared to the ICC arm but the Kaplan-Meier curves for PFS start to separate in favor of the tislelizumab arm approximately 3 months after randomization. FDA notes that the delayed separation of the PFS curves suggests that the proportional hazards assumption may have been violated.
L827: In regards to the ORR analysis, the review team would like to clarify that following RECIST 1.1,
L828: in randomized trials, the response does not need confirmation with a second scan confirming the findings of the first scan separated by at least 4 weeks. Although the ORR in the tislelizumab arm doubled the ORR in the ICC arm, the RATIONALE-302 ORR showed a modest effect of tislelizumab on ORR, but consistent with the class effect in patients with ESCC. ORR and DOR are considered exploratory results only. The p-value presented for ORR is considered nominal.
L829: FDA reviewed the Applicant's presentation of PRO results in the RATIONALE-302 CSR. FDA did not conduct independent analyses of the PRO endpoints. FDA does not agree with the Applicant's claim that treatment with tislelizumab was associated with a better overall HRQoL. No conclusions can or should be drawn regarding HRQoL given that no alpha was allocated to these endpoints and results are to be considered exploratory only. In addition, while FDA acknowledges that the 10-point threshold used to define clinically meaningful worsening has been cited in the Osoba et al. (1998) publication, FDA does not agree with this threshold as there are several limitations to the approach that was used to derive it, and it has not been justified for this specific disease setting. Results of exploratory time to deterioration endpoints should further be interpreted with caution given these limitations. Comparative claims are unsupported.

Tislelizumab exposure-response relationship for efficacy was inconclusive. The finding of effectiveness of tislelizumab was supported by a favorable overall survival of tislelizumab monotherapy against ICC with a stratified hazard ratio (HR) of 0.70 (95% CI 0.57, 0.85).

L857: ## Data:
