L862: ## Post-hoc Analysis of OS
L863: In addition to the above prespecified analysis, the following post-hoc analyses were performed for OS:
L864: - A post-hoc subgroup analysis was performed by excluding patients with baseline PD-L1 missing status
- A post-hoc analysis was performed by adding regions as strata in the analysis method.
- A post-hoc analysis was performed by adding a time-dependent variable, defined by treatment and log(time) interaction into the model to assess the potential violation of proportional hazards assumption in the stratified Cox proportional hazards model.
L865: ## The Applicant's Position:
L866: The OS benefit of Tislelizumab use (ie, HR &lt; 1) was consistent across all prespecified subgroups, including age, gender, smoking status, ICC option, ECOG PS score, region, race, and baseline PD-L1 expression. Imbalances in PD-L1 at baseline and metastatic status at diagnosis or of missing PD-L1 results were demonstrated to have had no impact on the primary endpoint. Thus, the improvement in overall survival with Tislelizumab was observed across all pre-specified and post hoc analyses.
L867: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L868: FDA notes that results of the post-hoc analysis to assess potential violation of proportional hazards and sensitivity analysis to address imbalances in metastatic status at diagnosis have not been presented by the Applicant in this Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation. In general, FDA acknowledges that the OS benefit of tislelizumab appeared consistent across various subgroup analyses and sensitivity analyses (both pre-specified and post-hoc) conducted by the Applicant and by FDA but emphasizes that these analyses are all considered exploratory (except for the key secondary endpoint of OS in PD-L1 vCPS\ge10%, which should also be interpreted with caution for reasons discussed in the Efficacy Results sections).
L869: ## 8.1.3. Integrated Review of Effectiveness
L870: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L871: Study 302 was designed to compare OS in patients with unresectable/metastatic ESCC that has been previously treated with one prior line of systemic therapy by comparing patients who receive tislelizumab to those who receive ICC of either paclitaxel, docetaxel, or irinotecan. Study 302 demonstrated a statistically significant, clinically meaningful improvement in OS for patients randomized to tislelizumab (n = 256) compared to ICC (n = 256). The median OS of patients in the tislelizumab arm was 8.6 months [95% CI: 7.5, 10.4] and the median OS of patients in the ICC arm was 6.3 months [95% CI: 5.3, 7.0] with an OS HR of 0.70 [95% CI:
L872: 0.57, 0.85; one-sided p-value=0.0001]. The pre specified key secondary endpoint of OS in patients with PDL1 vCPS \ge10% (N = 157) demonstrated a statistically significant difference between the tislelizumab arm (n = 89) and the ICC arm (n = 68) at the prespecified alpha level (stratified HR=0.53, 95% CI: 0.37, 0.77; one-sided p-value=0.006). As noted above, however, FDA notes that results of OS in patients with PDL1 vCPS \ge10% data should be interpreted with caution because approximately 19% of patients enrolled had missing PDL1 vCPS information (N = 99) and because PD-L1 status was not a stratification factor for this study. Other exploratory subgroup analyses of OS (e.g. ECOG performance status, race, region) are consistent with the OS benefit seen in the ITT population.
L873: RATIONALE-302 was an adequate and well-controlled clinical trial and the data submitted meet the statutory evidentiary standard for substantial evidence of the effectiveness of tislelizumab based on an OS benefit.
L874: ## 8.1.4. Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
L875: The efficacy of tislelizumab from the pivotal Study 302 is supported by efficacy results from 2 early phase clinical studies in previously treated patients with advanced or metastatic solid tumors including ESCC, study 001 and 102 (Table 15). Given the differences in eligibility criteria, study design, tislelizumab dose, and duration of follow up, efficacy results from the 2 supportive studies are presented separately by study.
L876: ## Primary Endpoints
L877: ## Data:
L878: ## Overall Survival
L879: The survival benefits with tislelizumab treatment was also observed in supportive studies 001 and 102. The median OS was 4.7 months (95% CI: 1.5 to 7.9 months) in Study 001 and with estimated 6-month and 12-month OS rates of 47.4% and 21.6%, respectively. In Study 102, the median OS was 4.8 months (95% CI: 3.6 to 8.5 months) with estimated 6-month and 12-month OS rates of 49.2% and 20.5%, respectively. In Study 102, the median OS was 4.8 months (95% CI: 3.6 to 8.5 months) with estimated 6-month and 12-month OS rates of 49.2% and 20.5%, respectively. The survival results in 001 and 102, compared with the historically observed median OS of 3.7 months and a 12-month OS rate &lt; 13% in the second- or third-line treatment setting of ESCC (Dutton et al 2014), supported the use of tislelizumab in patients with advanced or metastatic ESCC who had been treated previously with systemic therapy.
L880: ## The Applicant's Position:
L881: The OS data from Studies 001 and 102 provided supportive evidence for the efficacy of
L882: tislelizumab monotherapy in patients with advanced or metastatic ESCC who had been heavily treated previously.
L883: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L884: FDA acknowledges the applicant's presentation of OS results from supportive Studies 101 and 102. FDA did not independently verify the results since the results are supportive and exploratory. Further, OS results from single arm trials are not interpretable.
L885: ## Secondary Endpoints
L886: ## Progression-Free Survival (PFS)
L887: The median PFS was 2.0 months (95% CI: 1.3 to 4.2 months) in Study 001 and 2.1 months (95% CI: 2.0 to 4.2 months) in Study 102. The estimated PFS rates at 3 months and at 6 months were 38.5% and 23.1%, respectively, in Study 001, and 32.4% and 18.5%, respectively, in Study 102.
L888: ## Objective Response and Disease Control Rate
L889: Studies 001 and 102 supported tumor responses observed with tislelizumab in Study 302 with an encouraging ORR in patients with advanced or metastatic ESCC who had been heavily treated previously. The ORRs were 19.2% (95% CI: 6.6% to 39.4%) and 7.7% (95% CI: 0.9% to 25.1%) in Studies 001 and 102, respectively. The DCRs were 42.3% (95% CI: 23.4% to 63.1%) and 34.6% (95% CI: 17.2% to 55.7%) in Studies 001 and 102, respectively. More patients in Study 102 (38.5%) received \ge 3 lines of treatment than in Study 001 (11.5%), which may have contributed to the lower ORR and DCR in Study 102 compared with Study 001. However, the ORR in Study 102 was considered favorable when compared with the historical ORR of 0.4% to 3% observed in patients in the second- or third-line treatment setting (Dutton et al 2014). In general, the antitumor response observed in Studies 001 and 102 was comparable to the results in the Phase 2 studies with pembrolizumab (the KEYNOTE-180 study) and nivolumab (the ATTRACTION-1 study) in similar heavily pretreated populations.
L890: ## Duration of Response
L891: Studies 001 and 102 supported the durable antitumor activity of tislelizumab by showing an encouraging 12-month DOR rate in patients with advanced or metastatic ESCC who had been heavily treated previously. In Study 001, the median DOR was 4.2 months (95% CI: 2.8 months to NE) and the 12-month DOR rate was 40.0%. In Study 102, the median DOR was not reached, as 1 of 2 patients who achieved a response continued to respond to treatment as of the cutoff date; both patients achieved a DOR of \ge 12 months.
L892: ## The Applicant's Position:
L893: The efficacy results based on secondary endpoints from Studies 001 and 102 provided supportive evidence for the efficacy of tislelizumab monotherapy in patients with advanced or metastatic ESCC who had been heavily treated previously.
L894: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L895: FDA acknowledges the applicant's presentation of results for secondary endpoints for studies 101 and 102. FDA did not independently verify the results since these are supportive and considered exploratory. Furthermore, time to event endpoints like OS, PFS, and DCR results from single arm trials are not interpretable.
L896: ## Subpopulations
L897: ## Data:
L898: Subgroup analyses were not performed across studies as Studies 001 and 102 were single arm studies.
L899: ## The Applicant's Position:
L900: Not Applicable
L901: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L902: Not applicable.
L903: ## Additional Efficacy Considerations
L904: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L905: Not applicable.
L906: ## 8.1.5. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L907: ## Data:
L908: As a global multicenter study, Study 302 was designed in accordance with ICH guidelines (including ICH-E17) and international treatment guidelines for patients with ESCC. Key parameters of the study (ie, patient population, stratification factors, choice of comparator, primary endpoint, statistical analysis plan, and safety management plan) were designed in consultation with the US FDA, the European Medicines Agency (EMA), the Center for Drug Evaluation of the National Medical Products Administration (NMPA), and the Pharmaceuticals and Medical Devices Agency (PMDA).
L909: The patients enrolled in Study 302 are representative of patients with advanced or metastatic disease in the US. The control arm received the globally accepted standard of care at the time of
L910: accrual. The results from the pivotal Phase 3 Study 302 and the totality of the supporting clinical data demonstrate that tislelizumab provides benefit to patients with advanced or metastatic ESCC, with clinically and statistically significant improvements in OS as compared with chemotherapy. The improvement in OS with tislelizumab over ICC (ie, HR &lt; 1) was consistently observed across all prespecified subgroups (ie, age, gender, smoking status, baseline ECOG PS score, ICC option, race, region and PD-L1 expression status) and was accompanied by greater and more durable tumor responses, favorable PFS landmark rates after 3 months, and better reported HRQoL.
L911: Specifically, in Study 302, tislelizumab was superior to ICC for the primary endpoint of OS in the ITT population, with a statistically significant 30% reduction (1-sided p-value = 0.0001) in the risk of death and a clinically meaningful improvement in median OS of 2.3 months. tislelizumab also showed superiority to ICC for the key secondary endpoint of OS in the prespecified PD-L1 vCPS \ge 10% population, with a statistically significant 46% reduction (1-sided p-value = 0.0006) in the risk of death and a clinically meaningful improvement in median OS of 3.5 months.
L912: The improvement in OS with tislelizumab was also observed across prespecified subgroups in Study 302. Two subgroups of interest are summarized below:
L913: - By region: HR of OS was 0.55 (95% CI: 0.35 to 0.87) in patient from Europe/North America and 0.73 (95% CI: 0.59 to 0.90) in patient from Asia
- By baseline PD-L1 expression status: HR of OS was 0.54 (95% CI: 0.36 to 0.79) in patients with PD-L1 vCPS \ge 10%, 0.82 (95% CI: 0.62 to 1.09) in patients with PD-L1 vCPS &lt; 10%, and 0.67 (95% CI: 0.41 to 1.12) in patients with missing PD-L1 status
L914: The efficacy of tislelizumab was also observed across secondary endpoints of PFS, ORR, DOR, and HRQoL in Study 302, supporting the primary endpoint of OS:
L915: - **PFS:** Treatment with tislelizumab showed a PFS HR of 0.83 (95% CI: 0.67 to 1.01). Separation of the PFS Kaplan-Meier curves began approximately 3 months after randomization and favored tislelizumab for the remainder of the study (Figure 18) as indicated by PFS landmark analysis at 3, 6, 9 and 12 months.
- **ORR:** Tislelizumab treatment resulted in a clinically relevant increase in ORR compared with ICC (20.3% [95% CI: 15.6% to 25.8%] versus 9.8% [95% CI: 6.4% to 14.1%]) in Study 302, supported by response rated observed in the supportive studies 001 and 102 where patients were heavily pre-treated
- **DOR:** The median DOR was 7.1 months (95% CI: 4.1 to 11.3 months) in the Tislelizumab Arm compared with 4.0 months (95% CI: 2.1 to 8.2 months) in the ICC Arm, with 10 of 52 patients (19.2%) having ongoing overall responses at the time of data cutoff
L916: - **HRQoL:** The risk of worsening of the key esophageal cancer symptoms, particularly reflux, dysphagia, and physical functioning was lower in the Tislelizumab Arm compared with the ICC Arm.
L917: Efficacy results from patients with advanced or metastatic ESCC in Studies 001 and 102 provide supportive evidence of the efficacy of tislelizumab monotherapy in patients with advanced or metastatic ESCC who had been heavily pre- treated, the most patients (65.4% in Study 001 and 76.9% in Study 102) who were enrolled in these 2 studies had received \ge 2 prior lines of therapy.
L918: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L919: The efficacy results from the pivotal Study 302, together with the results from the 2 supportive Studies 001 and 102, provide evidence that tislelizumab is an effective treatment for patients with advanced or metastatic ESCC after prior systemic treatment in support of the proposed indication.
L920: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L921: FDA agrees that the primary OS results of RATIONALE-302 provide substantial evidence of the effectiveness of tislelizumab for the treatment of patients with advanced or metastatic ESCC with disease progression after one prior line of treatment. See FDA's assessment in Section 8.1.3 ("Integrated Review of Effectiveness"), above.
L922: ## 8.2. Review of Safety
L923: ## **Data:**
L924: ## 8.2.1. Overall Safety Evaluation Plan
L925: The safety analysis for this BLA is based primarily on the pivotal study, Study 302. The Study 302 dataset presents a clear comparison of the safety data of tislelizumab with the standard of care at the time of study initiation, which was single-agent chemotherapy. The safety data from the ESCC population represents the total experience of tislelizumab as treatment for ESCC at the time of BLA submission.
L926: Further details of these populations are provided in section 8.2.2 and safety assessment are listed in Table 26.

L943: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L944: The size of the safety database in this submission is adequate to support the use of tislelizumab monotherapy in the treatment of patients with unresectable recurrent locally advanced or metastatic ESCC after prior systemic therapy.
L945: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L946: FDA agrees with the summary description of the studies used to assess the safety of tislelizumab and agrees that the size of the safety database in this submission is adequate to characterize the safety profile of tislelizumab in this setting.
L947: ## **8.2.2. Safety Review Approach**
L948: The safety data package in support of this submission included the following 3 patient populations:
L949: **Study 302 population** (N = 495) included patients from the global Phase 3 study evaluating tislelizumab monotherapy (Tislelizumab Arm; n = 255) versus ICC of paclitaxel, docetaxel, or irinotecan (ICC Arm; n = 240) as the treatment for patients with ESCC after prior systemic therapy.
L950: **The ESCC population** (N = 307) included patients with ESCC who received tislelizumab from Study 302 (N = 255), Study 102 (N = 26), and Study 001 (N = 26). The safety results from the ESCC population were similar to that of the Study 302 population.
L951: **The All Doses All Indications population** (N = 1972) included patients treated with tislelizumab monotherapy from studies evaluating tislelizumab in multiple tumor types with available CSRs. This included the above 3 studies plus Studies 303, 208, 204, and 203. Patients who received comparator agents in these studies are not included in this population.
L952: The goal of this analysis of an integrated safety population is to provide a comprehensive safety profile for tislelizumab and to provide supportive data for the safety profile of tislelizumab in a larger population, as well as to identify possible rare safety signals. The analysis of this population reflects the full experience with tislelizumab in advanced cancers, including ESCC.
L953: This integrated safety population includes patients with different tumor types who received \ge 1 dose of tislelizumab monotherapy. Of these, 77.8% of patients received tislelizumab 200 mg once every 3 weeks. Patients in the supportive Studies 102, 303, 208, 204, and 203 received 200 mg once every 3 weeks, while patients in Study 001 received a variety of dose regimens, including 200 mg once every 3 weeks.
L954: All patients in Study 102 received 200 mg once every 3 weeks; 57 of the 300 patients (PK substudy) had a first-cycle duration of 28-days instead of 21 days, which was not considered to be a different dose regimen.
L955: Tislelizumab was administered intravenously at a dose of 200 mg once every 3 weeks in all studies, with the exception of Study 001 in which tislelizumab was administered at either 200 mg once every 3 weeks, or after a weight based- regimens including 0.5, 2.0, 5.0, 10.0 mg/kg once every 2 weeks, 2.0 and 5.0 mg/kg once every 3 weeks. Approximately 97% of the patients in the All Doses All Indications population received tislelizumab 200 mg once every 3 weeks or 2.0 and 5.0 mg/kg once every 3 weeks. Key features of all studies are summarized in Table 26.
L956: ## The Applicant's Position:
L957: The totality of the safety package is sufficient to characterize the safety profile of tislelizumab monotherapy and to guide the management of risks associated with tislelizumab as a treatment for ESCC. The safety review is focused on patients who received the dose of 200 mg once every 3 weeks (Table 26) as recommended by the proposed label.
L958: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L959: FDA agrees that the size of the safety database in this submission is adequate to characterize the safety profile of tislelizumab for the treatment of ESCC. For pivotal Study 302, the safety population consists of 255 patients who received tislelizumab 200 mg IV q3 weeks and 240 patients who received ICC. For the ISS, the safety population consists of 1,972 patients who received tislelizumab across seven studies: Study 102, Study 203, Study 204, Study 208, Study 302, Study 303, and Study 001, as described in the Applicant's table above. Although FDA's assessment of the safety of tislelizumab for the proposed indication is based on the overall safety data submitted in this Application, the review is focused on the results of RATIONALE-302.
L960: The Applicant included analyses of relatedness of AEs. Following standard FDA review practices, FDA review did not include relatedness in the safety analyses as the safety profile is best assessed through the comparison with the control arm. Thus, the FDA will not comment on the Applicant's analysis of "drug-related" TEAEs, except for Grade 5 TEAEs.
L961: ## 8.2.3. Review of the Safety Database
L962: ## Overall Exposure
L963: ## Data:
L964: In Study 302, a near 2-fold longer median duration of exposure was observed in the Tislelizumab Arm compared with the ICC Arm (2.76 months versus 1.49 months, respectively). The proportion of patients who received \ge 6 months of exposure was higher in the Tislelizumab Arm (25.5%) compared with the ICC Arm (8.8%). The median relative dose intensity in the Tislelizumab Arm was 100% compared with 89.4% for the ICC Arm. These differences are likely the result of an improvement in efficacy and a better tolerated safety profile for
L965: tislelizumab compared with chemotherapy. With a quarter of the patients receiving tislelizumab for more than 6 months in Study 302 and a median follow-up duration of 8.51 months, the overall exposure for tislelizumab was considered appropriate for an evaluation of safety in patients who were representative of the intended target patient population.
L966: Given the differences in exposure between the two arms described above, exposure-adjusted incidence rates were provided for key adverse event analyses (all grades, serious events, fatal events) of the Study 302 population in addition to overall event incidence.
L967: The exposure for patients in Study 302 was lower than the exposure for patients in the All Doses All Indications population for median duration (2.76 months versus 4.11 months, respectively), and fewer patients in Study 302 received at least 6 months of treatment (25.5% versus 37.3%). This may reflect differences in study population, line of therapy, study timing, and tumor type. For example, Study 302 enrolled more patients with ECOG PS score of 1 (74.5%) and had a population with more comorbidities associated with tobacco and alcohol use (73.3% current or former smoking status, 70.6% current or former alcohol use) than All Doses All Indications population (66.6% ECOG PS score of 1, 56.8% current or former smoking status, 30.8% current or former alcohol use); these are recognized differences between advanced ESCC and other tumor types.
L968: A difference in tislelizumab regimen existed between the Study 302 population and the All Doses All Indications population. The Study 302 population was treated with 200 mg once every 3 weeks regimen; the All Doses All Indications population was treated with 200 mg once every 3 weeks regimen (Studies 302, 102, 303, 208, 204, 203, and 001 [Part 3]) or weight-based regimens including 0.5, 2.0, 5.0, 10.0 mg/kg once every 2 weeks, 2.0 and 5.0 mg/kg once every 3 weeks (Study 001 Parts 1 and 2). Only a small proportion of patients (3.2%) in the All Doses All Indications population were treated with a regimen outside of these 3 dose regimens; hence, the difference in tislelizumab regimen is unlikely to impact the assessment of tislelizumab safety.
L969: ## The Applicant's Position:
L970: The Tislelizumab Arm had greater treatment exposure than the ICC Arm with longer median duration of exposure, a higher proportion of patients receiving \ge 6 months of study treatment, and a higher median relative dose intensity. These differences are likely the result of an improvement in efficacy and a better tolerability profile for tislelizumab compared with chemotherapy.
L971: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L972: FDA verified the results summarized above. Median treatment duration in the tislelizumab arm was 2.8 months compared to the ICC arm (1.5 months) and with the approximately 10% higher median relative dose intensity in the tislelizumab arm compared to the ICC arm in Study 302. FDA would not necessarily agree with the Applicant's conclusions that the tislelizumab is better
L973: tolerated. Although patients receiving anti-PD-1 therapy may have less myelosuppression and short-term GI toxicity than chemotherapy, tislelizumab may cause severe immune related events in a subset of patients, dramatically impacting those patients' quality of life.
L974: ## Relevant characteristics of the safety population:
L975: ## Data:
L976: ## Patient Disposition
L977: A higher proportion of patients remained on study treatment in the Tislelizumab Arm (6.3%) than in the ICC Arm (0.4%), as of the data cutoff date. Disease progression was the primary reason for treatment discontinuation in both arms, and a higher proportion was observed in the Tislelizumab Arm (69.4%) than the ICC Arm (55.4%). Adverse event was the second most common reason for treatment discontinuation in both arms, and a lower proportion of patients reported discontinuation due to adverse event in the Tislelizumab Arm (12.2%) than the ICC Arm (18.3%). These results would suggest that tislelizumab has a better-tolerated safety profile than chemotherapy.
L978: Patients in the ICC Arm discontinued study treatment due to patient withdrawal (12.1%) and investigator decision (2.1%) in higher proportions compared with patients in the Tislelizumab Arm (2.4% patient withdrawal, 0% investigator withdrawal). As described below, the safety profile of tislelizumab in Study 302 was similar to the safety profile of the ESCC population and All Doses All Indications; this similarity suggests no major impact from the open-label design.
L979: ## Demographic and Baseline Characteristics
L980: The demographic and baseline characteristics were balanced between the 2 arms in the key characteristics (Table 17). The higher proportion of patients in the Tislelizumab Arm (34.9%) compared with patients in the ICC Arm (27.1%) whose tumor had a known PD-L1 expression status of vCPS \ge 10% is not known to impact clinical safety. The Study 302 population was consistent with the protocol-defined eligibility criteria and was representative of the intended target patient population.
L981: ## All Doses All Indications Population
L982: The patterns of patient disposition and reason for discontinuation in the All Doses All Indications population were each similar to those in the Study 302 Tislelizumab Arm. Disease progression was the primary reason for study treatment discontinuation, and death was the primary reason for discontinuation from the study. The demographic and baseline characteristics for patients in the All Doses All Indications population were consistent with those in Study 302 Tislelizumab Arm. The medical history and prior anticancer therapy reflected the differences in tumor types between the Study 302 population and the All Doses All Indications population.
L983: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L984: The demographics and baseline characteristics were balanced between arms, except for PD-L1 expression status, where a higher proportion of patients in the Tislelizumab Arm had a PD-L1 expression status of vCPS \ge 10%. Patients included in the safety datasets are representative of US patients with advanced or metastatic ESCC following prior systemic therapy
L985: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L986: Table 17 in Section 8 described the demographics of the overall population. However, of the randomized patients, a total of 17 patients (one randomized to receive tislelizumab [0.4%] and 16 [16.3%] randomized to receive ICC) did not receive any study treatment. The most common primary reason for not receiving study treatment in the ICC Arm was “withdrawal by subject” (11 patients [4.3%]) which may be related to patients not wanting to receive standard cytotoxic chemotherapy on this open-label study. The demographics of the safety population were similar to that of the efficacy population and was generally balanced between the 2 arms as shown in FDA's Table 28, below.

L991: Regarding patient disposition, FDA analysis confirms that a higher percentage of patients in the ICC arm compared to the tislelizumab arm had drug interruptions due to any-cause AEs (33% vs 23%) and permanent discontinuations due to any-cause AEs (27% vs 19%).
L992: FDA would not concur with the Applicant's statements regarding the better tolerated safety profile of tislelizumab for the reasons described in the prior section of this review.
L993: ## 8.2.4. Adequacy of the safety database:
L994: ## Data:
L995: The totality of the safety package is sufficient to characterize the safety profile of tislelizumab and to guide the management of risks associated with tislelizumab as a treatment for recurrent,
L996: locally advanced, metastatic ESCC after prior systemic treatment. The overview of safety focuses on safety data from the Study 302 population and includes safety data from the pooled populations where relevant (see Table 26). For data integration, adverse event preferred terms (PTs) and system organ classes were coded using the Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA) Version 23.0. Adverse event severity grading was based on the National Cancer Institute-Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (NCI-CTCAE) Version 4.03.
L997: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L998: Study 302 was designed and initiated when single-agent chemotherapy was considered standard of care and the study design enrolled patients with advanced unresectable/metastatic ESCC that has progressed during or after first line therapy.
L999: A potential weakness in the design of Study 302 is the open-label identification of the specific drug, whether tislelizumab or one of the 3 chemotherapeutic agents, assigned to the patient. This element of the study may bias the reporting of adverse events and may have influenced the higher withdrawal rate by patient and by investigator seen in the ICC Arm compared with the Tislelizumab Arm. However, within the analyses of safety data, no obvious impact on the safety results was noted.
L1000: Regardless of the limitations noted, the totality of the safety package is sufficient to characterize the safety profile of tislelizumab and to guide the management of risks associated with tislelizumab as a treatment for ESCC.
L1001: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1002: FDA agrees that the safety database is adequate to evaluate the safety of tislelizumab compared to single-agent chemotherapy in this setting. See FDA's comments in Section 8.2.1, above.
L1003: ## 8.2.5. Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L1004: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L1005: ## **Data:**
L1006: The clinical sites for the safety analysis groups were monitored by clinical research associates following study-specific monitoring plans for consistency. Data were queried per study-specific data management plans. Additionally, external and internal Safety Monitoring Committee (SMC) reviewed safety data for all studies.
L1007: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1008: No issues were identified regarding data integrity or submission quality that had an effect on the safety review.
L1009: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1010: Prior to conducting the safety review, FDA's Office of Computational Science (OCS) generated a CoreDF report of data quality which did not identify any significant or unusual data issues. During the safety review, FDA did not identify any significant data issues that would impact the safety review or overall conclusions.
L1011: ## **Categorization of Adverse Event**
L1012: ## **Data:**
L1013: All adverse event summaries were based on the Safety Analysis Set. A treatment-emergent adverse event was defined as an adverse event that had an onset date or a worsening in severity from baseline (pretreatment) on or after the first dose of study treatment up to 30 days after study treatment discontinuation or initiation of a new anticancer therapy, whichever occurred first. Treatment-emergent adverse events also included all immune-mediated adverse events (for the Tislelizumab Arm) up to 90 days after the last dose of study treatment regardless of whether a patient started a new anticancer therapy. Only treatment-emergent adverse events were included in the summary tables.
L1014: The adverse event verbatim descriptions (investigator's description from the eCRF) were classified into standardized medical terminology using MedDRA. Adverse events were coded to MedDRA (Version 23.0) lower-level term closest to the verbatim term. The linked MedDRA System Organ Class (SOC) and PT were also classified.
L1015: The incidence of adverse events was summarized as the number (percentage) of patients with adverse events by SOC, PT, severity, and relationship to study drug. Patients with multiple occurrences of the same adverse event within a specific SOC and PT were counted only once within the SOC and PT, and the maximum grade per NCI-CTCAE (Version 4.03) was used. Serious adverse events, adverse events of \ge Grade 3, adverse events leading to treatment discontinuation or dose modification, and adverse events leading to death were also summarized by SOC, PT, and relationship to study drug.
L1016: All deaths and causes of death were summarized, including those that occurred during the study treatment period and those reported during the survival follow-up period after treatment completion/discontinuation.
L1017: Immune-mediated adverse events were identified according a 2-step process which included generation of a potential immune-mediated adverse event list and medical evaluation of potential immune-mediated adverse events. The incidence of immune-mediated adverse event (for all grades and \ge Grade 3) was summarized by category and worst grade per NCI-CTCAE (Version 4.03). The immune-mediated adverse events were also summarized by outcome and category. The immune-mediated adverse events leading to treatment discontinuation, dose modification, and death were summarized by category.
L1018: Infusion-related reactions were summarized based on the adverse event verbatim terms reported by the investigator and the list of symptoms/signs of infusion-related reactions outlined in the protocol and their association with a tislelizumab infusion in terms of causality and onset time.
L1019: If the adverse event term did not include the term infusion-related reactions, but met all of the following criteria:
L1020: - one or more of the infusion related reactions-associated symptoms per protocol
- occurred up to 1 day after the onset of the event
- deemed related to tislelizumab by investigator
L1021: the terms were firstly reviewed by the medical monitor, and then the investigator was queried to clarify if the event was considered an infusion related reactions, in which case, the adverse event term would have been updated to include the term infusion-related reactions. Infusion-related reactions were summarized by SOC, PT, and worst grade. Infusion related reactions leading to treatment discontinuation or dose modification were summarized by PT level.
L1022: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1023: Adverse events were collected and analyzed as described above.
L1024: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1025: National Cancer Institute Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (NCI-CTCAE), Version 4.03 were used to assess the severity of all TEAEs. The MedDRA version 23.0 dictionary was used to code TEAE data. TEAEs were assessed from the time of study treatment initiation and for 30 days after the last dose of study treatment. Because of the long half-life of monoclonal antibodies and the potential for late-onset treatment-emergent imAEs beyond the 30-day period following the last dose of tislelizumab, imAEs were also followed for 90 days after the last dose of study treatment per the study protocol.
L1026: Note that FDA, as indicated above, does not consider Applicant's attribution of whether an AE was drug-related or not when reviewing safety data.
L1027: ## Routine Clinical Tests
L1028: ## **Data:**
L1029: Key assessments included routine clinical laboratory tests (hematology, chemistry, thyroid function), ECGs, vital sign assessments, and pregnancy tests (Table 12).
L1030: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1031: Laboratory assessments were carried out as specified in the BGB-A317-302 protocol.
L1032: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1033: Overall, the routine clinical laboratory tests and physical exam elements that were performed in Study 302 are considered adequate for the evaluation of safety of tislelizumab in the target patient population.
L1034: ## 8.2.6. Safety Results
L1035: ## Deaths
L1036: ## **Data:**
L1037: Deaths due to adverse events (not due to ESCC) were reported with similar incidence in both arms (5.5% [14 patients] in the Tislelizumab Arm versus 5.8% [14 patients] in the ICC Arm) (Table 28).
L1038: Table 27 provides a summary of all fatal adverse events as documented on the Study 302 adverse event case report form, including events in 21 patients in the tislelizumab arm for whom “disease under study” (ie, ESCC) was reported as the primary cause of death. A review of the events indicates no clustering of event types, except for effects typically associated with ESCC disease progression. None of the deaths was associated with an immune-associated adverse event or an infusion related-reaction. Three patients (1.2%) in the Tislelizumab Arm and 1 patient (0.4%) in the ICC Arm died of ‘multiple organ dysfunction syndrome’. These 3 multi-organ events in the Tislelizumab Arm were considered by the Investigator to be due to the underlying ESCC and were unrelated to study drug, whereas the multi-organ failure in the ICC Arm was considered related to study drug (irinotecan). Deaths reported as due to “indeterminate” cause (7.1% in each treatment arm) or “other” (2.7% Tislelizumab Arm; 2.9% ICC Arm) occurred with an onset of &gt; 30 days after the last dose of study drug (ie, not treatment-emergent). Although the exact causes of deaths remain unclear, they were not reported by the Investigator to be due to adverse events and are presumed likely therefore to be disease related or of unknown etiology.
L1039: To account for the difference between the treatment arms in duration of exposure, an exposure-adjusted assessment of adverse events leading to death was conducted, which showed a notably lower rate in the Tislelizumab Arm (2.8 deaths per 100 person-months) compared with the ICC Arm (4.7 deaths per 100 person-months).
L1040: An assessment of deaths was conducted by geographic region and by baseline PD-L1 expression status. The overall trends and reasons for death in these subgroups were similar to those of the overall population for both the Tislelizumab and ICC Arms.

L1047: c The adverse event which was reported to be the cause of death in 1 patient in each arm had its event onset date &gt; 30 days after last dose and was not considered related to the study drug by the investigator, therefore, those two adverse events did not meet the definition of TEAE.

L1051: Noting the differences in tumor types, disease status, event reporting rules, and durations of follow-up between safety populations, deaths in the All Doses All Indications population, as in Study 302, predominantly occurred &gt; 30 days after the last dose of study drug and were likely due to the underlying malignancies. Treatment-emergent adverse events leading to death occurred in 2.3% of patients in the All Doses All Indications population. Most deaths in the All Doses All Indications population were considered to be unrelated to study drug.
L1052: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1053: The majority of deaths were due to disease under study. Deaths due to adverse events (not due to ESCC) were reported in low and similar proportions for both arms (tislelizumab [5.5%] and chemotherapy [5.8%]). The overall trend for death and reason for death were consistent across geographic region and baseline PD-L1 expression status.
L1054: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1055: FDA's analysis identified the same number of total deaths and deaths beyond 30 days after the last dose as the Applicant's Table 27. For deaths within 30 days after the last dose, FDA's analysis counted fewer deaths than the Applicant for both the tislelizumab arm (n = 13) and the ICC arm (n = 11). The reason for this discrepancy is the Applicant counting deaths due to disease progression as fatal AEs in its ADAE dataset (but not the ADSL dataset). See also the discussion of Table 29, below. Regardless, FDA agrees that there were more deaths in the ICC arm compared to the tislelizumab arm and that, in both arms, deaths were most often due to progression of disease.
L1056: FDA further examined the total number of deaths in the tislelizumab arm that were classified in the datasets as being due to “indeterminate” (n = 18), “other” (n = 7), and “concurrent illness” (n = 4). After reviewing these CRFs and/or narratives, FDA categorizes deaths in the tislelizumab arm as below (Table 29) with deaths in “other” and “concurrent illness” being re-categorized to either disease under study/progression of disease, adverse event, or indeterminate.
L1057: **Table 29. RATIONALE-302: Total Deaths in Tislelizumab Arm**
L1058: Total Deaths	196 (77%)
Disease Under Study	157 (62%)
Indeterminate	21 (8%)
Adverse Event	18 (7%)
Other	0
Concurrent Illness	0
L1059: For deaths other than those related to disease progression, the cause of death is difficult to
L1060: allocate into a single category, as these deaths are often being due to a combination of events involving the disease itself, comorbidities, and AEs due to treatment. As summarized by the Applicant above, all events of multiorgan failure were attributed to therapy in the ICC arm while the single event reported in the tislelizumab arm was attributed to disease progression, highlighting the challenges to interpret some causes of death common to treatment toxicity and disease progression in patients with advanced disease.
L1061: FDA does not agree with the Applicant's analysis summarized in Table 27. After further review of the CRFs and/or narratives, in FDA's analysis 18 deaths in the tislelizumab are due to AEs (where tislelizumab may have contributed to the event; however, causality is difficult to determine in all cases). Examination of the specific AEs resulting in death does not indicate a notable imbalance between the study arms and the events leading to death do not appear different from what is expected in this patient population undergoing treatment. Of note, 4 patients died of hemorrhages in the tislelizumab arm (Table 28).
L1062: FDA does not agree with the Applicant's statement that none of the deaths was associated with an immune-associated adverse event. One patient (ID# [REDACTED](b)(6)) on the tislelizumab arm died from hepatic failure on Day 45 of the study; based on the narrative, immune-mediated hepatitis could not be ruled out.
L1063: ## Serious Adverse Events
L1064: ## Data:
L1065: In Study 302, serious adverse events (SAEs) were reported with similar overall incidence in the two treatment arms (41.2% Tislelizumab Arm versus 43.8% ICC Arm). The exposure adjusted event rates for SAEs were lower in the Tislelizumab Arm compared with the ICC Arm at 12.5 per 100 person-months versus 28.9 per 100 person-months, respectively.
L1066: By preferred term, pneumonia was the most common SAE reported for both treatment arms (7.1% in both arms) (Table 30). Dysphagia was the only SAE that occurred with \ge 2\% higher incidence in the Tislelizumab Arm compared with the ICC Arm (4.7% versus 1.7%, respectively); the events were generally associated with the underlying ESCC and were not considered treatment related.
L1067: SAEs reported with \ge 2\% lower incidence in the Tislelizumab Arm compared with the ICC Arm included diarrhea (0% versus 3.3%), decreased appetite (0.4% versus 2.5%), febrile neutropenia (0% versus 3.3%), neutrophil count decreased (0% versus 4.2%), and white blood cell decreased (0% versus 3.3%).
L1068: In both arms, most SAEs were considered unrelated to study drug. The incidence of treatment-related SAEs was lower in the Tislelizumab Arm than in the ICC Arm (14.1% versus 19.6%, respectively) even without taking exposure-adjusted event rates into consideration.

L1094: FDA's analysis concurs with that of the Applicant regarding the SAEs that occurred at \ge 1\% in either arm with the following notes and/or exceptions below:
L1095: - Grouping terms for esophageal fistulas (including esophagomediastinal, tracheoesophageal, and esophagobronchial), incidence for this event is 2.3% and 2.5% in the tislelizumab and ICC arms, respectively. Given the nature of ESCC, esophageal fistulas are an important AE to monitor for, regardless of treatment modality.
- FDA combined diarrhea and colitis into one grouped-term (GT) and calculated the incidence of this event as 0.4% and 3.8% in the tislelizumab and ICC arms respectively.
- FDA combined aspiration pneumonia with pneumonia to calculate the incidence as 9% and 8% in the tislelizumab and ICC arms, respectively.
- FDA combined hemorrhage terms to calculate the incidence as 4.3% and 4.2% in the tislelizumab and ICC arms, respectively.
- FDA combined musculoskeletal pain terms to calculate the incidence as 1.2% and 0.4% in the tislelizumab and ICC arms, respectively.
L1096: The SAEs noted above are generally consistent with ESCC or are often associated with cytotoxic chemotherapy (e.g. diarrhea, nausea, febrile neutropenia, decreased neutrophil count, and decreased white blood cell count). For both the tislelizumab arm and the ICC arm, pneumonia was the most frequently reported SAE, as noted by the Applicant, above.
L1097: SAEs of dysphagia and pneumonitis appeared to occur more frequently in the tislelizumab arm compared to the ICC arm. The reason for dysphagia is unclear but pneumonitis is a known risk from anti-PD1(L1) antibodies.
L1098: ## Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Effects
L1099: ## Data:
L1100: Tislelizumab was associated with a lower incidence of adverse events (19.2%) that resulted in permanent discontinuation of study treatment compared with the ICC Arm (26.7%) in Study 302. A general trend of higher event rate of adverse events leading to treatment discontinuation was observed across multiple preferred terms in the ICC Arm, without clustering in any specific group. Adverse events leading to dropout/treatment discontinuation by region and by baseline PD-L1 expression status showed similar trends across all subgroups and between arms for each subgroup as those seen in the overall population.
L1101: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1102: Fewer adverse events leading to treatment discontinuation were observed in patients in the tislelizumab arm compared with patients in the ICC arm.
L1103: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1104: FDA agrees that 19% and 27% of patients permanently discontinued tislelizumab and ICC, respectively, due to AEs. As with SAEs, the events leading to treatment discontinuation are consistent with morbidity associated with ESCC itself as well as treatment toxicity (e.g. pneumonitis for the tislelizumab arm and diarrhea and fatigue for the ICC arm).
L1105: The AEs with incidence \ge 2% that led to permanent discontinuation of tislelizumab were:
L1106: - hemorrhage (2.4%)
- pneumonitis (2.4%)
- pneumonia (2%)
L1107: The AEs with incidence \ge 2% that led to permanent discontinuation of ICC were:
L1108: - hemorrhage (2.9%)
- pneumonia (2.1%)
- diarrhea (2.1%)
- fatigue (2.1%)
L1109: ## Dose Interruption/Reduction Due to Adverse Effects
L1110: ## Data:
L1111: For tislelizumab, dose modification included delays or interruptions in dosing. For ICC, dose reductions were allowed in addition to delays or interruptions. In Study 302, fewer patients in the Tislelizumab Arm (22.7%) required dose modification of study treatment compared with the ICC Arm (47.9%), suggesting that tislelizumab may be better tolerated than ICC.
L1112: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1113: Fewer adverse events leading to dose modification were observed in patients in the tislelizumab group compared with patients in the ICC group.
L1114: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1115: FDA agrees with the assessment above in that fewer dose interruptions were observed in the tislelizumab arm (23% by FDA calculation) compared to the ICC arm (~57% by FDA calculation). Management of dose modifications was different between study arms; while the standard of care with immune checkpoints is dose interruptions until recovery, with chemotherapy, often dose delays are followed by dose reductions.
L1116: ## **Significant Adverse Events**
L1117: ## **Data:**
L1118: ## **Immune-Mediated Adverse Events**
L1119: Overall, immune-mediated adverse events of any grade were reported in 54 patients (21.2%) treated with tislelizumab (Table 32). The prespecified adverse event searches for Study 302 included events of immune-mediated hypothyroidism, immune-mediated pneumonitis, immune-mediated skin reactions, immune-mediated colitis, immune-mediated hepatitis, immune-mediated myositis/rhabdomyolysis, immune-mediated adrenal insufficiency, immune-mediated myocarditis, and immune-mediated pituitary dysfunction. Note that the terms “immune-related adverse event” and “immune-mediated adverse event” are used interchangeably across various documents in the submission. Also note that the protocol did not specify collection of immune-mediated adverse events for ICC.
L1120: Consistent with other anti-PD-L1 inhibitors, tislelizumab was associated with immune-mediated adverse events. In tislelizumab-treated patients in Study 302, 21.2% reported immune-mediated adverse events of any grade, and 3.5% reported immune-mediated adverse events of \ge Grade 3 (Table 32). Serious immune-mediated adverse events were reported for 5.9% of tislelizumab treated- patients. None of the events was fatal.
L1121: Table 33 presents a summary of analyses relevant to immune-mediated adverse events, including adverse events by grade, event outcomes (discontinuation, death), and treatments administered. The most common immune-mediated adverse events (any grade) were immune-mediated hypothyroidism (9.0%), immune-mediated pneumonitis (7.1%), and immune-mediated skin adverse reaction (2.0%). These immune-mediated adverse events led to tislelizumab treatment discontinuation or dose modification (ie, delay or interruption) in 3.1% and 6.3% of patients, respectively. All patients who experienced immune-mediated
L1122: hypothyroidism received hormone replacement therapy. High-dose systemic corticosteroids (\ge 40 mg/day prednisone equivalent) were given as an intervention in 22 of the 54 patients (40.7%) who experienced immune-mediated adverse events; none of the patients received other immunosuppressive drugs (eg, Tumor Necrosis Factor (TNF) inhibitors or mycophenolate mofetil).
L1123: The above results were consistent with those seen in the All Doses All Indications population, in which immune-mediated adverse events of any grade were reported in 15.5% of patients and immune-mediated adverse events of \ge Grade 3 were reported in 4.5% of patients. Four patients (0.2%) died from an immune-mediated adverse event (3 from immune-mediated pneumonitis, 1 from immune-mediated hepatitis).

L1137: ## Infusion-Related Reactions (IRRs)
L1138: Infusion-related reactions were identified at the study level in accordance with methods specified in each study protocol. In Study 302, infusion-related reactions occurred in 8 patients (3.1%) of the 255 patients treated with tislelizumab (Table 34). The most frequent PTs were pyrexia (4 patients, 1.6%) and rash (3 patients, 1.2%). All events of infusion-related reactions were either Grade 1 or 2 in severity and none led to dose modification. Two of the 8 patients who
L1139: experienced infusion-related reactions were treated with corticosteroids, and 7 of 8 events were resolved.
L1140: In the All Doses All Indications population, infusion-related reactions occurred in 83 patients (4.2%) of the 1972 patients treated with tisrelizumab. The most frequent PTs were pyrexia (40 patients, 2.0%), rash (5 patients, 0.3%), and nausea (5 patients, 0.3%). Events of infusion-related reactions were typically Grade 1 or 2 in severity; only 6 patients (0.3%) in the All Doses All Indications population had events of \ge Grade 3. A Grade 2 infusion-related hypersensitivity reaction occurred in 1 patient (0.1%) in the All Doses All Indications population; no other infusion-related hypersensitivity reactions were reported. Five patients (0.3%) and 21 patients (1.1%) experienced an infusion-related reaction leading to tisrelizumab discontinuation or dose modification, respectively. Twenty-six of the 83 patients (31.3%) who experienced infusion-related reactions were treated with corticosteroids, and 79 of 83 cases (95.2%) were resolved.
L1141: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1142: Immune-mediated adverse events were generally low grade and responsive to corticosteroids or hormone therapy as needed. Infusion-related reactions were infrequent, low grade, and occurred at a similar incidence in both arms.
L1143: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1144: FDA agrees with the summary results described in the Applicant's Table 32. As noted above, however, FDA considers that there was an event of autoimmune hepatitis leading to fatal hepatic failure; although the Applicant did not flag this event as an imAE flag, inclusion of this patient would not change the incidence of imAEs significantly (55 patients or 21.6%).
L1145: Similarly, FDA analysis demonstrates 10 patients (3.9%) who experienced a \ge Grade 3 imAE compared to the Applicant's calculation of 9 patients (3.5%) who experienced a \ge Grade 3 imAE. The following were the Grade \ge 3 imAEs:
L1146: - hepatitis (3 patients)
- pneumonitis (3 patients)
- myositis/rhabdomyolysis (2 patients)
- myocarditis (1 patient)
- hypothyroidism (1 patient)
L1147: All of these were Grade 3 events except for 1 of the events of hepatitis (Grade 5, as discussed above) and an event of Grade 4 hypothyroidism. FDA analysis agrees with the remaining data in Table 32 including the use of systemic steroids, hormone therapy, and drug discontinuations. Additionally, though not noted in the Applicant's Table 33, above, FDA found 1 patient who experienced immune-mediated pituitary dysfunction (Grade 2). That patient was treated with systemic steroids and was not discontinued from study therapy. This information is consistent with the Applicant's Table 19 in the Clinical Overview document submitted to the BLA.
L1148: The events and frequencies of imAEs in Study 302 are similar to those observed with other anti-PD1/L1 antibodies used as monotherapy. While most events are Grade 1-2, these events should be monitored closely in all patients.
L1149: FDA analysis of the ISS population (n = 1972) demonstrated similar conclusions as the Applicant. These findings are summarized in Section 5 of the USPI.
L1150: FDA agrees with the frequency of IRRs in Study 302 noted by the Applicant. Eight patients (3.1%) in the tislelizumab arm and 11 (4.6%) patients in the ICC arm experienced an IRR. In the tislelizumab arm, the individual AEs reported were pyrexia, rash, hypotension, and pruritus. All of these were Grade 1 events except for one Grade 2 event of pyrexia.
L1151: ## Treatment Emergent Adverse Events and Adverse Reactions
L1152: ## Data:
L1153: In Study 302, the overall safety profile of tislelizumab compared favorably with that of chemotherapy (Table 34). Almost all patients in both arms experienced an adverse event: 95.7% for the Tislelizumab Arm and 98.3% for the ICC Arm. The most patients in the Tislelizumab Arm had only low grade (Grades 1 or 2) adverse events; \ge Grade 3 adverse events occurred in 46.3% of patients in that arm. In contrast, the most patients in the ICC Arm had adverse events \ge Grade 3 (67.9%). Treatment-related adverse events across categories were consistently lower in the Tislelizumab Arm compared with the ICC Arm.
L1154: Fewer patients in the Tislelizumab Arm experienced adverse events leading to treatment discontinuation (19.2%) or adverse events leading to dose modification (22.7%) compared with patients in the ICC Arm (26.7% and 47.9%, respectively). Serious adverse events occurred at a similar incidence in both arms (41.2% versus 43.8%), although the exposure-adjusted serious adverse events were lower in the Tislelizumab Arm (12.5 per 100 person-months) than in the ICC Arm (28.9 per 100 person-months). Deaths due to any cause in Study 302 occurred in fewer patients who received tislelizumab than ICC (77.0% versus 83.2%, respectively) (Table 27). Deaths due to adverse events occurred in 14 patients in each arm (5.5% tislelizumab; 5.8% ICC).
L1155: In the All Doses All Indications population, a similar safety profile was observed compared with the Study 302 population suggesting consistency of the tislelizumab safety profile across multiple tumor types (Table 35)

L1162: ## **Adverse Events**
L1163: In Study 302, the most frequently reported adverse events (ie, \ge 5\% patient incidence) in the Tislelizumab Arm consisted of various preferred terms, including anemia, weight decreased, cough, pyrexia, decreased appetite, and constipation (Table 35). These preferred terms (PTs) were not typically associated with immune-mediated adverse events, and the incidences of these events were generally similar between the Tislelizumab Arm and the ICC Arm. Adverse events with a \ge 5\% higher incidence reported for the Tislelizumab Arm compared with the ICC Arm were cough, transaminase elevation, and hypothyroidism, consistent with an immune-mediated association.
L1164: The most frequently reported adverse events (\ge 15\% patient incidence) in the ICC Arm were predominantly related to hematologic, constitutional, and gastrointestinal events, and these events were also the types of adverse events reported with a higher incidence for ICC compared with tislelizumab. The events were generally consistent with the safety profile of paclitaxel, docetaxel, and irinotecan in the ICC Arm.
L1165: In exposure-adjusted analyses, tislelizumab was associated with a substantially lower rate of adverse events of any grade compared with ICC (171.8 per 100 person-months versus 442.3 per 100 person-months).
L1166: In the All Doses All Indications population, adverse events of any grade were consistent overall with those in Study 302 Tislelizumab Arm (Table 35).

L1182: ## \ge Grade 3 Adverse Events
L1183: In Study 302, the incidence of \ge Grade 3 adverse events was lower in the Tislelizumab Arm (46.3%) compared with the ICC Arm (67.9%) (Table 37). The most frequently reported \ge Grade 3 adverse events in the Tislelizumab Arm were dysphagia, anemia, and hyponatremia. These PTs were not typically associated with immune-mediated adverse events, and the incidence of these events in the Tislelizumab Arm were either similar or lower than the ICC Arm, except for dysphagia. All \ge Grade 3 dysphagia events in the Tislelizumab Arm were assessed by investigators as related to the tumor.
L1184: The most frequently reported \ge Grade 3 adverse events in the ICC Arm were predominantly hematologic events, and these \ge Grade 3 adverse events also occurred at higher incidence in the ICC Arm compared with the Tislelizumab Arm. A number of \ge Grade 3 hematologic adverse events were associated with dose modification and, to a lesser extent, study drug discontinuation. These findings were consistent with known adverse events of \ge Grade 3 of paclitaxel, docetaxel, and irinotecan in the ICC Arm.
L1185: In the All Doses All Indications population, \ge Grade 3 adverse events were consistent with those in the Study 302 Tislelizumab Arm, except for a higher dysphagia in Study 302 compared with the All Doses All Indications population (6.3% versus 1.4%). The higher rate of dysphagia in Study 302 is likely due to dysphagia related to the underlying ESCC and disease progression.

L1195: ## Treatment-Related Adverse Events
L1196: In Study 302, the incidence of treatment-related adverse events, by PT, for all categories including all grades, \ge Grade 3, serious, leading to treatment discontinuation or leading to dose modification, were consistent with the overall adverse event incidences (ie, those assessed regardless of relatedness). For all categories, a lower incidence of treatment-related adverse events was reported in the Tislelizumab Arm than in the ICC Arm.
L1197: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1198: The TEAEs with a higher incidence in the Tislelizumab Arm were consistent with the profile of immune-mediated adverse events for tislelizumab, such as hypothyroidism and transaminase elevation. The TEAEs with a higher incidence in the ICC Arm were consistent with the safety profiles of paclitaxel, docetaxel, and irinotecan. The adverse events \ge Grade 3 findings were consistent with the safety profile of paclitaxel, docetaxel, and irinotecan in the ICC Arm.
L1199: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1200: FDA agrees with the overall stated incidence of TEAEs and that patients in the tislelizumab arm had a lower incidence of Grade \ge 3 events compared to the patients in the ICC arm (46% vs. 68%). FDA also concurs with the description of Grade 5 TEAEs, events leading to permanent discontinuation and dose interruptions, SAEs, iMAEs, and IRRs with the exceptions already described throughout this section. As noted above, FDA did not consider Applicant's attribution ("treatment-related") when reviewing AEs. Also, based on FDA assessment, deaths due to adverse events occurred in 18 patients in the tislelizumab arm (see Table 29, above).
L1201: See FDA Table 38, below, for FDA-calculated TEAEs occurring in Study 302. The TEAEs, including laboratory findings, with the highest frequency (\ge 20\%) in the tislelizumab arm were: increased glucose, decreased hemoglobin, decreased lymphocytes, decreased sodium, decreased albumin, increased alkaline phosphatase, anemia, fatigue, increased AST, musculoskeletal pain, decreased weight, increased ALT, and cough.
L1202: Additional TEAEs that were significantly elevated in the ICC arm compared to the tislelizumab arm include alopecia (18% compared to 0%), peripheral neuropathy (13% compared to 3%), decreased neutrophil count (39% compared to 2.4%), and decreased white blood cell count (41% compared to 3.5%). These TEAEs are consistent with AEs from cytotoxic chemotherapy.
L1203: When comparing the tislelizumab arm with the 1,972 ISS population, the frequencies of AEs
L1204: generally appear similar with the exception of dysphagia (11 % compared to 3%) which is possibly a function of the specific disease studied in Study 302 (ESCC).
L1205: FDA analysis is summarized in Table 38, below.

L1210: To further explore other AEs of interest due to either the disease under study or the mechanism of action of the drug, FDA examined the frequency of fistula formation and ocular toxicities, below.
L1211: ## - Esophageal/upper GI Fistulas
L1212: Given the nature of esophageal cancers and the risk of fistulas seen in this patient population, including in the setting of therapy with anti-PD1(L1) antibodies, FDA examined the frequencies of esophageal/upper GI fistula formation in Study 302. In the tislelizumab arm, 12 events of esophageal fistula formation occurred in 11 (4.3%) patients (7 patients or 2.7% had SAEs), including a fatal event of esophagobronchial fistula. In the ICC arm, 16 events of
L1213: esophageal/gastric/tracheal fistulas occurred in 10 (4.2%) patients (8 patients or 3.3% had SAEs) and two of these events were fatal (one event of “acquired trachea-esophageal fistula” and one event of “esophageal fistula”). Based on these data, there does not appear to be a significant difference between the treatment arms. Similar incidence of fistulas have been reported in other ESCC studies (Opdivo USPI). See also “Serious Adverse Events” section, above.
L1214: ## • Ocular Toxicity
L1215: Rare but serious ocular toxicities have been reported with anti-PD1(L1) drugs. A total of 18 (7%) and 8 (3%) of patients experienced eye disorders in the tislelizumab and ICC arms, respectively. None of these events were Grade \ge 3. In the tislelizumab arm, In one patient, Grade 2 uveitis was considered to be an imAE on preliminary assessment and treated with topical steroids but this was not adjudicated ultimately by the Applicant as an imAE. The PTs in the tislelizumab arm were visual impairment, vision blurred, uveitis, retinopathy, retinal tear, retinal edema, retinal exudates, ocular hypertension, macular degeneration, lacrimation increased, dry eye, conjunctival hyperemia, chalazion, and cataract. All retinal toxicities occurred in 5 individual patients and were all Grade 1 events. Only 1 of these events was considered recovered/resolved. In the ICC arm, all 9 events (8 patients) were Grades 1-2 and the PTs were: visual acuity reduced, lacrimation increased, eyelid ptosis, blepharitis, vision blurred, visual impairment, and conjunctival pallor.
L1216: Although relatively rare, ocular toxicities are a known risk of anti-PD1(L1) drugs and patients must be monitored for any eye or vision complaints.
L1217: ## Laboratory Findings
L1218: ## Data:
L1219: ## Study 302:
L1220: **Hematology:** Overall, ICC was associated with a worsened hematology profile relative to tislelizumab. A greater number of shifts of \ge 2 NCI-CTCAE toxicity grades for decreased values of hemoglobin and white blood cell counts occurred in the ICC Arm compared with the Tislelizumab Arm. Patients in the ICC Arm experienced a reduction from baseline in mean hemoglobin over time; in contrast, there was an overall increase from baseline in the mean hemoglobin in the Tislelizumab Arm. Patients in the ICC Arm also experienced greater reduction from baseline in mean neutrophil counts over time. There was no clinically meaningful difference between the 2 arms in the means of the lowest postbaseline values for lymphocytes or platelets.
L1221: **Serum Chemistry:** The proportion of patients who reported serum chemistry values with deterioration by \ge 2 NCI-CTCAE toxicity grades was comparable between the arms. Few patients (&lt; 10% in each arm) reported serum chemistry values with shifts of \ge 2 NCI-CTCAE toxicity grades for most parameters, and differences observed between the 2 treatment arms were
L1222: not considered meaningful. No clinically meaningful changes from baseline were observed in the median values for serum chemistry analytes of interest (ALT, AST, bilirubin, CK, CK-MB, creatinine, potassium, and sodium) in either treatment arm (Table 39).

L1235: ## **Vital Signs**
L1236: ## **Data:**
L1237: There were no clinically meaningful changes from baseline in systolic or diastolic blood pressure, pulse rate, temperature, or body weight over time in each arm in Study 302. Analyses of vital signs, physical examination findings, and other observations related to safety were not integrated for Studies 302, 102, 001, 303, 208, 204, and 203. No additional analyses were conducted for this assessment. Any clinically relevant changes in vital signs, physical examination, or other safety-related observations associated with tislelizumab use and those that led to substantial interventions were reported as adverse events.
L1238: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1239: Tislelizumab did not appear to have clinically meaningful adverse effects on patient vital signs during any clinical study.

L1242: ## Electrocardiograms (ECGs)/ QT
L1243: ## Data:
L1244: The ECG values were calculated and summarized by different methods, including corrected QT interval by Fridericia's formula (QTcF), corrected QT interval by Bazett's formula (QTcB), and the unspecified method. For the patients who had \ge 1 postbaseline measurement, few patients had a postbaseline value of &gt; 480 msec in the analysis by QTcF method (1 patient in the Tislelizumab Arm) and by QTcB method (1 patient in the ICC Arm). In the analysis by the unspecified method, 3 patients in the Tislelizumab Arm and 3 patients in the ICC Arm had a postbaseline value of &gt; 480 msec. For the patients who had a baseline QTc measurement and \ge 1 postbaseline QTc measurement, few patients had QTc prolongation of &gt; 60 msec in the analysis by QTcF method (1 patient in the ICC Arm) and by QTcB method (1 patient in the ICC Arm). In the analysis by the unspecified method, 6 patients in the Tislelizumab Arm and 3 patients in the ICC Arm had QTc prolongation of &gt; 60 msec.
L1245: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1246: The total number of patients in each arm who had a postbaseline value of &gt; 480 msec (4 patients in the Tislelizumab Arm and 4 patients in the ICC Arm) or QTc prolongation of &gt; 60 msec (6 patients in the Tislelizumab Arm and 5 patients in the ICC Arm) irrespective of QTc measurement method was small, and the differences between the 2 arms were not considered clinically meaningful.
L1247: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1248: The FDA concurs with the Applicant's analysis.

L1256: ## Immunogenicity
L1257: ## Data:
L1258: Monitoring of ADA to tislelizumab and titer determination for confirmed positive ADA samples have been performed in all clinical studies included in this submission. NAbs were evaluated in the confirmed positive ADA samples.
L1259: In Study 302, 14.5% (32/221) of evaluable patients tested positive for treatment-emergent ADA. NAbs were detected in 0.5% (1/221) of patients. Exploratory analyses of clinical endpoints in Study 302 showed that the patients who were treatment-emergent ADA-positive had comparable OS to patients who were treatment-emergent ADA-negative, with an HR of 1.16 (95% CI: 0.77, 1.75). In addition, numerically higher ORR (18.8% versus 16.4%) and disease control rate (56.3% versus 51.3%) were noted in the treatment-emergent ADA-positive group than those of the ADA negative group, respectively. However, these minor numerical differences are not considered to be clinically relevant. Overall, comparable safety profiles (incidence rate of imAEs, AESIs, and AEs leading to dose modification or discontinuation) were observed, except patients who were treatment-emergent ADA-positive had a slightly lower rate of safety events of \ge Grade 3 AEs than ADA negative patients (62.5% versus 39.2%, respectively). These trends were not seen when larger sample size from additional studies were compared.
L1260: Summary of immunogenicity from seven clinical studies (001, 102, 203, 204, 208, 302, and 303) with total 1785 ADA evaluable patients indicated that 16.5% (295/1785) and 0.6% (11/1785) incidence of treatment-emergent ADA and NAbs was observed, respectively, across all dose regimens (0.5 to 10 mg/kg once every 2 weeks, 2.0 or 5.0 mg/kg once every 3 weeks, or 200 mg once every 3 weeks). Of the recommended dose of 200 mg once every 3 weeks across all the aforementioned studies, 16.3% (232/1424) patients had treatment-emergent ADA and NAbs were detected in 0.8% (11/1424) patients. Population PK analysis as described in Section 6.3.1 showed that, although treatment-emergent ADA status was a significant covariate, the presence of treatment-emergent ADA did not impact PK, efficacy, or safety to a clinically meaningful extent.
L1261: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1262: Based on the totality of available clinical data, ADA status did not have a clinically relevant impact on safety in patients with ESCC.
L1263: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1264: FDA (clinical) agrees and defers to the FDA assessment in Section 6.
