L1272: ## 8.2.8. Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability
L1273: ## Data:
L1274: Validated and most frequently used PROs instruments in oncology studies, esp. in PD-L1 studies, were used measure and to assess HRQoL. These included the EORTC QLQ-C30 to measure general cancer signs and symptoms, its esophageal cancer-specific module, the EORTC QLQ-OES18, and the EQ-5D-5L to measure general health, were used to further characterize treatment benefits of tislelizumab beyond radiographic measures.
L1275: The assessment of the HRQoL were based on of the comparison of the key PRO endpoints in esophageal cancer. The PRO key endpoints in this study included the global health status/QoL (GHS) and physical function as measured by the EORTC QLQ-C-30 and esophageal cancer main symptoms of pain, eating, dysphagia and reflux as measured by EORTC QLQ-OES18.
L1276: ## Compliance
L1277: The adjusted completion rates (the number of patients who completed the PRO questionnaires divided by the number of patients who were still in treatment at that visit) from baseline through Cycle 6 were &gt; 95% for each questionnaire and were similar in both treatment arms.
L1278: ## **Score Changes From Baseline**
L1279: Changes from baseline to Cycle 6 (mean change [standard deviation]) in HRQoL outcome scores favored the Tislelizumab Arm versus the ICC Arm. Specifically:
L1280: Eating and pain symptom scores decreased (indicating improvements from baseline) in the Tislelizumab Arm compared with increases (indicating worsening) in the ICC Arm (Tislelizumab: -2.5 [15.92] and -1.1 [14.29] for eating and pain, respectively; ICC: 6.7 [22.94] and 1.9 [15.36], respectively)
L1281: Although both dysphagia and reflux symptom scores slightly worsened in both arms from baseline to Cycle 6, patients in the Tislelizumab Arm experienced less worsening compared with the ICC Arm (Tislelizumab: 1.6 [28.50] and 0.2 [14.22] for dysphagia and reflux, respectively; ICC: 2.7 [37.15] and 1.4 [22.28], respectively)
L1282: A smaller reduction in physical functioning was observed in the Tislelizumab Arm compared with the ICC Arm (Tislelizumab: -1.9 [10.33]; ICC: -5.7 [12.05]) (Figure 19).
L1283: Global health status (GHS) improved from baseline in the Tislelizumab Arm but worsened in the ICC Arm (Tislelizumab: 1.9 [16.45]; ICC: -6.3 [14.82]).
L1284: Lastly, overall symptoms indicated by the index score of the QLQ-EOS18, worsened in the ICC Arm whereas is somewhat stabilized in the Tislelizumab Arm (Tislelizumab: -0.6 [8.63]; ICC: 3.0 [12.05]) (Figure 20).
L1285: The health state, measured via EQ-5D-5L VAS scores also showed better outcomes for the tislelizumab treat arm (Tislelizumab: 0.6 [14.81]; ICC: 5.9 [16.34]).
L1286: **Figure 19: BeiGene - Summary of EORTC QLQ-C30 Scores by Visit - Physical Functioning (Study 302) (ITT Analysis Set)**
L1287: Source: ADQS. Data cutoff: 01DEC2020. Data extraction: 15JAN2021.
L1288: Datapoints represent means and 95% CI.
Increases in scores for physical functioning are improvements for C30.
L1289: **Figure 20: BeiGene - Summary of EORTC QLQ-OES18 Scores by Visit - Index Score (Study 302) (ITT Analysis Set)**
L1290: Source: ADQS. Data cutoff: 01DEC2020. Data extraction: 15JAN2021.
L1291: Datapoints represent means and 95% CI.
Increases in scores for index score are deteriorations for OES18.
L1292: Median time to clinically meaningful worsening in GHS and physical functioning per EORTC QLQC30 and dysphagia, eating, reflux, and pain per EORTC QLQOES18 was not reached in either arm using the \ge 10 point threshold. However, in the ITT population, the TTD analysis showed patients treated with tislelizumab were at a lower risk for clinically meaningful worsening of key symptoms of dysphagia (stratified HR of 0.76 [95% CI: 0.53, 1.07], P = 0.0562), reflux (0.50 [95% CI: 0.32, 0.80], P = 0.0014) as well as worsening of physical function (0.67 [95% CI: 0.45, 1.00], P = 0.0239) through the course of treatment. Meanwhile, the PD-L1 vCPS \ge 10\% patients treated with tislelizumab showed to be at lower risk for pain (0.52 [95% CI: 0.24, 1.13], P = 0.0460).
L1293: Tislelizumab Arm experienced lower risk of clinically meaningful worsening compared to the ICC Arm in physical functioning (stratified HR: 0.67 [95% CI: 0.45 to 1.00]), measured via EORTC QLQ-C30, and symptoms of reflux (stratified HR: 0.50 [95% CI: 0.32 to 0.80]), dysphagia (stratified HR: 0.76 [95% CI: 0.53 to 1.07]) and pain (stratified HR: 0.89 [95% CI: 0.59 to 1.35]), pre-measured via EORTC QLQES18. No clinically meaningful differences in GHS between the Tislelizumab Arm compared to the ICC Arm were observed (stratified HR: 0.96 [95% CI: 0.65 to 1.41]).
L1294: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1295: These findings show that tislelizumab treatment improved symptoms of pain and eating, decreased the risk of worsening of EC symptoms, particularly reflux, dysphagia, physical functioning, and pain in PDL1 sub population, compared with ICC.
L1296: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1297: FDA does not agree with the applicant's position that tislelizumab treatment improved symptoms of pain and eating or decreased the risk of worsening ESCC symptoms. No conclusions can or should be drawn regarding HRQoL given that no alpha was allocated to these endpoints and results are to be considered exploratory only. All p-values presented by the applicant with respect to HRQoL analyses are considered nominal only. Furthermore, FDA does not agree with the proposed 10-point threshold for clinically meaningful worsening. See FDA's comments in Section 8.1.2 regarding the HRQoL secondary endpoints for more details.
L1298: ## 8.2.9. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
L1299: ## **Data:**
L1300: ## **Age**
L1301: The overall incidence of adverse events was similar among the 3 age subgroups (&lt; 65 years, ≥ 65 to &lt; 75 years, and ≥ 75 years). No clear pattern of higher incidence of adverse events with increasing age was observed.
L1302: Numerically higher incidence of constipation, nausea, diarrhea, pyrexia, fatigue, and pruritus was observed for the ≥ 75 years of age subgroup compared with the other two subgroups; these results should be interpreted with caution due to the small sample size of the ≥ 75 years of age subgroup.
L1303: Numerically higher incidence of diarrhea, dysphagia, and pyrexia was observed in the ≥ 65 to &lt; 75 years of age subgroup compared with the &lt; 65 years of age subgroup.
L1304: Numerically higher incidence of aspartate aminotransferase increased, alanine aminotransferase increased, decreased appetite was observed in the &lt; 65 years of age subgroup compared with the other two subgroups.
L1305: The overall incidence of immune-mediated adverse events and the incidence of immune-mediated adverse events under each event category were similar between the subgroups of &lt; 65 years and ≥ 65 to &lt; 75 years, with the exception of immune-mediated pneumonitis (5.7% versus 10.6%, respectively). No immune-mediated adverse events were reported in patients ≥ 75 years of age in the Study 302 Tislelizumab Arm.
L1306: ## Gender
L1307: More male patients were enrolled than female patients (84.7% versus 15.3%; Table 17); this is likely related to the higher incidence of ESCC in male than in female population; the results of adverse events by gender should be interpreted with caution due to the small sample size of the female subgroup.
L1308: The overall incidence of adverse events was similar between the male and female subgroups. The incidence of adverse events across most of the PTs was similar between the 2 gender subgroups.
L1309: Numerically higher incidence of diarrhea, nausea, back pain, and fatigue was observed in the female subgroup compared with the male subgroup.
L1310: Numerically higher incidence of pneumonia, weight decreased, asthenia, and anemia was observed in the male subgroup compared with the female subgroup.
L1311: The overall incidence of immune-mediated adverse events was higher in the female subgroup than in the male subgroup (28.2% versus 19.9%). The immune-mediated adverse event of pneumonitis was higher in the female subgroup compared with the male subgroup (12.8% versus 6.0%). The incidence of the other immune-mediated adverse events was similar between the 2 subgroups.
L1312: ## Race
L1313: More patients in Study 302 were Asian compared with white (78.8% versus 20.4%). The results of adverse events by race should be interpreted with caution due to the small sample size of the White subgroup. The overall incidence of adverse events was similar between the Asian and White subgroups.
L1314: Numerically higher incidence of laboratory-related adverse events was reported in the Asian subgroup than in the White subgroup; these include hyponatremia, hypoalbuminemia, alanine aminotransferase increased, aspartate aminotransferase increased, gamma-glutamyltransferase increased, hypoproteinemia, and blood alkaline phosphatase increased. A similar trend was observed in patients treated with chemotherapy (ICC Arm) in the Asian subgroup.
L1315: The two subgroups by race, Asian and white, are almost identical to the two subgroups by region, Asia and Europe/North America. A laboratory evaluation of shifts in NCI-CTCAE grade from baseline to the worst postbaseline grade for hematology parameters and serum chemistry parameters showed similar shifts between the Asia subgroup and the Europe/North America subgroup, which indicated no clinically meaningful regional differences. Hence, there was likely no differences between the Asian and White subgroups based on the shifts in laboratory values.
L1316: The higher incidence in laboratory-related adverse events may reflect the differences in reporting of adverse events between regions.
L1317: For non-laboratory-related adverse events, numerically higher incidence of weight decrease, pneumonia, and pruritus was observed in the Asian subgroup compared with the White subgroup. Numerically higher incidence of asthenia, diarrhea, fatigue, dysphagia, decreased appetite, nausea, pyrexia, and back pain was observed in the White subgroup compared with the Asian subgroup. Taking considerations noted above, the incidences of the most adverse events were similar between the Asian subgroup and the White subgroup.
L1318: The overall incidence of immune-mediated adverse events was higher in the Asian subgroup than in the White subgroup (21.9% versus 15.4%). The immune-mediated adverse event of pneumonitis was higher in the Asian subgroup compared with the White subgroup (8.0% versus 1.9%). The incidences of the other immune-mediated adverse events were similar between the 2 subgroups.
L1319: ## Weight
L1320: The overall incidence of adverse events was similar between the 2 baseline weight subgroups (“&lt; Median” and “\ge Median”, where the calculation of median baseline weight of 59.00 kg is based on the Study 302 Safety Analysis Set). The incidence of adverse events across most of the PTs were generally similar between the 2 weight subgroups.
L1321: Numerically higher incidence of pruritus, pyrexia, and pneumonia was observed in the “&lt; Median” subgroup compared with the “\ge Median” subgroup.
L1322: Numerically higher incidence of asthenia, aspartate aminotransferase increased, alanine aminotransferase increased, and gamma-glutamyl transferase increased was observed in the “\ge Median” subgroup compared with the “&lt; Median” subgroup.
L1323: The overall incidence of immune-mediated adverse events was similar between the “&lt; Median” subgroup and the “\ge Median” subgroup (19.4% versus 23.0%). The incidence of immune-mediated adverse events was similar between the 2 subgroups.
L1324: ## Region:
L1325: More patient in Study 302 were enrolled in Asia compared with Europe/North America (78.8% versus 21.2%). The results of adverse events by region should be interpreted with caution due to the small sample size of the Europe/North America subgroup.
L1326: The overall incidence of adverse events was similar between the Asia and Europe/North America subgroups. The two subgroups by region, Asia and Europe/North America, were almost identical to the two subgroups by race, Asian and white. The findings for adverse events by region were similar with that by race (Section 8.2.9).
L1327: The overall incidence of immune-mediated adverse events was higher in the Asia subgroup than in the Europe/North America subgroup (21.9% versus 18.5%). The immune-mediated adverse event of pneumonitis was higher in the Asia subgroup compared with the Europe/North America
L1328: subgroup (8.0% versus 3.7%). The incidence of the other immune-mediated adverse events was similar between the 2 subgroups.
L1329: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1330: The safety profile of tislelizumab is similar across demographic subgroups (age, gender, race, weight and region).
L1331: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1332: FDA agrees with the Applicant's analysis. However, data based on demographic subgroups should be interpreted with caution as these analyses are exploratory in nature. Additionally, 21% of patients in the study were enrolled outside of Asia, which further reduces strength of conclusions based on demographic groups.

L1348: ## Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
L1349: ## **Data:**
L1350: A literature-based assessment of the potential effects of tislelizumab on embryofetal development via weight-of-evidence approach demonstrated that blocking the PD-1/PD-L1 pathway can result in fetal loss in animal models. The potential risks of administering tislelizumab during pregnancy include increased rates of abortion or stillbirth. Based on
L1351: tislelizumab's mechanism of action, fetal exposure may increase the risk of developing immune-mediated disorders or altering the normal immune response. Therefore, tislelizumab is not recommended during pregnancy unless the clinical benefit outweighs the potential risk.
L1352: There are no data on the presence of tislelizumab in either animal or human milk or on the effect of tislelizumab on the breastfed child or on milk production. Because of the potential for serious adverse reactions in breastfed children, advise women not to breastfeed during treatment and for 4 months after the final dose.
L1353: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1354: Tislelizumab is not recommended during pregnancy unless the clinical benefit outweighs the potential risk.
L1355: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1356: FDA acknowledges that tislelizumab has not been studied in pregnant women and is therefore no information to report for use of tislelizumab in pregnancy or lactation.
L1357: ## **Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth**
L1358: ## **Data:**
L1359: No information is provided in the current submission.
L1360: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1361: On November 10, 2020, tislelizumab received agreement for an amended initial Pediatric Study Plan (b)(4) Refer to section 10.
L1362: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1363: Not applicable.
L1364: ## **Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound and Ability to Drive**
L1365: ## **Data:**
L1366: There is no specific antidote for tislelizumab. Patients who experience an overdose should be closely monitored and provided with appropriate supportive treatment.
L1367: There is no nonclinical or clinical evidence that tislelizumab has potential for drug abuse, and specific clinical studies evaluating abuse potential have not been conducted.
L1368: There is no evidence of withdrawal or rebound phenomena associated with discontinuation of tislelizumab in any clinical study.
L1369: No specific studies have been conducted to evaluate the influence of tislelizumab treatment on the ability to drive or operate heavy machinery or impairment of mental ability.
L1370: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1371: Neither the effects of overdose of tislelizumab nor an antidote to overdose are known. The potential for tislelizumab drug abuse and dependence is unknown.
L1372: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1373: Based on its pharmacological properties, there are no concerns regarding the potential for abuse, withdrawal, or rebound with tislelizumab.
L1374: ## 8.2.12. Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1375: ## Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L1376: ## **Data:**
L1377: The first marketing authorization (MA) for tislelizumab was granted in China on 27 December 2019, followed by subsequent MAs in April 2020, January 2021, and two indications authorized in June 2021. As of 25 December 2020, a cumulative total of [REDACTED] (b) (4) vials of tislelizumab have been supplied to the market in China (equivalent to [REDACTED] (b) (4) infusions based on the recommended dose of 200 mg per infusion). This represents an exposure of approximately [REDACTED] (b) (4) patient-years [REDACTED] (b) (4) patient-months) based on the treatment cycle of every 3 weeks.
L1378: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1379: The data available to BeiGene have been analyzed to provide a critical analysis of the benefit-risk profile of tislelizumab in the context of new information on both benefits and risks that emerged during the relevant reporting period (26 December 2019 to 25 December 2020). The benefits and risks (safety experience) of treatment with tislelizumab have been assessed based on both clinical studies and post-marketing experience and overall indicate a positive benefit-risk profile for tislelizumab. BeiGene considers that the benefit-risk profile for tislelizumab in the currently approved indications continues to remain favorable.
L1380: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1381: FDA reviewed the Periodic Benefit-Risk Evaluation Report discussed above and acknowledges the Applicant's position.

L1389: ## 8.2.13. Integrated Assessment of Safety
L1390: ## **Data:**
L1391: This comprehensive review, based primarily on the large, well-controlled, pivotal Phase 3 Study 302 and supported by collective data from 1972 tislelizumab-treated patients, establishes that tislelizumab provides a favorable safety profile compared to chemotherapy in patients with advanced or metastatic ESCC after prior systemic therapy. Study 302 allows for a clear comparison of tislelizumab safety against the safety of single-agent chemotherapy, the standard of care at the time of study initiation. Patients in Study 302 are representative of the general ESCC population in terms of age, sex, race, geographic region, disease status, and overall health,
L1392: and the study's sample size is adequate to allow for evaluation of safety in important ESCC subgroups. Taken together, the data package is sufficient to characterize the safety of tislelizumab and to guide the management of risks associated with tislelizumab as a treatment for ESCC.
L1393: The safety profiles of the treatment arms in Study 302 were differentiated by their distinct mechanisms of action. As with other PD-1 CPIs (Kojima et al 2020; Kato et al 2019), tislelizumab safety was characterized by immune-mediated adverse events, such as hypothyroidism, pneumonitis, and skin adverse reactions. The events were generally low grade and responsive to corticosteroids or hormone therapy as needed. Tislelizumab had notably lower rates of adverse events than ICC (mainly hematologic, gastrointestinal, and neurologic toxicities, constitutional symptoms, and alopecia). As evidenced by the higher rates of treatment discontinuation or delay/interruption due to adverse events with ICC compared with tislelizumab, these toxicities can be dose-limiting for ICC. The overall tislelizumab safety profile appeared consistent across key ESCC patient demographics. No new signals for tislelizumab were identified when safety findings were evaluated in the larger pooled All Doses All Indications population.
L1394: The favorable safety results in this submission provide an ample dataset to adequately support the use of tislelizumab monotherapy in the treatment of patients with unresectable recurrent locally advanced or metastatic ESCC after prior systemic therapy.
L1395: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1396: The totality of the safety package sufficiently support the use of tislelizumab 200 mg intravenously once every 3 weeks as a single agent to treat patients with unresectable recurrent locally advanced or metastatic ESCC after prior systemic therapy.
L1397: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1398: FDA agrees that Study 302 adequately characterized the safety of tislelizumab for the second line treatment of patients with ESCC. In general, the adverse reactions observed with tislelizumab were consistent with the mechanism of action and toxicity observed in prior clinical studies with tislelizumab and the pharmacological class. The primary risks related to tislelizumab are immune related reactions. These serious risks are adequately addressed in the Warnings and Precautions and Dosage Modifications sections of tislelizumab product labeling. The major safety risks of tislelizumab are toxicities that oncologists frequently manage and the toxicity profile of tislelizumab is considered acceptable when considering the improved survival observed in a patient population with a serious and life-threatening condition.
L1399: ## SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L1400: ## 8.3. Statistical Issues
L1401: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1402: No major statistical issues were identified during review of the data and results submitted in this application. However, FDA notes that there are some uncertainties in evaluating the efficacy results for PDL1 subgroups, including the key secondary endpoint of OS in patients with PD-L1 vCPS \ge 10%, due to missing PDL1 status of 19% of the patients in the ITT population. FDA further notes that only ~10% of the patients in the ITT population had vCPS &lt;1% compared to ~71% of the patients with vCPS \ge1%.
L1403: ## 8.4. Conclusions and Recommendations
L1404: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1405: Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness (SEE) was established with one adequate and well-controlled clinical investigation with highly persuasive results that is considered to be the scientific equivalent of two clinical investigations.
L1406: Based on the totality of evidence, the product quality, clinical pharmacology, clinical, and statistical review teams determined that the evidence submitted provides substantial evidence of the effectiveness of tislelizumab for the treatment of patients with unresectable or metastatic ESCC after prior systemic therapy that does not include an anti PD-(L)1 inhibitor and recommends approval. The recommended dosage regimen for this indication is 200 mg IV once every 3 weeks.
L1407: The data presented in this BLA demonstrating a modest but statistically significant improvement in OS in patients with ESCC was derived from RATIONALE-302, a multicenter, randomized, open-label, active-controlled trial. Patients were randomized to receive tislelizumab or investigator's choice of cytotoxic chemotherapy (paclitaxel, docetaxel, or irinotecan). The median OS was 8.6 months [95% CI: 7.5, 10.4] in the tislelizumab arm compared to 6.3 months [95% CI: 5.3, 7.0] in the ICC arm with a stratified hazard ratio of 0.70 [95% CI: 0.57, 0.85, p = 0.0001]. Tislelizumab was generally well tolerated and fewer adverse events were reported than in patients receiving chemotherapy. Immune-related adverse events, characteristic of anti-PD1(L1) drugs, including hypothyroidism, pneumonitis, and rash, occurred in approximately 20% of patients receiving tislelizumab. The adverse reaction profile observed in patients receiving tislelizumab in RATIONALE-302 was consistent with the known tislelizumab profile in different studies across indications and the pharmacologic class. These adverse reactions were generally manageable with dose delays and supportive care in most patients, although at least one fatal event was reported.
L1408: A review issue considered during the review of this application is the applicability to the U.S. population. Of note, randomization was stratified by location: Asia [excluding Japan] versus Japan versus United States [US]/European Union [EU]). Only 21% of patients in Study 302 were
L1409: from non-Asian countries and only 2 patients were from the U.S. (both randomized to the tislelizumab arm). Although worldwide ESCC is the dominant histological subtype (~90%) of esophageal cancer (EC), in the US, only approximately 1/3 of patients have ESCC and the majority of patients have esophageal adenocarcinomas. Although only exploratory in nature, forest plot of demographic subgroups of the ITT efficacy population do not indicate differential activity based on race/ethnicity (of those explored) or for patients in Europe/North America. Importantly, based on the SEER database analysis, there are no differences in outcomes for patients with ESCC based on race or ethnicity (Then EO, 2020). Although minorities have not been adequately represented in the study, based on the epidemiological data, tislelizumab's mechanism of action, known safety profile of the drug class, and the clinical pharmacology conclusions, the review team agrees that the study results are applicable to the US population. Based on FDA Clinical Pharmacology analyses, no clinically meaningful difference in PK of tislelizumab were found for in patients based on race (Asian, White, and Black); a flat relationship between exposure and efficacy and safety was observed.
L1410: Another review issue considered was whether labeling would inform of the results of the key secondary endpoint of OS in patients with PD-L1 expression \ge 10\% and whether the indication should be restricted based on tumor PD-L1 expression subgroups. The primary endpoint was OS in the ITT population but the key secondary endpoint was OS in the vCPS \ge 10\% population. A survival benefit from tislelizumab was observed in both populations with a HR for OS 0.70 in the ITT population and 0.53 in the vCPS \ge 10\% population. However, approximately 19% of patients in Study 302 had missing vCPS information and the Applicant did not submit data supporting a 10% vCPS cutoff versus other cutoffs in patients with ESCC with disease progression after first-line treatment. In addition, the subset of patients with PD-L1 expression &lt; 1\% was too small to derive conclusions. Thus, the indication recommended for approval is based on the ITT population.

L1457: ## 17. Division Director (Clinical)
L1458: I agree with the overall approval recommendations for tislelizumab-jsgr (tislelizumab) from the review teams. Study RATIONALE-302 demonstrated an improvement in OS for tislelizumab when compared to investigators' choice of chemotherapy. RATIONALE-302 was a multi-regional trial; however, most patients were enrolled in Asia (consistent with the lower incidence rate of ESCC in the US and Europe). The study enrolled a limited number of patients in Asia outside of China and close to 20% of patients in Europe or North America. The point estimate for the treatment effect in Europe and North America was favorable (0.55) and the PK of tislelizumab across races (Asian, White patients, and Black patients – with data collected beyond RATIONALE-302) appeared comparable.
L1459: Because FDA inspectors were not able to travel to China due to COVID-19 restrictions in China, action on this application was delayed (i.e., past the PDUFA deadline). FDA carefully considered the potential impact of the delayed action. Notably, this application does not address an unmet medical need as there are other treatment options in the same therapeutic class available to patients with unresectable advanced, recurrent or metastatic esophageal squamous cell carcinoma (ESCC) after prior fluoropyrimidine- and platinum-based chemotherapy and also for patients with unresectable advanced or metastatic esophageal squamous cell carcinoma as first-line treatment in combination with chemotherapy (See Section 2.2).

L1521: ## 19.4.1. Bioanalytical
L1522: ## **FDA Assessment:**
L1523: ## **Summary of Bioanalytical Method Validation and Performance**
L1524: In Study GBG-A317-001 and BGB-A317-302, serum tislelizumab concentrations were measured using a validated antigen-binding enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay (ELISA) method. The method performance is summarized in Table 44 and Table 45.
L1525: **Table 44. Summary Method Performance of a Bioanalytical Method Validated by
to Measure Tislelizumab (BGB-A317) in Human Serum**
L1526: Bioanalytical method validation report name, amendments, and hyperlinks	Validation of an ELISA Method for the Measurement of BGB-A317 in Human Serum (Report VAL136)
Method description	This assay method employs the quantitative indirect enzyme immunoassay technique. The tislelizumab antigen, human PD-1 (PDCD1) protein, is coated on a 96-well immunoplate and then blocked using a nonspecific protein-blocking buffer. Samples for calibration and quality control and clinical study samples are pipetted into the wells. Tislelizumab is bound to the immobilized human PD-1 (PDCD1) in the immunoplate. After washing away any unbound substrate, a horseradish peroxidase (HRP)-conjugated goat anti-human IgG (fragment crystallizable [Fc] region specific) HRP secondary antibody is added to the wells. Then after a second washing, HRP conjugate bound complexes are quantified by catalysis of color development using the chromogenic substrate, tetramethylbenzidine, with a spectrophotometer.
Materials used for calibration curve & concentration	BGB-A317 in human serum: 78.125, 156.250, 312.500, 625, 1250, 2500, 5000, 10,000, 20,000, 40,000, and 80,000 ng/mL
Validated assay range	400 to 40,000 ng/mL
Material used for QCs & concentration	400, 1200, 8000, 32,000, and 40,000 ng/mL

L1530: **Table 45. Summary Method Performance of a Bioanalytical Method Validated by (b) (4) to Measure Tislelizumab (BGB-A317) in Human Serum**
L1531: Bioanalytical method validation report name, amendments, and hyperlinks	Validation of a Method for the determination of BGB-A317 in Human Serum using Enzyme-Linked Immunosorbent Assay (ELISA) (Report 8354-363)
Method description	This assay method employs the quantitative indirect enzyme immunoassay technique. The tislelizumab antigen, human PD-1 (PDCD1) protein, is coated on a 96-well immunoplate and then blocked using a nonspecific protein-blocking buffer. Samples for calibration and quality control, and clinical study samples are pipetted into the wells. Tislelizumab is bound to the immobilized human PD-1 (PDCD1) in the immunoplate. After washing away any unbound substrate, a horseradish peroxidase (HRP)-conjugated goat antihuman IgG (fragment crystallizable [Fc] region specific) secondary antibody is added to the wells. Then after a second washing, HRP conjugate bound complexes are quantified by catalysis of color development using the chromogenic substrate, tetramethylbenzidine, with a spectrophotometer.
Materials used for calibration curve & concentration	BGB-A317 in human serum: 100, 200, 400, 625, 1250, 2500, 5000, 10,000, 20,000, 40,000, and 80,000 ng/mL
Validated assay range	400 to 40,000 ng/mL
Material used for QCs & concentration	400, 1200, 8000, 32,000, and 40,000 ng/mL
Minimum required dilutions (MRDs)	1:200
Source & lot of reagents (LBA)	PD-1/His: Supplier: BeiGene (Beijing) Co., Ltd, Lot No.: 20161108; Goat anti-Human IgG (Fc specific) HRP: Supplier: (b) (4) Lot No.: 023M4782
Regression model & weighting	5 PL (Marquardt); 1/Y^2
Validation parameters	Method validation summary	Source location

L1535: ## 19.4.2. Population PK Analysis
L1536: Population PK Summary Table
L1537: General Information
Objectives of PPK Analysis	
Characterize the PK profile of tislelizumab in patients with cancer.
Evaluate the effect of patient demographics, pathophysiologic factors, immunogenicity, and organ function etc. on tislelizumab PK
Evaluate impact of body weight distribution on flat dosing and body weight-based dosing regimens using population PK simulation approach.
Predict individual tislelizumab exposure for E-R analysis

Study Included	
Phase I:
BGB-A317-001, BGB-A317-102
Phase II:
BGB-A317-203, BGB-A317-204, BGB-A317-205, BGB-A317-206, BGB-A317-208, BGB-A317-209
Phase III:
BGB-A317-302, BGB-A317-303, BGB-A317-304, and BGB-A317-307.

L1565: ## **FDA's Assessment:**
L1566: Applicant's population PK analysis was assessed during the review. The population PK model was generally acceptable due to good agreement between model predictions and observations. In the population PK model, race was not identified as significant covariate. Empirical Bayes estimates of tislelizumab clearance among Asian, White, and Black or African American races were shown in Figure 24. White and Black or African American subjects showed slightly higher clearance than Asian subjects, which were mainly due to the relatively high bodyweight in these subjects comparing with Asian subjects. Tislelizumab exposure was expected to be similar between different races. Renal and hepatic functions were not identified as significant covariates either. Patients with renal impairment (mild &amp; moderate) and hepatic impairment (mild &amp; moderate) are expected to have similar tislelizumab exposure as patients with normal renal and hepatic function. (Figure 25)
L1567: **Figure 24. Empirical Bayes estimates of clearance in different races.**
L1568: Source: Reviewer's analysis.
L1569: **Figure 25. Empirical Bayes estimates of clearance in subjects with different renal and hepatic functions.**
L1570: Source: Reviewer's analysis.

The ER relationship of OS in the ITT population was explored by Kaplan-Meier curves stratified by	Acceptable
L1575: exposure (C_{\text{avg,dose1}}) quartiles. A longer OS was observed in the highest tislelizumab exposure quartile compared to those patients in the lower three quartiles. In Cox proportional hazard model, ALB, tumor growth rate (TGR) and the highest exposure quartile (Q4) were identified as significant predictor relative to the lowest exposure quartile (Q1). However, the ER relationship is likely inconclusive due to the imbalance in the baseline factors. (Figure 28) The exposure-response relationship of the ORR was explored by the probability of response plots. Although the plots showed that a slightly positive trend between tislelizumab exposure and the ORR, logistic regression analysis indicated that neither tislelizumab Exposure nor race is significant predictor in ER analysis.

L1593: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1594: Applicant's ER efficacy analysis was verified by the reviewer. Relatively longer overall survival time and higher ORR was observed in patients with highest quartile tislelizumab exposure. However, the effect of tislelizumab exposure on survival and ORR may be confounded by an imbalance in baseline factors of patients in different quartiles of tislelizumab exposure. Higher baseline tumor sizes were observed in patients with lower exposure (C_{avg,dose1}) of tislelizumab, which may affect the ER relationship (Figure 23). The race effect on OS and ORR was shown in Figure 30. No clear difference was observed between White and Asian patients. The efficacy in Black patients was not evaluated due to limited number patients in the analysis. The ADA effect on OS and ORR was shown in Figure 31 and no clear difference was observed between patients with positive and negative ADA status.
L1595: **Figure 30. Comparison of ORR and OS in White and Asian patients in Study BGB-A317-302.**
L1596: Source: Reviewer's analysis.
L1597: **Figure 31. Comparison of ORR and OS in patients with negative and positive ADA status in Study BGB-A317-302.**
L1598: Source: Reviewer's analysis

L1622: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1623: Applicant's analysis was checked by the reviewer. Safety data from Study BGB-A317-001, BGB-A317-102, BGB-A317-203, BGB-A317-204, BGB-A317-302 were pooled for further ER analysis. Among patients receiving 5 mg/kg or 200 mg Q3W tislelizumab treatment, no significant differences were observed between Asian and White patients. (Figure 35) Among patients receiving 5 mg/kg or 200 mg Q3W, no clear difference on AE rates were observed between ADA positive and ADA negative patients (Figure 38). The selected AEs in Black or African American patients was not evaluated due to limited number patients in the analysis. The probability of safety endpoints in different dose group in subjects with ESCC were shown in Figure 36. Higher rates of IMAE and AESI were observed in patients receiving 5 mg/kg tislelizumab than 200 mg which appears consistent to the slightly positive ER trends observed in Figure 37, while none of them were significant in ESCC patients.

L1647: Figure 1 is a dose-response curve showing the binding of Tisrelizumab (Ab conc. ng/ml, logarithmic scale) to PD-1 expressed on cells from different species, measured by OD450 (Y-axis, linear scale). The curves show increasing binding saturation with increasing antibody concentration.
Red circles (human PD-1): OD450 reaches approximately 6.5 at 10^2 ng/ml.
Blue squares (monkey PD-1): OD450 reaches approximately 7.0 at 10^2 ng/ml.
Black triangles (mouse PD-1): OD450 remains near 0 across the tested concentration range (10^{-1} to 10^4 ng/ml).
L1648: Figure 2 consists of two FACS dose-response curves showing Mean Fluorescence Intensity (MFI) versus Ab concentrations (\mug/ml, logarithmic scale). The Y-axis (MFI) ranges from 0 to 400 (top panel) and 0 to 250 (bottom panel).
The legend identifies four conditions:
BGB-A317 114-11 (Open circle)
BGB-A317 102-07 (Solid circle)
hulgG (Open diamond)
Placebo 114-13 (Solid diamond)
Top Panel (PD-L1 Binding): BGB-A317 114-11, BGB-A317 102-07, and hulgG show high MFI (around 300-350) across the concentration range. Placebo 114-13 shows a dose-dependent decrease in MFI, starting around 300 at 10^{-4} \mug/ml and dropping sharply below 100 MFI by 10^0 \mug/ml.
Bottom Panel (PD-L2 Binding): BGB-A317 114-11, BGB-A317 102-07, and hulgG show high MFI (around 200-250) across the concentration range. Placebo 114-13 shows a dose-dependent decrease in MFI, starting around 200 at 10^{-4} \mug/ml and dropping sharply below 50 MFI by 10^0 \mug/ml.
L1649: Figure 3 is a dose-response curve showing IL-2 secretion (pg/ml) versus antibody concentrations (µg/ml) on a logarithmic scale. Four treatments are compared: BGB-A317/E15B103-01 (red circles), Nivolumab (yellow diamonds), Pembrolizumab (green circles), and HlgG (purple triangles). BGB-A317/E15B103-01 shows the highest IL-2 secretion, reaching approximately 450 pg/ml at 1 µg/ml. Nivolumab and Pembrolizumab show intermediate levels, reaching approximately 350 pg/ml at 1 µg/ml. HlgG shows minimal IL-2 secretion, remaining below 50 pg/ml across the tested concentrations.

L1654: Figure 8 is a scatter plot showing Key safety event rates (Rate, 0% to 100%) versus Safety Event (G3AE, IMAE, AEDISC, IRR, AEDose_Group, AESI) for two dose groups: 200 mg Q3W (n=749) and 5 mg/kg Q3W (n=354). The 200 mg Q3W group generally shows higher rates for most events, particularly G3AE (around 45%) and AESI (around 30%). The 5 mg/kg Q3W group shows lower rates, generally below 25% for most events, except for G3AE (around 55%) and AESI (around 35%). Error bars are included for all data points.

L1676: Figure 30 displays two plots comparing outcomes in White and Asian patients from Study BGB-A317-302.
The left plot, titled "ORR in different RACE group," is a forest plot showing the Objective Response Rate (ORR) for White and Asian patients. The ORR for White patients is approximately 35% (95% CI: 20% to 50%). The ORR for Asian patients is approximately 25% (95% CI: 10% to 40%).
The right plot, titled "Overall Survival," is a Kaplan-Meier curve showing Overall Survival probability versus Time (Month). The survival curves for White and Asian patients are very close, indicating similar overall survival outcomes. The median survival time is approximately 20 months for both groups.
L1677: Figure 31 displays two plots comparing outcomes in patients with negative and positive ADA status from Study BGB-A317-302.
The left plot, titled "ORR in ADA positive vs negative," is a forest plot showing the Objective Response Rate (ORR) for patients with Negative and Positive ADA status. The ORR for patients with Negative ADA status is approximately 30% (95% CI: 15% to 45%). The ORR for patients with Positive ADA status is approximately 40% (95% CI: 25% to 55%).
The right plot, titled "Survival probability," is a Kaplan-Meier curve showing Survival probability versus Time (Month) for patients with Negative and Positive ADA status. The survival curves are very close, with a p-value of 0.61, indicating no statistically significant difference in overall survival between the two groups. The median survival time is approximately 20 months for both groups. The number at risk is shown below the curve.

The Y-axis for all plots represents the Probability (ranging from 0.0 to 1.0). The X-axis represents the exposure metric (ranging from 0 to 500 \mu\text{g/mL} or 0 to 200 \mu\text{g/mL}).

Endpoint	Cmax,ss (\mu\text{g/mL})	Cmin,ss (\mu\text{g/mL})	Cavg,ss (\mu\text{g/mL})	Cavg,dose (\mu\text{g/mL})

Probability of AE Leading to Dose Modification (Plots 9-12): Probability remains low (near 0.0) across all exposure levels.
Probability of Immune-Mediated AE (Plots 13-16): Probability increases with increasing exposure metrics (Cmax,ss, Cmin,ss, Cavg,ss, Cavg,dose), showing a positive correlation.
